- **Per-page CSS bloat:** previously flagged as a real but large follow-up
  ("touches the core build pipeline broadly, deserves its own pass"). The
  owner then sent PageSpeed Insights data confirming it's real and asked to
  fix it, so implemented it:
  - **LCP image priority:** the Taylor Swift birthday page's PageSpeed report
    flagged its profile image as missing `fetchpriority="high"` (it was
    already `loading="eager"`). Added `fetchpriority="high"` to the shared
    profile-image `<img>` in `build-events.mjs` — a one-line change that
    benefits every birthday/event page with a profile photo, not just Taylor
    Swift's.
  - **CSS split by page type:** PageSpeed measured 13.6 KiB of CSS on that
    page with 11.8 KiB (87%) unused — the shared stylesheet ships every tool
    widget's CSS (alarm/timer/stopwatch/world-clock/home-dashboard) to every
    page, even the ~230 event/countdown pages that never render any of it
    (they use `.unit .num` for their digits, not the 7-segment `.dig`/`.seg-*`
    system). Verified exclusivity by grepping every generator for each
    class prefix before touching anything (`.ac-*`/`#ac-*`/digit segments →
    alarm only; `.tool-*`/`.timer-*`/`.egg-*` → timer/stopwatch only; `.wc-*`
    → world-clock only; `.tc-*`/`.tool-grid`/mini calendar preview → home
    page only). Marked those six regions in `style.css` with
    `/*TOOLCSS-START*/…/*TOOLCSS-END*/` pairs (same pattern as the existing
    `THEMES-START/END` markers) and added a `needsToolCss(rel)` predicate in
    `build-inline.mjs` that strips them for every page that isn't the alarm/
    timer/stopwatch/world-clock/home page, combined with the existing
    theme-stripping so there are now 4 CSS variants instead of 2. Taylor
    Swift's page: 53.0 KB → 31.3 KB raw CSS (41% smaller), 10.8 KB → 6.4 KB
    gzip. Tool pages (alarm/timer/stopwatch/world-clock/home) are byte-for-
    byte unchanged. Incidental fix: `/widget/` had a `.seg` class name
    collision (the embed configurator's own segmented-control toggle vs. the
    alarm's digit-segment CSS) that's now moot since `/widget/` no longer
    ships the alarm CSS at all.
  - Verified with a full visual regression pass (18 page types
    screenshotted: every tool page, an event page, hub/country/calendar/
    popular/trending, create/builder, c.html, widget, terms, how-it-works) —
    zero console errors beyond expected `file://` CORS noise from the
    Cloudflare Function API calls, and the one page that looked odd (a white
    cutoff partway down the Taylor Swift page) was confirmed via a byte-for-
    byte before/after screenshot comparison to be a pre-existing `file://`
    rendering quirk, not something this change introduced.

### 2026-07-06 — Answered a battery-polling question + minified the inline alarm-widget JS
- **Battery check frequency (question, no change needed):** confirmed there's
  no polling loop — `navigator.getBattery()` is called once and reacts only
  to the browser's own `chargingchange`/`levelchange` events, so there's no
  timer to tune down; it's already zero-cost between real battery changes.
- **PageSpeed "Minify JavaScript" (2 KiB) — valid, fixed:** the alarm page's
  inline widget script (`WIDGET_JS` in `alarm-widget.mjs`) ships hand-written
  with real indentation and `/* */` doc comments for maintainability, and
  wasn't being minified anywhere in the build. Added a conservative
  `minifyJs` helper to `lib.mjs` (same proven approach as the existing
  `minifyCss`: strip `/* */` block comments and blank lines, trim
  indentation — never joins lines, so it can't change ASI behaviour) and
  applied it at export time to `WIDGET_JS`, `HOME_CLOCK_JS`, and
  `HOME_COLOR_JS` in `alarm-widget.mjs`, so every consumer (the alarm page,
  the alarm-time landing pages, and the home page) benefits automatically
  from one change. Source stays fully readable; only the generated output
  is minified. Verified: gzip transfer size of `WIDGET_JS` drops from 11.3
  KiB to 8.7 KiB (2.6 KiB saved, matching/exceeding PageSpeed's estimate),
  `new Function()` confirms all three scripts still parse, and a Playwright
  pass confirmed the actual page still works — digit rendering, opening the
  alarms dialog, saving a new alarm to `localStorage`, and cycling the
  colour swatch all behave identically on both the alarm page and the home
  mini-card.
- **PageSpeed "Reduce unused JavaScript" (74 KiB, gtag.js) — investigated,
  no change made:** this is Google's own hosted analytics library, already
  loaded lazily (on first interaction or ~2.5s after load, well off the
  critical path — see `GA_SNIPPET` in `lib.mjs`). The "unused" bytes are
  generic library code we can't trim ourselves without dropping GA or
  self-hosting a stripped proxy of it; flagged to the owner as understood-
  but-not-actionable without a bigger analytics-provider decision.

### 2026-07-06 — Full-screen layout rework: one-line controls, padding-based corner clearance
Owner sent a screenshot from an actual mobile device (landscape, ~2424x1080)
showing the previous fix broken in a new way: the clock's bottom digits
overlapped the "Add/Edit"/"Stop alarm" buttons, and the control row was
still split across two stacked lines. Root cause was architectural — the
corner-clearing offset was a fixed-pixel `transform` on `.ac-clock`, and
the controls were independently `position:fixed` at `top:80%` — two
unrelated positioning schemes with no knowledge of each other, so they
were bound to collide on some aspect ratio. Replaced both with a layout
that can't overlap by construction:
- `#ac-stage` (which centers `.ac-clock` via flexbox) now reserves the
  danger zones with real padding (`64px` top for the badge/close button,
  `84px` bottom for the controls) instead of shifting the clock's
  transform by guessed pixel amounts. `.ac-clock`'s transform is now
  *only* the small hourly anti-burn-in nudge (`--burn-x`/`--burn-y`), so
  there's no separate "base offset" for the burn-in JS to fight with.
- `.ac-controls` is now anchored to `bottom:18px` (a fixed margin from the
  true bottom edge) instead of `top:80%` (a percentage that put it in a
  different place on every aspect ratio).
- Controls are one line: reused the exact `flex-direction:row` +
  `.ac-ctrl-main{flex:1 1 220px}` / `.ac-ctrl-icons{flex:0 0 auto}`
  pattern the home mini-card already uses for the same "cram the two big
  buttons and four icon buttons into one row" layout, plus the
  `.ac-ctrl-row{width:auto}` override it also needed (the row's own
  `width:100%`, meant for the old stacked layout, was forcing a wrap even
  with the right flex values set on its parent — caught by measuring
  actual row positions, not just eyeballing a screenshot).
- Verified with the owner's exact resolution (2424x1080) plus two smaller
  landscape phone sizes (915x412, 812x375) and four desktop sizes: zero
  overlap between the clock, controls, battery badge, and close button at
  any tested size; controls render as a single row everywhere tested; the
  desktop battery-badge clearance from the previous fix still holds (no
  regression).

### 2026-07-06 — Fix full-screen clock losing its corner-clearing offset
Owner reported no padding on the right side of the date/"set alarms" text
in desktop full screen. Root cause: `burnShift()` (the hourly anti-burn-in
nudge) set `.ac-clock`'s inline `style.transform` directly, which — being
higher specificity than the CSS class rule — completely overwrote the
`translate(-120px,60px)` corner-clearing offset added earlier this session
for the battery badge, every time it ran (including immediately on
entering full screen via `onFsChange`). The clock silently snapped back to
just the ~1vw/1vh burn-in nudge, leaving far less room on the right than
intended. Fixed by moving both offsets into CSS custom properties
(`--burn-x`/`--burn-y`, defaulting to 0) referenced from a single
`calc()`-based transform in style.css, and having `burnShift()` only set/
clear those two properties instead of the whole `transform`. Verified
across three desktop sizes (1366x768, 1600x900, 1920x1080): the computed
transform now correctly combines both offsets (e.g. `-120px + 1vw`) and
the right-side gap is restored (~190-220px vs. the ~70-100px it had
shrunk to).

### 2026-07-06 — Old-clock hour-tens digit + dimmer unlit segments
- **Hour-tens digit (h1) restricted to blank/"1" only:** on an old-fashioned
  digital alarm clock the hour-tens position physically only had room for a
  narrow "1" (hours only ever go 10-12), not a full 7-segment character —
  owner wanted that same visual limit here instead of showing the ghost
  outline of a full digit. Added `.dig[data-d="h1"] .seg-a/.seg-d/.seg-e/
  .seg-f/.seg-g{display:none}` in style.css so only segments b/c (the "1"
  shape) can ever render there; confirmed via grep that `data-d="h1"` is
  exclusive to alarm-widget.mjs's clock markup (timer/stopwatch use a
  separate segMarkup()/SEG_JS system from lib.mjs keyed by character
  position, not data-d, so they're untouched).
- **Dimmed unlit segments:** owner wanted the always-visible off segments on
  the other three digits (h2, m1, m2 — the ones that still need full 0-9
  capability) to read as more subtle. Darkened `--seg-off` to ~45% of its
  previous brightness across all 6 colour themes (red/purple/blue/green/
  yellow/orange) and updated the `.seg` fallback hex to match.
- Verified with a mocked system clock (11:30 AM and 12:05 PM) across full
  screen, the regular alarm page, and the home page mini-card: h1 renders a
  clean minimal "1" with no ghost segments, h2/m1/m2 keep full digit
  capability with a much subtler unlit state, and the separate timer digit
  display (`/timer/2-hours/`) is unaffected.

### 2026-07-06 — Dead CSS audit
Wrote a script to extract every class/id selector in style.css and check
whether the exact string appears anywhere else in the repo (all
seo/tools/*.mjs, seo/templates/*.html, assets/js/*.js, root html files —
with `<style>` blocks stripped first, since every generated page inlines
the full stylesheet and would otherwise trivially "find" every class name
inside its own copy of the CSS). Found 19 candidates; investigated each
individually before removing anything:
- 13 were false positives: the `t-<theme>` classes (t-christmas, t-newyear,
  etc.) are built via string concatenation (`` `t-${theme}` ``, `"t-"+id`)
  in build-events.mjs/build-themes.mjs/c.html, so the compound string never
  appears literally in source. Confirmed each is genuinely reachable
  (including `t-newyear`, kept as a deliberate legacy alias to `fireworks`
  so old shared `?theme=newyear` links keep rendering).
- 6 were genuinely dead: `.sec-title`, `.sec-grid` (+ its media-query
  variant), `.sec-card` (+ its `:hover`/`strong`/`.sec-d` sub-rules),
  `.tc-sub`, `.ac-empty` — leftovers from a pre-redesign home page layout
  and an alarm-list empty-state that's now just rendered as nothing.
  Removed all six (759 -> 749 lines). Rebuilt, re-ran the scan (only the 13
  confirmed-live theme classes remain), and smoke-tested 6 page types
  (alarm/timer/stopwatch/home/terms/an event page) plus a full-page
  screenshot of the home page — no console errors, no visual regression.

**Broader finding, not acted on:** "is all the CSS used on *each* page" has
a second, bigger answer beyond dead code. Per-page-type usage of the
(now-clean) stylesheet: Alarm clock 38%, Home 35%, Timer 25%,
Event/countdown page 24%, Stopwatch 21%, World clock 19%. None of this is
dead — it's each page shipping every *other* page-type's CSS too, since
`build-inline.mjs` inlines one monolithic stylesheet everywhere (already
proven safe/working in principle: it already does exactly this kind of
per-page trimming for theme palettes via a `cssLite` variant, gated on
`usesThemes(rel)`). Extending that same mechanism to also strip
alarm-only/timer-only/stopwatch-only rule blocks per page type is a real,
scoped, doable follow-up — but touches the core build pipeline across many
page types and dynamically-toggled classes, so it deserves its own focused
pass with real regression testing rather than folding into this one.
Flagged to the owner as a candidate next task.


### 2026-07-06 — Alarm clock JS efficiency pass
Owner asked for a CPU/battery efficiency audit, citing the minute-vs-second
tick as the kind of thing to look for. Confirmed the clock already ticks
once per minute aligned to the minute boundary (done earlier this session,
not a fresh fix). Found and fixed two real ones while reading through:
- `pointerdown/pointermove/touchstart/mousemove/keydown/wheel` (three of
  them high-frequency) were registered on `document` unconditionally at
  page load and left attached for the entire visit, even though they only
  exist to detect activity for the full-screen idle-fade — a feature that
  only applies in full screen. `devicemotion` was already correctly scoped
  to attach-on-enter/detach-on-exit; brought the other six in line with it.
  Verified via a listener-log patch: 0 mousemove listeners before entering
  full screen, 1 added on entry, removed on exit.
- `burnShift()` (the hourly anti-burn-in nudge) re-queried `.ac-clock` from
  the DOM and rewrote its `transform` on every one-minute tick even though
  the position only actually changes once an hour. Cached the element and
  added a same-hour early-return, so it's now a no-op 59 out of every 60
  ticks instead of a DOM query + style write every time. Verified the shift
  still applies on entering full screen and clears on exit.


### 2026-07-06 — Shrink the top-left exit-fullscreen button
Reduced the top-left X button from 44x44 to 32x32 (icon 18px -> 16px) so
there's less empty space around the X. Verified it's still clickable and
exits full screen correctly.


### 2026-07-06 — Top-left exit-fullscreen button
- The "drag from the top / swipe to exit fullscreen" message the owner
  described is the browser/OS's own system-level fullscreen-entry notice,
  not anything rendered by the page — there's no API for a site to remove
  it (by design, so a page can't silently trap someone in fullscreen). Added
  the one real mitigation available: `requestFullscreen({navigationUI:
  'hide'})`, a hint some Chromium versions honour to suppress their own
  in-page exit-fullscreen affordance, with a try/catch fallback for browsers
  that reject the options object. Not expected to remove the OS-level toast.
- Added a second, more discoverable way to exit: a top-left X button (44x44,
  `position:fixed;top:16px;left:16px`) mirroring the top-right battery
  badge, full-screen only. Learned from the battery badge bug earlier
  today — put it as a sibling of `.ac-clock` (not a descendant), so
  `.ac-clock`'s `transform` can't hijack its `position:fixed` containing
  block. Wired to the same `closeView()`/`exitFs()` as the existing
  bottom-row close button.
- Verified across 5 viewport sizes (narrow, desktop, ultrawide 2424x1080,
  phone portrait, phone landscape): visible only in full screen, no overlap
  with the digits or anything else, actually exits full screen on click, no
  console errors.

### 2026-07-06 — Actually fix the full-screen battery badge overlap
### 2026-07-06 — Actually fix the full-screen battery badge overlap
Owner sent screenshots (2424x1080) showing "22%" still sitting directly on
top of the date ("SUN 7/5"), despite the previous "give it a background
chip" attempt — that made it legible but not what was asked (genuinely
clear of the date). Root-caused and fixed properly this time:
- The real bug: `#ac-batt` was a *descendant* of `.ac-clock`, and adding
  `transform` to `.ac-clock` (to shift it away from the corner) makes it
  the containing block for any `position:fixed` descendant per the CSS
  spec — so the "viewport-fixed" battery badge was actually tracking
  `.ac-clock`'s transformed box, not the real viewport, completely
  defeating the fix. Moved `#ac-batt` out to be a sibling of `.ac-clock`
  (both children of `#ac-stage`) so `.ac-clock`'s transform can no longer
  interfere with the badge's fixed positioning.
- Gave `.ac-clock` a `transform:translate(-120px,60px)` in full screen to
  shift the (still full-size) clock content away from the true top-right
  corner, so the corner is genuinely empty for the badge rather than
  relying on a background chip to sit on top of overlapping text.
- Fallout from moving `#ac-batt` out of `.ac-clock`: on the regular
  (non-full-screen) page its `right:14px` was now relative to `#ac-stage`,
  which is wider than the visible clock panel, so the badge floated off to
  the right of the panel instead of sitting in its corner. Fixed by giving
  `#ac-stage` the same `max-width:560px;margin:0 auto` as `.ac-clock` by
  default (full screen's own override resets both back to `none`/`0`).
- Verified with the owner's exact viewport size (2424x1080) plus four other
  sizes (narrow 1000x700, desktop 1440x900, phone portrait 414x896, phone
  landscape 896x414): badge sits cleanly in the corner with no overlap in
  all five, colour-sync and full-screen sizing still correct, default page
  unaffected, no console errors.

### 2026-07-06 — Remove full-screen wake note; battery badge pinned + always-on
- Removed the "Alarm only rings when screen is unlocked…" note specifically
  once full-screen bedside mode has actually started — no more pop-up
  message once the clock is already running full screen. It still shows on
  the regular (non-full-screen) page, pointing at the Full screen button.
  Cleaned up the now-dead full-screen-specific wake-note CSS.
- Battery badge in full screen: now `position:fixed` at 16px from the top
  and right of the actual browser viewport (not the clock content box), a
  larger `clamp(17px,2.4vw,24px)` font (up from ~12-16px), and no longer
  fades when idle — always visible per the owner's explicit ask, overriding
  my earlier idle-fade/anti-burn-in choice.
  - Tradeoff worth knowing: a true fixed screen-corner position can overlap
    the date/alarms text (or, on some portrait phone sizes, the digits/AM
    label) at certain aspect ratios, since the clock content is centered and
    sized off vw/vh. Gave the badge its own solid background chip + high
    z-index so it stays legible even where it visually overlaps other
    content, rather than trying to carve out a permanently content-free
    corner (attempted via a reserved-inset approach first; the centering
    math made it unreliable across aspect ratios, so backed it out in favor
    of the chip). Verified readable at desktop, narrow, and both phone
    orientations; flagged the portrait-phone/landscape-phone overlap cases
    to the owner rather than silently declaring it perfect.

### 2026-07-06 — Battery level/charging badge + another colour-sync bug
- **Battery badge:** added a small "82%" (+ bolt icon while charging)
  indicator, top-right of the clock face, using the same Battery Status API
  the wake-lock code already partially used. Same theme colour as the rest
  of the display, updates live on `chargingchange`/`levelchange`. Only
  Chrome/Edge on Android and desktop support this API (no Safari/Firefox at
  all) — stays hidden everywhere else, same graceful-degradation pattern as
  the existing wake-lock feature.
  - Scope/placement decisions made without user confirmation (the
    clarifying-question tool failed twice this session): shows on the main
    alarm page and alarm-time landing pages (wherever the full widget runs),
    **not** on the home page's mini clock card (kept that one lightweight).
    In full screen it's pinned above the content (not the literal viewport
    corner — an earlier attempt at that overlapped the date text on some
    viewport sizes) and fades with the controls after 15s idle, both to
    match the existing full-screen behavior and to avoid a static bright
    badge contributing to OLED burn-in overnight. Content = percentage +
    charging bolt (not charging-only or a viewport-pinned overlay).
  - Verified with a mocked Battery API: shows/updates correctly, matches
    the active theme colour, positions cleanly at mobile/desktop/phone
    fullscreen sizes with no overlap, and stays hidden on the home card.
- **Second colour-sync bug found & fixed:** `.ac-dim` (the "no alarms"
  placeholder text) had a hardcoded dark red independent of the theme
  system — same root cause class as the alarm-list bug fixed earlier today,
  just a different element. Changed it to dim the active `--seg-on` colour
  via opacity instead of a fixed hex.

### 2026-07-06 — Fix colour-sync bug + brighten red for contrast (PageSpeed)
- **Bug:** the alarm list, dialogs, and (on the home page) the Add/icon
  button row all live outside `#ac-panel` as siblings, so the `--seg-on`
  etc. custom properties — only ever defined on `#ac-panel[data-c=...]` —
  never reached them. Picking purple, for example, correctly recoloured the
  clock face but left the checkbox and "Set an alarm for…" text always red.
  Fixed by also setting the same `data-c` attribute on `<body>` (in both
  `applyColor()` in the main widget and the home card's colour script) and
  mirroring every `#ac-panel[data-c=...]` CSS rule as `body[data-c=...]`.
  Kept the `#ac-panel`-scoped rules too (unchanged) so a cloned panel still
  carries its colour correctly into a Picture-in-Picture window. Also added
  an explicit `accent-color` to the alarm on/off checkbox, which had no
  colour rule at all before (native browser blue, regardless of theme).
- **Contrast:** measured real contrast on the live page with an in-browser
  WCAG luminance audit (not just eyeballing the CSS) — the default red
  (`#ff2b2b`) actually already cleared 4.5:1 (~5.3:1) by the numbers, but per
  the owner's PageSpeed Insights report, brightened it anyway for a solid
  margin: new default `--seg-on:#ff6b6b` (~7.1:1 against the panel's
  `#0b0b0c` background) on the regular alarm page, alarm-time landing pages,
  and the home page card. Full screen and the Picture-in-Picture popout keep
  the original dimmer red as their default (`body.ac-fs #ac-panel:not([data-c])`,
  `.ac-pip #ac-panel:not([data-c])`) since PageSpeed never audits those
  transient view states and the owner asked not to change them. The other
  five colours (purple/blue/green/yellow/orange) are unchanged. Verified
  with a scripted contrast pass (0 failures site-wide on the audited page)
  and confirmed fullscreen still renders the original red.

### 2026-07-05 — Full countdown date audit + a real "estimated date" reminder system
Ran `check-dates.mjs` and independently fact-checked every near/medium-term
"guessed" date (retailer sale windows, sports/awards/film dates) against
official sources. Found and fixed three real bugs:
- **Amazon Prime Day** (`prime-day-countdown`) was showing July 14, 2026 as
  upcoming; Amazon actually moved it to **June 23–26, 2026**, which had
  already happened — exactly the bug the owner spotted. Corrected the 2026
  date; 2027+ are still forward estimates (Amazon hasn't announced that far
  out, and the schedule just proved it isn't fixed to July every year).
- **Walmart Deals Event** had the same problem — showed July 7, 2026, but
  Walmart moved theirs to **June 22–28, 2026** to align with Prime Day.
  Corrected the 2026 date.
- **World Cup Final** kickoff time was off by 4 hours (15:00 ET instead of
  the confirmed **19:00 ET** kickoff at MetLife Stadium). Fixed.

Verified correct (no change needed): GTA 6 (Nov 19, 2026), World Cup final
*date* (Jul 19, 2026), Oscars 2027 (Mar 14), Super Bowl LXI (Feb 14, 2027),
March Madness 2027 final (Apr 5), Avengers: Doomsday (Dec 18, 2026),
election day / inauguration (fixed by law). Did not re-verify every sports/
awards date on the site beyond this list — flagged as a possible follow-up
below.

**New mechanism — `estimated: true`:** added a flag events.json entries can
carry for dates nobody has officially confirmed (owner best guesses). Added
a check in `check-dates.mjs` that fires **only when the estimated date is
within 60 days** — a timely nudge instead of a permanent daily nag — and
flows through the same daily GitHub Actions maintenance job that already
emails/sheets findings to the owner (`.github/workflows/maintenance.yml`).
Simulated it against a fake "today" near commencement season and confirmed
it fires correctly (~30–60 days out) and stays silent the rest of the year.

Marked as `estimated: true`:
- **All 10 college graduation countdowns** (Harvard, Stanford, UCLA, UT
  Austin, Michigan, Florida, Penn State, USC, NYU, MIT) — **owner's open
  item: these use a fixed month/day guessed from past years' typical
  commencement timing, not real confirmed dates.** No specific dates
  available yet per the owner. The site will now nudge automatically ~30-60
  days before each guessed date every year until real dates are supplied
  (or the pages are reworked, e.g. tying to an academic-calendar source).
- **Prime Day, Walmart Deals Event, Prime Big Deal Days** — retailer-set
  windows announced only weeks ahead; same yearly nudge going forward so
  this exact bug (a passed date still shown as upcoming) can't recur
  silently.

**Open follow-up (not done this pass):** a from-scratch verification of
every remaining `once`/`dates` sports and entertainment date beyond the
short list above — lower urgency since none were flagged as wrong, but not
independently fact-checked either.

### 2026-07-02 — Bedside-clock "skip" option + home card note + fuller mini-cards
- The home card's full-screen icon links to `/alarm-clock/?bedside=1`, which
  shows a one-button overlay ("Start bedside clock"). Added a second, secondary
  button — "Go to the alarm clock page" — that just dismisses the overlay,
  leaving the visitor on the normal (non-fullscreen) alarm page, for anyone
  who clicked the icon but didn't want full-screen bedside mode.
- Added a small note in small letters directly below the home card's button
  row (not the overlay) — "Open [fullscreen icon] to see the full screen
  bedside clock." (the real FS_ICON svg inline) — so the icon's purpose is
  clear right on the home page.
- World Clock and Countdowns mini-cards on the home page only showed 4 and 3
  rows respectively, leaving visible empty space compared to the
  equal-height Timer/Stopwatch/Calendar cards in the same grid row. Expanded
  both to 6 rows: World Clock now shows New York, Los Angeles, London,
  Dubai, Tokyo, Sydney (same tz/city naming as build-world-clock.mjs);
  Countdowns now shows the 6 soonest upcoming events instead of 3.

### 2026-07-02 — Home alarm card: single-line buttons; confirmed colour persistence
- Verified colour persistence already works correctly (was asked to confirm):
  both the home card and the real page read `localStorage.ac_color` on load
  and write to it on click, tested across real reloads on both.
- Home card layout: put the "Add" button and the icon row on one line
  (`.tc-alarm .ac-controls{flex-direction:row}`) since the card only has one
  main button and there's room; the icon row wraps to its own line only if
  the card gets very narrow (flex-wrap, no fixed breakpoint needed). Real
  alarm page keeps its stacked Add/Edit + Stop alarm row, unaffected.

### 2026-07-02 — Home alarm card: real working buttons matching the real page
- Replaced the home card's "Create alarm"/"Bedside clock" text buttons with
  the same button language as the real alarm page: a full-width "Add" button
  plus a row of three 44x44 icon buttons (?, colour swatch, full-screen).
  The "?" jumps straight to /alarm-clock/#ac-instructions; the swatch is a
  real, working control (new HOME_COLOR_JS) that cycles the same rainbow
  order and reads/writes the same `ac_color` localStorage key as the real
  page, so a colour picked on either page shows on both.
- Refactored the swatch CSS from an ID selector (#ac-color) to a reusable
  `.ac-controls .btn.ac-swatch` class rule so both pages' buttons share it —
  hit and fixed a specificity regression where `.ac-swatch` alone (1 class)
  lost to the existing `.ac-controls .btn` rule (2 classes) and the rainbow
  disappeared site-wide; bumped to a 3-selector rule to win unconditionally.
  Verified the rainbow renders on both the home card and the real page.
- Exported `FS_ICON` from alarm-widget.mjs for reuse; scoped the "hide the
  widget's own controls" CSS to `.tc-alarm #ac-panel .ac-controls` so the
  home card's new (separate, real) control row isn't caught by it. Removed
  the now-dead `.tc-go`/`.btn-clock` CSS.

### 2026-07-02 — Icon touch targets + redirect after saving on a time-preset page
- Icon buttons (?, colour, share, full screen, show-on-top) are now 44x44
  (square) instead of 44x22, for a proper mobile touch target.
- Saving an alarm from a "set alarm for HH:MM" landing page (e.g.
  `/alarm-clock/6-am/`) now redirects to `/alarm-clock/` — nothing left to do
  on the single-time page once it's saved. Gated on `window.AC_SETTIME`
  (only set on those pages), so the main alarm page's Save is unaffected.
  Not a Google/SEO concern: it's a post-form-submission navigation triggered
  by a real user action, not a bot-facing redirect — crawlers never submit
  the form, so they always see the page's full content and it stays indexed
  normally with its own canonical.

### 2026-07-02 — Full-screen clock bigger + anti-burn-in + icon polish
- **Full screen:** clock is now width-driven (`min(23.5vw,36vh)` digits, right
  panel `min(5.4vw,7.4vh)`) so it fills ~97% of the screen width, still capped
  by height for landscape.
- **OLED anti-burn-in:** in full screen (the overnight bedside view) the whole
  clock face nudges to one of 8 positions (~1–2vw/vh drift) once an hour via
  `burnShift()` in the widget, so no pixel holds a static image all night;
  transform is cleared on fs exit. (Burn-in is still a real risk on
  OLED/AMOLED phones — which is the bedside device — so this stays.)
- **Icon row order** is now ? · colour · share · full screen (· show-on-top on
  desktop); in full screen the fs/on-top buttons hide and Close shows
  (pre-existing rule). The colour button is now a muted red→purple rainbow
  gradient filling the whole box (dark tint overlay so it doesn't glow).

### 2026-07-02 — Alarm page redesign (square clock, colour, button rows) + home layout
- **Square clock** on the alarm page: on wider screens (`min-width:561px`) the
  clock is padded to a 1:1 aspect ratio (max min(500px,68vh)), readout centered.
  Mobile keeps the approved stacked rectangle; full screen exempt.
- **Removed the intro paragraph** under the H1 on /alarm-clock/.
- **Display colour picker:** a swatch icon button cycles the LED colour
  (red → amber → green → blue → white), persisted in `localStorage.ac_color`
  and applied via `--seg-on`/`--seg-off`/`--seg-glow`/`--ac-line` on `#ac-panel`
  (digits, AM/PM, date, alarms and the control buttons all recolour together).
- **Control bar → two rows:** Add/Edit + Stop alarm at 50/50 on the first row;
  a second row of small icons — Full Screen, Share, colour swatch, Show-on-top
  (desktop only), and a **?** that scrolls to the on-page instructions
  (`#ac-instructions`). Close is hidden except in full screen. Scoped changes
  don't touch the home mini-card (its controls are hidden).
- **Home desktop:** World Clock now sits to the right of the alarm clock
  (2fr/1fr `.tc-top-row`); it drops below when the page narrows (<900px). The
  tool grid is now four cards in one row (Timer, Stopwatch, Countdowns,
  Calendar); World Clock gained a 4th city (Sydney) for the taller card.

### 2026-07-02 — Flat dark backgrounds + mobile clock layout v2
- **Flat dark site background:** the default `:root` background is now a flat

### 2026-07-02 — Mobile-portrait clock redesign + Trump one-off removal
- **Mobile portrait alarm clock redesign:** below ~560px the clock face now

- **Performance:** ran Lighthouse across every page type, fixed CLS/perf
  issues found, re-verified 100/100/100/100 with near-zero CLS.
- **Graduation-countdown dates:** researched official 2027 commencement dates
  for all 10 universities in `events.json`; where no official date was
  published yet, kept a researched estimate and added a visible on-page "date

  copy; "Return to alarm clock" now navigates to a clean `/alarm-clock/` URL.
- **Taylor Swift page:** added `fetchpriority="high"` to the hero image for
  PageSpeed; a subsequent image swap was reverted per owner feedback while
  keeping the fetchpriority fix.
- **JS payload audit:** confirmed the home page's JS was already page-scoped;
  the actual bloat was embedded *data* (search index, view counts), which is
  now lazy-loaded instead of inlined.
- **New feature — "Programmatic Escapism" micro-event countdowns:** built
  evergreen, zero-staleness countdown pages targeting daily/weekly/recurring
  searches ("countdown to Friday", "next federal holiday", "next long
  weekend"). Round 1 shipped as 4 flat pages
  (`/countdown-to-5pm/`, `/countdown-to-friday/`, `/next-federal-holiday/`,

    `observedShift()` / `upcomingFederalHolidays()` in `lib.mjs`.
  - **Round 2 restructure** (owner: clean URLs, consolidate into one hub,

    `microevent-widget.mjs` for multi-instance use with **per-element
    fullscreen** (`el.requestFullscreen()` + `:fullscreen` CSS) instead of the

    `build-microevents.mjs` to generate `/work/` (hub: all 3 widgets, each
    independently full-screenable, each with an "Open just this countdown"
    link, plus a `documentPictureInPicture` pop-out button reusing the
    clone-behind/relocate-live-DOM pattern from the alarm clock's `popTop()`)
    and clean per-countdown pages `/work/friday/`, `/work/holiday/`,
    `/work/long-weekend/`. The standalone 5pm page was folded into Friday

    Friday-only card) with all 3 countdowns as one-line rows (label, live
    value, Open button) linking individually, card itself linking to `/work/`;

    "Weekend") don't truncate.
  - Wired into the build pipeline (`package.json`, `build-inline.mjs`
    HTML+BUNDLES, `build-sitemap.mjs`).
  - Verified with Playwright + screenshots: redirects, hub widget count/ticks,
    per-widget fullscreen enter/exit (`document.fullscreenElement` scoped to
    just the clicked widget), pop-out (live DOM relocated into the PiP window,

### 2026-07-07 (cont.) — /work/ polish: single Open button, hub-wide full screen, bigger titles
- **Homepage card:** owner wanted the three per-row "Open" buttons gone in
  favor of one Open button at the bottom (matching the Timer/Stopwatch card
  pattern) — the whole card already links to `/work/`, so per-row links were
  redundant. Simplified `.wk-row` back down to just label + live value.
- **Hub full screen:** owner didn't want three separate per-widget Full
  screen buttons on `/work/` — wanted one button showing all three
  countdowns at once, styled like the homepage card's "all three together"
  view. `mcWidgetHtml()` gained a `controls:false` option that omits a
  widget's own Full screen/close buttons; the hub now fullscreens
  `#mc-hub-grid` itself via a single `#mc-hub-fs` button, with its own close
  button, wake lock, and hourly anti-burn-in nudge (mirrors the per-widget
  version, just scoped to the grid). Individual `/work/<slug>/` pages keep
  their own per-widget full screen since there's only one countdown to show.
- **Bigger titles:** the "Until Friday at 5 PM"-style label was too small to
  "stand out" per owner feedback. Made `.mc-label` container-query sized
  (`clamp(15px,5.4cqw,21px)`, `.mc-widget{container-type:inline-size}`) so it
  scales to whatever width the widget actually has — big (21px) alone on its
  own page, still bold (15-17px) as one of three in the hub grid — without
  separate breakpoints per context.
- **Caught while verifying:** enlarging things exposed a pre-existing bug —
  the clock's 4 digit units (min-width 74px each) overflowed/overlapped in
  the hub grid's narrower ~250px columns. Fixed with container-scaled unit
  sizing (`.mc-widget .clock`/`.unit`/`.num`/`.lab`, cqw-based with the same
  74px/40px caps so full-width pages are unaffected) plus `flex-wrap` as a
  safety net. The new hub-grid *fullscreen* mode needed its own cqw-based
  sizing too (not viewport-vw) since three widgets split the screen unevenly
  — first attempt used vw units sized for a single full-width widget, which
  caused the same overlap/wrap problem at full-screen scale; fixed by
  switching to `flex:1 1 0` + cqw off each widget's own share of the screen.
- Verified with `wrangler pages dev` + Playwright/screenshots at multiple
  viewports (1100px desktop, 390px mobile): hub grid shows one Full screen +
  one Pop out button and zero per-widget buttons; clicking Full screen
  fullscreens `#mc-hub-grid` (confirmed via `document.fullscreenElement.id`)
  showing all three widgets in a single row each, correctly labeled, close
  button working; individual pages unaffected (still their own per-widget
  full screen, unchanged single-row digit layout); labels render at 21px
  alone / 15-17px in the grid with no overflow at any tested width. `npm run
  check` clean apart from the same two pre-existing SpaceX findings.

### 2026-07-07 (cont. 2) — "Create your own countdown" on /work/; hub full screen stacked vertically
- **Create your own countdown:** owner asked for a way to make a custom
  countdown from the /work/ hub and its three sub-pages, using the same
  abuse-language safety protocol as the existing countdown builder. Checked
  first whether this was the same functionality as the full builder
  (`seo/templates/create.html` + `assets/js/create.js`, reachable today only
  via the generic hamburger-menu link) — it's conceptually the same
  capability (name + date/time -> shareable link) but that builder is a much
  heavier flow (theme/effect/sound/art pickers, email capture, share-button
  row) that doesn't fit a page whose whole point is "quick, get back to
  work." Built a separate, lighter-weight widget instead, per the owner's
  "if not, create a different JavaScript set and keep them easily
  identifiable" instruction:
  - `seo/tools/quick-create-widget.mjs` (new) — the card's HTML: name +
    date/time only, no theme/effect/sound/art/email.
  - `assets/js/quick-create.js` (new) — its controller, explicitly commented
    as NOT `create.js`. Builds the same kind of `/countdown/<slug>/?name=...`
    link the full builder does (so the result is a completely normal,
    fully-supported countdown page), but with zero server round-trip to
    create — same "the whole countdown lives in the URL" model as
    everything else on the site.
  - **Safety protocol reused, not reimplemented:** the same generated
    blocklist (`window.AC_MOD_RE`/`AC_MOD_COMPACT_RE`/`AC_MOD_ALLOW` from
    `assets/js/moderation.js`, built by `build-waf.mjs` from
    `cloudflare/blocklist.txt`) that the full builder and the server/edge
    layers (`functions/_middleware.js`) already enforce — a name is blocked
    here under the identical rules, not a separate, lesser check. The
    resulting `/countdown/*` link is also independently re-checked at the
    edge/server regardless of which flow produced it, same as always.
  - Wired in via `build-inline.mjs`'s `BUNDLES`, scoped to only the 4
    `/work/` pages (`["moderation", "effects", "quick-create"]`) — not
    site-wide, matching the session's existing page-scoped-JS discipline.
    Reused existing global CSS primitives (`.card`, `label`/`input`/`.row`,
    `.notice`/`.notice-warn`, `.linkbox`, `.btn`) already shipped on these
    pages, so zero new CSS was needed.
- **Hub full screen restacked:** owner wanted the three countdowns in the
  hub's full-screen view stacked one above another instead of side by side,
  so each could go bigger and be easier to read. Changed
  `.mc-hub-grid:fullscreen` from a horizontal 3-column flex row to a vertical
  column stack; unit/label sizing now combines `vw` *and* `vh` caps (each
  widget only owns about a third of the viewport's height there) instead of
  the container-query sizing the side-by-side layout needed.
- Verified with `wrangler pages dev` + Playwright: the widget is present
  with the moderation globals loaded on all 4 pages; a slur-containing name
  is blocked (error shown, no link generated); a normal name produces a
  working link that was then loaded and confirmed to render as a real,
  ticking countdown; an allowlisted phrase containing a blocked substring
  ("Killer Party") correctly is *not* blocked, confirming the exact-match
  allowlist carries over too. Screenshotted the stacked full-screen layout
  at desktop (1100×900), wide-short (900×500), and mobile portrait
  (412×915) — big, legible, centered, no overflow at any of them. `npm run
  check` clean apart from the same two pre-existing SpaceX findings.

### 2026-07-07 (cont. 3) — /work/ countdowns: Day/Time/Time zone controls, then color/share/pop-out
**Day/Time/Time zone controls.** Owner asked for dropdowns under each work
countdown to pick the day and time it targets (holiday: last workday before
vs. on the holiday itself; long weekend: last workday before vs. first day
of the long weekend, the new default) plus a time zone for remote workers on
a different clock. Choices persist and drive both the label text and the
actual countdown, on the regular view and full screen.
- `microevent-widget.mjs`'s `computeTarget()` rewritten to take a settings
  object (`{mode, time, tz}`) per widget, generalizing the old hardcoded
  `startOfWeekend` flag into a `mode` concept (`on` / `before` / `first`)
  shared by the holiday and long-weekend widgets. Timezone math reuses
  `assets/js/util.js`'s `AC_UTIL.targetDate()` (the same IANA-timezone
  conversion the countdown builder/result page already use) instead of
  reimplementing it — now bundled into the 4 `/work/` pages.
  `mcSettingsHtml()` (new export) renders a Day/Time/Time zone panel as a
  **sibling** of `.mc-widget`, not a descendant, so it's automatically
  excluded when a single widget goes full screen (nothing outside the
  fullscreened element renders); the hub's grid-level full screen needed one
  extra CSS rule since there the panel *is* a descendant of the fullscreened
  grid.
- Label text is now fully dynamic (e.g. "Until Friday, 3:30 PM (EDT)"),
  built client-side from the resolved mode/time/tz plus a live, DST-aware
  short zone name via `Intl.DateTimeFormat(...).formatToParts()` rather than
  a hand-maintained abbreviation table.
- Settings persist in `localStorage` (key `ac_work_settings`) **keyed by
  widget id, not by page** — a choice made on `/work/friday/` also applies
  on the `/work/` hub and vice versa.
- Updated intro/FAQ copy on all 3 pages to describe the new default and the
  customization options; trimmed two FAQ items the owner flagged as obvious
  ("why 5pm not midnight", "does it reset weekly").
- Verified: default labels/mode-options correct per widget; changing
  time+tz on `/work/friday/` updates the label immediately, survives a
  reload, and shows up on `/work/` in the same browser context (confirming
  cross-page persistence — an earlier same-assertion test using a *fresh*
  browser context per page falsely looked like a failure, since Playwright
  isolates localStorage per context by default); holiday mode switch
  changes both the label and the actual countdown numbers; single-widget
  full screen visually and correctly covers the settings panel (Playwright's
  `isVisible()` doesn't account for the Fullscreen API's top-layer occlusion
  — confirmed by screenshot + bounding-box inspection instead, not just the
  DOM check); hub full screen hides all three panels. Quick-create and
  moderation still function correctly alongside the new `util` bundle
  addition. `npm run check` clean apart from the two pre-existing SpaceX
  findings.

**Color, share, pop-out — "make it look more like the alarm clock."**
Immediately after, owner asked for the same alarm-clock feature set on
these countdowns: choose the color, share, and pop out.
- **Color:** a swatch cycles the identical red/purple/blue/green/yellow/
  orange palette via the *same* `ac_color` localStorage key the alarm
  clock's swatch already uses, applied via `body[data-c]` — so a color
  picked on the alarm page shows up on `/work/` and vice versa, no new
  state. The digit color/glow hex pairs are duplicated directly (not a
  reuse of `--seg-on`/`--seg-glow`) because those variables live inside the
  `TOOLCSS`-marked block `build-inline.mjs` strips from every page that
  isn't alarm/timer/stopwatch/world-clock/home — `/work/` isn't in that
  list, so referencing them would've resolved to nothing.
- **Share:** an icon button (hub-level and per-widget) copies the current
  page's own URL, or opens the native share sheet where supported —
  mirroring the alarm clock's simpler single-link `share()` helper, not its
  multi-alarm share dialog (overkill here, since each work countdown is
  already just one shareable page).
- **Pop-out:** each standalone `/work/<slug>/` page now has its own
  pop-out button (previously only the hub, for all three together, had
  one) — the exact same clone-behind/relocate-live-DOM pattern, scoped to a
  single widget instead of the whole grid.
- New `.mc-icon-row`/`.btn.mc-swatch` CSS rather than reusing the alarm's
  `.ac-icon`/`.ac-swatch` classes — same TOOLCSS-stripping reason as the
  color variables above. Caught and fixed a follow-up styling bug: the new
  icon buttons initially inherited the site's bright primary-button style
  (no `.secondary` class), clashing with the muted "Full screen" button
  next to them — added `.secondary` and bumped `.mc-swatch`'s selector
  specificity to `.btn.mc-swatch` so its rainbow gradient background wasn't
  itself overridden by `.btn.secondary`'s background rule.
- Verified: clicking the swatch actually changes the rendered digit color
  (confirmed via computed style, not just the DOM attribute) and persists
  across a page navigation within the same browser context; share copies
  the exact current-page URL and shows brief "Copied!" feedback; pop-out
  clones the widget in place and confirms the live (non-clone) widget is
  gone from the main page (moved into the PiP window) exactly like the
  hub's existing group pop-out. Zero console errors across all 4 pages.
  `npm run check` clean apart from the two pre-existing SpaceX findings.

### 2026-07-08 — Promote the Work Countdowns card into the home-page top row
Owner wanted the Work card moved up (in front of the timer, ideally in front
of the world clock) since `/work/` should be a popular page, and flagged
that the differently-sized cards make clean spacing hard. It was the lone
5th card in the four-column tool grid, so it always orphaned onto its own
row with three empty columns beside it (visible at 1200px and, as a lone
card, at the 2-col 760px width too). Moved it into the `.tc-top-row`,
stacked above the World Clock in the right-hand column, so it sits right
under the alarm — before both the World Clock and the timer. That drops the
tool grid to an even four cards (Timer/Stopwatch/Countdowns/Calendar), which
fills perfectly at 1/2/4 columns with no orphan.
- CSS (`.tc-top-row` ≥900px): now `grid-template-columns:2fr 1fr` with
  `grid-template-rows:1fr 1fr`; the alarm is explicitly placed at
  `grid-column:1;grid-row:1/span 2` so it spans the full height of the two
  stacked right cells, and Work/World Clock auto-place into the right column
  (Work row 1, World Clock row 2, matching DOM order). Because the alarm is
  then taller than its own content, added `justify-content:space-between` to
  it on wide screens so its blocks distribute (title top, LED clock centered,
  Add row + note toward the bottom) — the extra height reads as breathing
  room, not dead space. Narrow (<900px) is unchanged single-column stacking,
  so the three top cards fall Alarm → Work → World Clock, keeping Work high.
- Verified with real-browser screenshots at 1200/1000/905/760/430 (including
  both sides of the 900px breakpoint): tool grid is a clean 4-across / 2×2 /
  1-col with no orphan at any width, alarm content distributes cleanly, and
  the Work card's live values still tick (2d 16h / 61d 7h / 58d 16h) and its
  whole-card click still navigates to `/work/` from the new location. No
  console errors. `npm run check` clean apart from the two pre-existing
  SpaceX findings.

- **Work Countdowns card** (`build-home.mjs` + `style.css`): reverted the

  without a leading glyph).
- Verified in a real browser at 1200/905/430: card rows render cleanly with
  no overflow, Go buttons and the See-all link point at the right URLs,
  labels/values are correct and still tick, the nav menu shows the new item
  in the right spot, and the full top-region stays balanced. No console

### 2026-07-08 — Launch-video facade on the rocket pages (from a Gemini idea-dump)
Owner shared a batch of Gemini suggestions (gaming/eSports countdown vertical
+ execution tactics) and asked me to judge fit against our static/fast model
and ask where things conflict. Assessment: most of the "execution strategy"
is already built — auto-timezone via the browser's own `Intl` (better than
Gemini's "read their IP", which would break edge-caching), `Event` JSON-LD
with `startDate`/`VirtualLocation`, static `.ics` + Google Calendar, and
`FAQPage` AEO answers. The genuine conflicts (IP detection, per-user local
time in the indexed `<title>`, real-time APIs for volatile data) I flagged
rather than adopted. Via AskUserQuestion the owner chose: gaming vertical =
**curated tentpole events only** (static, hand-maintained — queued for a
follow-up), volatile data = **build-time only / pure static**, and the launch
video = **facade → a fixed SpaceX video**.
- Built the launch-video facade (this session's one feature). Any event with
  a `launchSite` (the 4 rocket pages: Starship, Cape Canaveral, Kennedy,
  Vandenberg) now shows a click-to-play video box under the countdown; a
  per-event `video` (YouTube id) overrides the default. Default is SpaceX's
  **Falcon Heavy Test Flight** (`wbSwFU6tY1c`) — verified via search as the
  canonical, permanently-hosted, embeddable SpaceX launch webcast (I couldn't
  hit YouTube's oEmbed from the sandbox, so I confirmed the id by search).
- It's a true **facade**: the visitor sees a styled 16:9 placeholder (play
  button + "Watch a SpaceX launch"), and **nothing loads from YouTube until
  they tap** — a tiny inline `onclick` swaps in a `youtube-nocookie.com`
  iframe. So zero third-party request/script/iframe weight on first paint;
  it can't hurt LCP/PageSpeed. `aspect-ratio:16/9` reserves the box so the
  player swap causes no layout shift. Separate from the existing (API-driven,
  LL2_API_KEY-blocked) live-stream button, so it works today with no key.
- `build-events.mjs` computes `videoId`/`videoFacade` (empty for non-launch
  events, so every other page is byte-unchanged); `.cd-video*` CSS added to
  the shared stylesheet. No CSP on the site, so the iframe needs no header
  change.
- Verified with Playwright on `/astronomy-countdowns/spacex-cape-canaveral/`:
  facade visible, **0 YouTube requests before click**, iframe with the right
  `youtube-nocookie` src loads on click, bounding box identical before/after
  (no CLS), and exactly the 4 launch pages carry the markup (Christmas /
  Taylor Swift confirmed to have none). No console errors; `npm run check`
  clean apart from the two pre-existing SpaceX date findings.
- **Queued for next session:** the curated tentpole gaming/eSports pages —
  these ride the existing `build-events.mjs` pipeline (Event schema, .ics,
  timezone-correct countdown all already there), so the work is data: pick a
  handful of big, confirmed-date events and add them to `events.json` under a
  suitable category.

### 2026-07-08 — "420 Countdown" novelty pages (daily 4:20 PM + annual 4/20)
Owner wanted a pair of tongue-in-cheek 420 countdowns: one to 4:20 PM each
day, one to April 20 at 4:20 PM, both titled "420 Countdown", cross-linked,
with pot leaves flanking the title and dry copy that plays innocent while
everyone clearly gets the joke.
- New `seo/tools/build-420.mjs` generates `/420-countdown/` (daily) and
  `/420-day-countdown/` (annual). Pure client-side, same pattern as the
  other evergreen countdowns: the target is computed from the visitor's own
  clock (timezone-correct, never stale), ticks each second, auto-advances at
  zero, and fires the existing `effects` confetti. No build-time date, no
  backend.
- **Leaves:** there's no cannabis emoji in Unicode (people fake it with
  🌿/🍁), so I drew a proper inline **SVG cannabis leaf** (7 leaflets from one
  rotated/shortened path) — crisper, unmistakably a pot leaf, and on-brand
  with the site's all-SVG art. Flanks the H1 on both pages; the countdown
  digits are tinted green (`.cd420-clock`) to tie it together.
- **Tone:** dry and winking — "Couldn't tell you… something about it being a
  very relaxing time of day. If you know, you know." / "a great many people
  will be… celebrating something. We didn't ask what, and we're far too
  polite to guess." Never names anything explicitly; the leaves + copy do the
  indirect acknowledgment the owner asked for.
- Wired into the pipeline: `package.json` (runs `build-420.mjs` before
  sitemap/inline), `build-inline.mjs` (HTML array for CSS+nav inlining;
  BUNDLES = `["effects"]` for the confetti), `build-sitemap.mjs` (both URLs).
  Not added to the main nav (kept it a discoverable-by-link novelty);
  cross-linked to each other. URLs are clean of any blocklisted term, so the
  edge WAF/middleware doesn't touch them.
- Verified in a real browser: both pages render with the leaf-flanked title,
  correct labels ("Until 4:20 PM" / "Until 4/20"), ticking countdowns
  (annual correctly ~286 days out to next 4/20), working cross-links, and no
  console errors. `npm run check` clean apart from the two pre-existing
  SpaceX date findings.

### 2026-07-08 — 420 pages: full screen, pop-out, share (incl. Facebook), green picker
Owner wanted the 420 countdowns to have the same "leave it up / show it off"
kit the alarm clock and /work/ countdowns have, a colour picker limited to
greens, and — added mid-task — Facebook sharing ("start with 420 and see how
it works… my friends will be confused but I think it'd be funny").
- Wrapped each 420 countdown in a `.cd420-stage` and extended the page's

### 2026-07-08 — Add _headers: fresh HTML, 24h edge cache, SW no-cache
Added a root `_headers` file (Pages honors it now that "Ignore headers" is off,
with "use Cache-Control if present, bypass if not"). Default `/*`:
`public, max-age=0, must-revalidate, s-maxage=86400` — browsers always
revalidate (instant freshness; browser caches can't be purged remotely) while
Cloudflare's edge caches 24h and is auto-wiped on every Pages deploy (fresh +
fast). Because CSS/JS are inlined, the HTML is the whole page, so stale HTML =
stale everything — hence must-revalidate. Only static exception: the timer +
alarm service workers (`no-cache`) so notification updates ship immediately.
API Functions set their own Cache-Control (no-store etc.) and are unaffected.
Files: _headers (new), CLAUDE.md (source list). Verify after deploy with
`curl -sI` on /, /timer/sw.js.

### 2026-07-08 — Remove inline Google Analytics (now runs via Cloudflare Zaraz)
GA4 now loads through Cloudflare Zaraz (edge-side), so the inline gtag snippet
is removed to avoid double-counting + re-adding the 181 kB gtag.js to the
client. Set GA_SNIPPET = "" in lib.mjs (every generator still interpolates it
and build-inline's GA_RE still strips any old inline gtag from committed HTML);
also deleted the hardcoded GA block from seo/templates/create.html. GA id kept
in lib.mjs for easy revert. Verified: 0 gtag/googletagmanager references in any
generated page, and in a real browser across 5 page types 0 GA requests fire
and window.gtag/dataLayer are undefined. Files: lib.mjs, create.html.

### 2026-07-09 — NOAA tide charts: /tides/ + 67 station pages
New section built on two free, keyless, CORS-open APIs, fetched client-side so
pages stay static: NOAA CO-OPS (official U.S. tide predictions, ~a year ahead)
and Open-Meteo (16-day wind/gust/pressure/rain for the storm overlay). Pages:
/tides/ hub (tool + by-state directory) and /tides/<slug>/ for 67 curated
stations (seo/tools/tide-stations.mjs — finite curated set, same indexing
philosophy as timer durations; the tool can still live-search all ~3,300 NOAA
stations via mdapi + geolocate the nearest). Features: next high/low summary,
storm+high-tide alert banner, canvas tide chart (7/14/30d, hover tooltip, red
dots on storm-flagged highs), date-range high-tide finder up to a year out
(min-height filter, highs-only, 👑 top-10% king tides, per-high weather cell),
storm & wind outlook card. ⚠ thresholds: gusts ≥35 mph, wind ≥25, pressure
≤996 hPa, heavy rain; advisory-only note points to NWS. All times parsed as
station-local wall time (manual parse — Safari-safe). Files: tide-stations.mjs,
build-tides.mjs, assets/js/tides.js (new); style.css (TOOLCSS block),
build-inline.mjs (pages list + tides JS bundle + needsToolCss + 🌊 nav link),
build-sitemap.mjs, package.json, CLAUDE.md. Verified via Playwright with
route-mocked APIs in documented shapes (alert, chart pixels, ⚠/👑 table,
search/switch station, no JS errors). Sandbox blocks egress, so live-API smoke
test happens in production — the UI surfaces a clear error per card if a
station/API misbehaves. Open Q: home-page tides card; per-station OG images.

### 2026-07-09 — Tides v2: own-site quality pass
Leveled /tides/ up in case it graduates to its own domain. New: today's full
tide list (chips, past dimmed); "Next king tides" card (scans a year of highs,
top 10% clustered into ≤4-day windows, capped so quantized data can't chain);
Sun & moon card (sunrise/sunset via the NOAA solar approximation converted to
station wall time using Open-Meteo's utc_offset_seconds; moon phase +
illumination from the mean synodic month — all computed locally, no API);
night-shaded tide chart (sunset→sunrise bands); CSV export of the finder;
per-station "About this station" with coordinates, coast region, and the 5
nearest stations with computed miles (unique per-page content), plus
BreadcrumbList + Place/GeoCoordinates JSON-LD and king-tide keywords in
titles; 🌊 home-page card with popular-station chips. Files: tides.js,
build-tides.mjs, build-home.mjs, style.css. Verified with mocked APIs in a
real browser: today chips, plausible Miami sun times (6:36 AM/8:15 PM),
king windows, CSV download (tides-8723214.csv), night bands painted, no JS
errors. Follow-ups: per-station OG images; live smoke test in production.

### 2026-07-09 — Tides v3: slider chart, storm rows + toggle, saved stations, 100 stations
Chart range is now a 1–30 day slider (today-baseline) replacing the 7/14/30
chips. Storm overlay: forecast storm hours merge into 🌀 "storm window" rows
interleaved chronologically in the tide table (when a storm may intensify the
tides), affected highs show a minimum surge estimate (inverse-barometer, ~1 ft
per 30 hPa below 1013), and a "Storm overlay" toggle switches the whole page
(table columns/rows, ⚠ flags, red chart dots, alert) to the pure astronomical
tide. Picking a station now NAVIGATES — curated stations go to their dedicated
page (fixes "Miami" H1 over Newport data; hub = search home, ?station= for
non-curated). Saved stations: ☆ Follow list + Set-as-default (localStorage
td_favs/td_home), "My stations" dropdown for one-tap switching, default
auto-loads on the hub; TIDE_CFG now carries slug so saved curated stations
deep-link to their page. +33 stations (big-city gaps, famous beaches, and the
giant-tide set: Skagway/Valdez/Seward/Nikiski/Kodiak/Ketchikan, plus Guam +
Pago Pago) → 100 pages; hub gains an "America's biggest tides" section and a
world/US highest-tides FAQ (Bay of Fundy honesty). Files: tides.js,
build-tides.mjs, tide-stations.mjs, style.css. All verified with mocked APIs:
surge label on a storm-aligned high, toggle on/off, slider refetch, search nav,
follow/default/dropdown flows. Decision: NOT generating thousands of pages for
every searchable location — curated 100 + live all-station search keeps the
index clean.

### 2026-07-09 — Tides: beta label + safety/liability disclaimers
Every tides page now opens with a "🧪 Beta" notice: data from NOAA/Open-Meteo
can be wrong/delayed/unavailable; NOT for navigation, boating, swimming, flood
response, or any decision affecting safety, health, property, or money; use
official sources (NOAA/NWS/USCG/local authorities); use at your own risk. The
tides footer repeats: provided "as is" — use at your own risk; we accept no
liability. terms.html gains §5 "Tide, weather & marine data (beta)"
(id=tides-data; sections 5–11 renumbered 6–12) with the full disclaimer +

weather information and reliance on it. Files: build-tides.mjs (BETA_NOTE,
TIDE_FOOTER), terms.html, style.css (.td-beta). Verified banner + footer render
on hub and station pages.

### 2026-07-09 — Tides: movable chart window + storm bands on the chart
Chart window is now fully movable: From/To date pickers (min today, max ~a year
out — as far as NOAA predictions go) + a 1–30-day span slider, all kept in
sync; To beyond 30 days clamps with a persistent "max window" note; default =
the next 4 days. Decoupled the next-tide summary/today chips from the chart
fetch (new sumHilo today→+2d) so they stay live when the chart is scrolled to a
future window. Storms now draw ON the chart: translucent red bands over
forecast storm windows (labelled 🌀 + max gust when wide enough), alongside the
night shading and red high-tide dots — storm and tide timing read together;
bands honor the storm-overlay toggle. Files: tides.js, build-tides.mjs,
style.css. Verified with mocks: default 4-day window from today, start+60d

### 2026-07-09 — Tides: "purpose of your search" + usage analytics

### 2026-07-09 — Tides: pressure line, graded storm shading, city directory, biggest-tides page

### 2026-07-09 — Tides chart: dual-handle date slider
Replaced the single span slider with a DUAL-handle slider (two overlaid range

next 364 days — both the start and end handles are draggable. Start-date picker
sits top-left, end-date picker top-right, above the slider; both boxes update
LIVE while dragging (paintWin on every input event), fetch on release. Window

(chartNote explains). Pickers still drive the slider too. Files: tides.js,

on release, layout screenshot, no JS errors.

### 2026-07-09 — Tides hub: "how fast the tide comes in" safety card
Added a hub-only card on tidal speed as the hidden danger, fact-checked before
writing: low→high duration is essentially fixed (~6¼ h semidiurnal), so a
bigger range MUST rise proportionally faster (Anchorage ~6–7 ft/hr at peak vs
inches on a 3-ft coast); the Rule of Twelfths (1-2-3-3-2-1) means half the
rise lands in the middle two hours — the accurate version of the owner's
"10-ft tide in 1–2 hours" intuition; regime differences (diurnal Gulf ~12 h =
slower) and bore/asymmetric estuaries (Turnagain, Severn) are where a flood
really is compressed. Practical guidance: flats/sandbars fill behind you,
head back before the middle hours, earlier with onshore wind or a ⚠ flag.
File: build-tides.mjs (hub only, per request).

### 2026-07-09 — Tides dual slider: fixed overlap wedge; 4-day min; chart context
Root cause of the "handles mess up" bug: with a 364-day track and ≤30-day
windows, the two native range thumbs sit pixels apart (touching at 4 days), and
the top input always steals the grab — drags wedged and could collapse the
window to 0–1 days, mangling the chart. Fix: pointer handling is now CUSTOM on
the track — pointerdown picks the NEAREST handle (tie → direction), pointer
capture drives it, release commits; the two <input type=range> stay for
keyboard access only (all pointer-events off, touch-action:none on the track).
Window is now 4–30 days (MINW=4 per owner; pushing past a limit slides the
other handle). Chart adds context: fetch pads the chosen window (1 day/side at
≤6 days, else 2 — "½–2 days, use your judgment"), out-of-window regions draw
dimmed with dashed gold lines at the exact chosen start/end; night-shade loop
bound widened for the padded span; To-picker min = today+4. Verified with real
mouse drags (after fixing the test itself — elements were below the fold, so
earlier drags landed outside the viewport): live picker updates mid-drag,
30-day push, 4-day floor on overlap attempts, padded fetch (today−1→+5),
screenshot shows bright window between dashed bounds with dimmed context.
Files: tides.js, build-tides.mjs, style.css.

## 2026-07-09 — Fix: two Default radios could show at once (dup My Stations)
Owner hit "I added a default and now have two." Root cause: renderMyStations
assigns radio .checked while the inputs are still DETACHED from the DOM —
radio-group exclusivity doesn't apply there — so if td_favs held the same
station twice (older flows could save it with a string id one time and a
number id another; entries were never deduplicated), BOTH rows rendered with
a checked Default radio. Fix: favGet() now normalizes every id to a string
and drops duplicates as it reads; homeGet() normalizes too; every id
comparison across the feature (radio checked, items unshift, edit-remove,
saved-note, Add nearest, Add to My Stations) is String()-safe; selecting a
default persists the deduped list; and boot self-heals stored duplicates
once per load so already-corrupted devices clean themselves. Regression test
seeds the exact corruption (string + number id + numeric default) and
asserts one row per station, exactly one checked radio, self-healed storage,
and that switching defaults leaves exactly one. Also verified the hub and
station admin sections are byte-identical in generated HTML (the owner's
"copy the admin section to /tides" request was already satisfied by the
previous unification deploy — they were looking at the prior cached build).
Files: assets/js/tides.js.

## 2026-07-09 — One default, everywhere: hard invariant + cross-page sync
Owner saw THREE checked Default radios. Two causes addressed. (1) Hard
invariant in renderMyStations: a defSeen flag lets at most ONE radio render
checked regardless of how mangled the stored data is (radio-group
exclusivity doesn't apply while inputs are detached, so corrupted duplicates
used to all light up); combined with favGet's dedupe/normalize + boot
self-heal, a hostile fixture (same station 3×, junk/null ids, numeric
default) now collapses to clean rows with exactly one default. (2) The
default wasn't sticking across ALREADY-OPEN pages: each page rendered its
panel from localStorage once at load, so other tabs (or bfcache-restored
pages) kept stale defaults — which reads exactly like "3 defaults" across 3
tabs. Now every tide page live-syncs: a storage event from another tab, a
pageshow from the back-forward cache, or regaining visibility re-reads
localStorage and re-renders the My Stations panel, saved note, and purpose
count. E2E: triple-corruption test + a real two-tab test (radio in tab 2 →
tab 1 auto-updates) — hub suite 47 checks, chart suite 40, all green.
Files: assets/js/tides.js.

## 2026-07-10 — Owner traffic excluded from stats via a device flag
Owner asked to filter their own traffic. IP filtering rejected (rotating
home/cellular IPs, and an IP in the public repo is a privacy smell); instead
a device flag: opening /admin/stats sets localStorage ac_notrack=1
automatically, and both the main-page visit beacons (build-inline snippet)
and the tide-purpose tally (purposeTally) skip flagged devices. The stats
page shows the exclusion status with a "Count this device again" undo link
(and a re-enable link if unset). Set once per browser/device by opening the
stats page there; private/incognito windows aren't flagged. Verified the
full loop: fresh visitor counts → open stats → browsing fires no beacons,
purpose picks aren't tallied → undo → counting resumes. Tide suites green.
Files: seo/tools/build-inline.mjs, assets/js/tides.js,
admin/stats/index.html.

## 2026-07-10 — Admin reports: filter dropdown by report type
/admin/reports/ now shows a dropdown after loading, listing each distinct
report type present in the stored data with its count ("Site idea (2)",
"Spam or scam (1)", …) plus a default "All reports (N)" option, sorted
alphabetically. Selecting a type filters the table in place and the status
line reads "X of N report(s) — <type>"; the dropdown stays hidden when
there are no reports. Purely client-side — items are cached from one
/api/reports fetch and re-rendered on change; no API changes. Verified via
mocked-API Playwright run (19 checks: option counts, filtering, restore,
empty state). Files: admin/reports/index.html.

## 2026-07-10 — Stats: one visit per IP address per day
/api/views now double-dedupes the dashboard numbers: the hourly (vh:) and
daily (vd:) stats buckets only bump on the FIRST visit from an IP address
per page per day ("sip:" keys, ~36h TTL, storing only a short h36 hash of
the address — never the raw IP). Repeat visits, extra browsers/devices on
the same connection, and privacy-mode visits all collapse to one stat/day,
which also caps the owner's own traffic at one per page per day (on top of
the ac_notrack device flag). The public per-countdown counter (v:) keeps
its per-browser dedupe so shared IPs (offices, CGNAT) aren't undercounted
where the number is visible to visitors. Verified with wrangler pages dev
local KV: 3 browsers on 2 IPs → public count 5, stats 3; bots and repeats
counted nowhere. Stats-page copy updated to describe the rule.
Files: functions/api/views.js, admin/stats/index.html.

## 2026-07-11 — Multi-agent audit (27 agents) + 14 fixes
Ran a 6-dimension adversarially-verified audit (js-correctness, functions
security, a11y, seo-meta, perf-weight, content-quality; every finding
re-verified by a skeptic agent; 19 confirmed). Fixed:
- SECURITY: /api/share-email was an open relay (any recipient, any link,
  sent from our signed domain) — the shared url must now be on
  alarm-clock.org.
- Alarm: startSound() now clears any prior ring interval (two same-minute
  alarms used to orphan an unstoppable 800ms beep loop); shared ?a= links
  now dedup against existing alarms and strip the query via replaceState
  (used to re-add a duplicate on every reload).
- create.js/quick-create.js: \p{...} regex literal (parse-time SyntaxError
  in pre-2018 engines, killing the whole builder) → RegExp constructor in
  try/catch with an ASCII-ish fallback.
- countdown.js: invalid ?tz= no longer throws an uncaught RangeError
  (falls back to the visitor's zone; ticks instead of a dead page).
- /work/ "Last workday before": Tue/Wed holidays now target the day
  before (previously fell through to the holiday itself).
- 108 event pages' "plan an event" CTA pointed at nonexistent /create/ —
  now /create-custom-countdown/ with the same prefill params.
- Home "Next 3-Day Weekend" teaser now targets the same moment as the
  /work/long-weekend/ page default (first day, 9am — was 5pm-day-before).
- PERF: event pages no longer ship ~12KB of theme-palette CSS they never
  use (usesThemes dropped eventRels; ~1.9MB across 162 pages).
- A11Y: live 7-segment displays mirror their digits into aria-label;
  alarm on/off toggles labeled; timer h/m/s inputs labeled; /work/
  settings selects labeled; invalid listbox/option roles removed from
  search results; role=menu removed from the timer sound menu; tides
  Saved Stations/Edit/Purpose buttons expose aria-expanded (Mutation
  Observer) + purpose input labeled; admin stats sortable headers are
  keyboard-operable with aria-sort.
Deferred (documented, larger refactors): TOOLCSS per-family split
(~24.5KB unused CSS on tide pages), externalizing the 60KB tides.js
bundle for cross-page caching, tide FAQ near-duplication across 102
pages, SpaceX stale dates (needs network). Both tide e2e suites green;
14 browser checks on the fixes.
Files: functions/api/share-email.js, seo/tools/alarm-widget.mjs,

## 2026-07-11 — Sun dial: hand can never go stale
Investigated the caching concern: the served/edge-cached HTML contains an
EMPTY svg — the whole dial including the hand is drawn client-side at
view time, so cache TTLs can't freeze the hand. Fixed the real staleness
paths instead: (1) visibilitychange repaint — background tabs throttle
timers, so a tab refocused hours later now redraws the hand immediately;
(2) fixed a live-interval leak where changing the date picker left the
old today-interval repainting its stale wedge over the new view (token
on the svg makes superseded drawDial calls self-clean, detached minis
clear their timers); (3) hub mini dials switched to live (tick every
minute + refocus repaint); (4) midnight rollover — a page left open past
midnight (picker on today) flips dial, stats, date field and 7-day table
to the new day. 8/8 Playwright checks incl. geometric hand-angle vs
current minute. Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs (+ regenerated).

## 2026-07-11 — Sun search: "Canby Oregon" + Enter now resolves
Two root causes fixed: Enter before the geocoder debounce did nothing,
and "City State" queries were sent verbatim to Open-Meteo (which finds
nothing for "Canby Oregon"). Now: splitPlace() parses trailing state
names/abbreviations ("canby oregon", "salem, or") — the geocoder is
queried with the city alone and results filtered to that state; local
matching understands the same syntax (Salem Oregon → /sun/salem-or/
directly). Enter/Go with no match navigates to noindex /sun/anywhere/
?q=…, which auto-resolves: existing city page → redirect there;
geocoder hit → renders that location (name in the H1/URL); nothing →
"No location found for “X” — check the spelling…" message (shown in
the hub dropdown too) while the clock-nearest city renders as
fallback. 11/11 Playwright checks (mocked geocoder incl. two-state
Canby disambiguation). Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs (+ regenerated).

descriptions from the generic "Set a free timer…" to a hook + real
benefits that answer the top search hesitations — free, sounds an alarm
even in a background tab, works on any device, no sign-up — under 160
chars, no "loud" over-claim (volume depends on the device). Added
per-page structured data to all 62 duration pages: BreadcrumbList

tabs? / works on my phone? / what's it good for?) — the visible+schema
pairing is what makes the pages eligible for FAQ rich results, the
biggest CTR lever for these near-identical listings. Verified: desc 150
chars, 3 valid ld+json blocks, FAQ schema count matches visible.

## 2026-07-12 — World clock City/State/Country + homepage nearest-beach tide card
World clock main cards now label locations "City, ST, USA" (US) or
"City, Country" (elsewhere) via locLabel() — added st to the 6 US zones.
Homepage tide card is now LOCATION-AWARE while staying static/fast:
SSR renders the rotating featured beach + an illustrative curve + its 3
nearest stations (city-name-only chips) so it paints instantly with no
layout shift. A small inline script then (1) fetches the featured beach's
LIVE NOAA predictions and redraws the curve with real data, and (2) calls
the new /api/geo Cloudflare Function (reads Cloudflare's edge IP-geo —
no external API, no cookies) to find the visitor's NEAREST station,
swapping the title/link/curve to it and replacing the nearby chips with
the visitor's 3 closest stations. Inland users get the nearest coastal
station (= nearest big-city beach). Verified with mocked geo+NOAA:
San Diego visitor → "Tide chart for San Diego, CA", nearby La Jolla /
Newport Beach / Los Angeles, 60-point live curve, 2 high-tide dots, no
JS errors. Everything degrades gracefully (no JS / geo denied → the SSR
featured beach with real data if the NOAA fetch succeeds).
Files: functions/api/geo.js (new), seo/tools/build-home.mjs,
build-world-clock.mjs, CLAUDE.md (+ regenerated).
