## 2026-07-12 — Homepage tide card: geolocation-gated nearest station
Reworked the tide card to key off explicit location SHARING (browser
geolocation permission) rather than silent edge-IP geo. States:
• Shared/granted → heading "Tide chart for <City, ST>", the nearest
  station's LIVE NOAA chart (clickable to its page), and the 3 next-
  nearest as city-name buttons (each linking to its tide page).
• Not shared (prompt/denied/no Permissions API) → the chart is replaced
  by a dashed "Enable location sharing to see local tide charts" button
  (location-pin SVG, sized to the chart so there's no layout shift);
  clicking it triggers the browser prompt, and on grant the nearest
  chart loads. A denied click shows "Location is blocked — allow it in
  your browser…".
• No-JS / crawlers keep the static featured-beach chart + link in the
  HTML (SEO preserved; the beach still rotates per build).
Bottom link shortened to "Search all tide charts". /api/geo is no longer
called here (left in place as a utility). Verified 3 states in-browser
(San Diego granted → nearest+3; not-granted → button; click→grant NYC →
Sandy Hook/Kings Point/Bridgeport), no JS errors.
Files: seo/tools/build-home.mjs, assets/css/style.css (+ regenerated /).

## 2026-07-13 — Homepage tide card: real chart detail (scale + hi/lo dots)
Redesigned the mini tide chart to carry the same INFORMATION as the real
/tides/ chart — ft-scale gridlines with labels, day gridlines with real
dates, and both HIGH (gold) and LOW (gray) dots — with none of its
interactivity (no days selector, no zoom, no drag/tooltip). Card grew
from a 320x96 abstract sine teaser to a 320x150 chart with proper axis.
- SSR illustrative version (build-home.mjs tideCurveSvg, shared geometry
  constants in TIDE_MINI_GEO): a real 4-day/8-cycle curve with real
  build-date day labels, vmin/vmax=-1..3ft gridlines, alternating gold/
  gray extrema — so no-JS visitors and crawlers see genuine scale +
  dots, not just a decorative squiggle.
- Client redraw (granted-location path): now fetches BOTH interval=h
  (smooth curve) and interval=hilo (exact highs/lows) for a 4-day window
  from NOAA, mirroring the real chart's own fetch pattern, and rebuilds
  the mini SVG's innerHTML with real ft gridlines, real day labels, and
  true high/low dots from the hilo product (previously only derived
  "highs" from a coarse 30-min curve via local-maxima approximation).
- Card & enable-location button both use aspect-ratio:320/150 (was a
  fixed 96px height) so sizing matches exactly at any width — verified
  the no-JS static chart and the not-granted button render at the same
  ~2.13 aspect ratio, no layout shift between states.
Verified: SSR has 5 ft gridlines + 4 day labels + 8 gold/8 gray dots;
live path (mocked NOAA h+hilo) renders real ft scale + day labels + 4
gold/4 gray dots matching the mock; no JS errors.
Files: seo/tools/build-home.mjs, assets/css/style.css (+ regenerated /).

### 2026-07-13 — Homepage tide-card loading state + world clock city/region labels
Two fixes. (1) The homepage tide card was flashing the SSR-featured
beach (often far from the visitor, e.g. Florida) before swapping to the
visitor's real nearest station once geolocation resolved. Made the
"hide the wrong SSR content" step happen synchronously as soon as
`navigator.geolocation` is known to exist (before the permission check
and NOAA fetches even start), added a new neutral third state — a
spinner card ("Loading your local tide chart…") — to bridge the gap,
and only reveal the real chart + "Tide chart for <city>" label together,
atomically, once genuine NOAA data has actually been drawn. Also added
a defined failure path (previously a silent catch that would leave the
spinner stuck forever): if a located station's fetch fails, show the
enable-location button with a "Couldn't load that tide chart — try
again" message instead. (2) World clock rows showed only "City,
Country" — added a `region` field (short, universally-recognized
abbreviations only: US state codes, Canadian province codes, Australian
state codes, Brazilian state codes) so labels now read "City, Region -
Country" (e.g. "Honolulu, HI - USA", "Halifax, NS - Canada", "São
Paulo, SP - Brazil") where a short code exists, falling back to plain
"City, Country" everywhere else (Tokyo, London, Paris, etc.) per the
user's own mobile-width caveat — no exhaustive worldwide province
mapping attempted.
Verified via Playwright (mocked geolocation + NOAA responses): granted-
permission path shows the spinner before data resolves then the loaded
Astoria, OR chart+label together; not-granted path shows the enable-
location button immediately with no SSR flash; world clock renders all
30 labels cleanly at mobile width with no overflow. Screenshots:
tide-loading-state.png, tide-loaded-astoria.png, world-clock-labels.png.
Files: seo/tools/build-home.mjs, assets/css/style.css,
seo/tools/build-world-clock.mjs (+ regenerated /, /world-clock/).

### 2026-07-13 — Homepage: new Sunrise & Sunset card; tide chart + world clock moved lower
Owner wanted the tide card (async, geolocation-dependent) out of the very top
of the homepage on mobile, plus a new sunrise/sunset card above it, plus the
world clock grouped with it. Restructured the portal layout:
- `.tc-top-row` is now just Alarm Clock + Important Work Countdowns (2 cards,
  CSS grid updated from a stale 3-column `1.5fr 1fr 1fr` to `1.7fr 1fr`).
- New full-width "Sunrise & Sunset" card (`#home-sun`) sits right after the
  Timer/Stopwatch/Calendar/Sporting-Events/420 grid: 8 mini sun-dial
  subcards (Honolulu HI, Seattle WA, Phoenix AZ, Denver CO, Chicago IL, New
  Orleans LA, Miami FL, Boston MA — picked for latitude/timezone spread), 4
  wide on desktop via the existing `.tool-grid` breakpoints, reusing the
  `.sun-mini-card` styling and `drawDial`/`sunCalc` renderer already built
  for the `/sun/` hub's "near you and beyond" mini dials. Unlike the tide
  card this needed no loading state at all — it's pure synchronous local
  SunCalc math (no fetch, no permission prompt), so the dials just paint on
  load; SSR ships empty dial circles + "—" times for crawlers/no-JS.
- Below that, a new 2-card row holds the Tide Charts card (moved out of the
  top row) and the World Clock card (moved out of the tool grid) side by
  side — grouping the three location-flavored cards together, well below
  the mobile fold, so none of their async reveals read as an up-top flash.
- **Refactor to make the dial reusable:** `SUN_JS`/`DIAL_JS` (the SunCalc
  math + 24-hour dial SVG renderer, previously local consts in
  build-sun.mjs) moved to the shared `lib.mjs` module — build-sun.mjs runs
  top-level file-writing side effects at import time, so build-home.mjs
  importing directly from it would have re-run the entire /sun/ build as a
  side effect. Both generators now import from lib.mjs; build-sun.mjs's
  behavior/output is unchanged (byte-identical dial code, just relocated).
Verified with Playwright: card order top-to-bottom is Alarm/Work → tool
grid → Sunrise & Sunset (8 dials, correct city/state labels and plausible
rise/set times) → Tide+World Clock row → search → upcoming events, at both
1280px and 390px widths; `/sun/miami/` and the `/sun/` hub's 8 mini-dials
still render correctly post-refactor with no console errors. `npm run
check` clean apart from the pre-existing sandbox network-block findings.
Files: lib.mjs, build-sun.mjs, build-home.mjs, style.css (+ regenerated
/, /sun/, all /sun/<city>/ pages).

### 2026-07-14 — Tide pages: liability/risk pass on storm & surge language
Owner shared a legal/risk review flagging the tide section's storm-risk and
surge-estimate features as the site's highest-liability content: the
disclaimer and Terms were called out as good, but the feature framing
("windows most likely to bring minor coastal flooding", "surge estimate",
"storm flag") reads as personalized safety guidance, not general data
labels. Implemented every item on the reviewer's "what I'd change
immediately" list:
- **Renamed the storm feature away from alert/prediction language**
  throughout build-tides.mjs and assets/js/tides.js: "storm risk" → "weather
  overlap", "storm flag(s)"/"storm-flagged" → "weather overlap
  indicator(s)"/flag, the "Storm & wind outlook" card → "Weather overlap
  outlook", the "Storm + high tide" alert → "Weather overlap at next high
  tide", the 🌀 table rows → "Weather overlap window", page titles ("...with
  Storm Alerts") → "...with Weather Overlap", meta descriptions and JSON-LD
  descriptions synced to match.
- **Surge language reframed as a simplified adjustment, not a forecast:**
  "minimum surge estimate" → "simplified pressure-only adjustment"; the
  inline "+X ft surge possible" table badge → "+X ft (pressure-only est.)"
  with a title tooltip spelling out "not a surge forecast"; the CSV export
  column "Storm flag" → "Weather overlap".
- **Added the reviewer's exact persistent qualifier** — "Not a flood,
  storm-surge, navigation, or public-safety forecast." — directly beside
  every red warning indicator: the chart legend hint, the finder's weather
  hint, the Weather overlap outlook card, and the dynamic
  weather-overlap-at-next-high-tide alert box.
- **Softened the king-tide card's "best days for X" recommendation framing**
  to a descriptive "biggest predicted tides... check current conditions and
  official NOAA/NWS guidance before planning any beach, boating or shoreline
  activity," and synced the "How fast does the tide come in?" educational
  card's storm-flag reference to the new terminology plus an explicit
  "general background, not a personal safety plan" framing.
- **Added a new "Official sources — storms, flooding & safety" card** to
  every one of the 101 curated station pages, linking directly to (1) NOAA's
  own official prediction page for that exact station
  (tidesandcurrents.noaa.gov/noaatidepredictions.html?id=), (2) the National
  Weather Service point forecast + active alerts for that lat/lon
  (forecast.weather.gov/MapClick.php), and (3) the National Hurricane
  Center — the three sources the reviewer asked to be linked per station.
- **Synced terms.html's tide-data clause** (id="tides-data") to the new
  terminology and added a line noting every station page links to those
  three official sources.
- Refactor: moved SUN_JS/DIAL_JS out of scope for this change (unrelated);
  no code restructuring here, pure copy/link changes — `stormAt`/`stormOn`/
  etc. internal JS identifiers and the `td-storm-row`/`td-warn` CSS classes
  were left as-is (implementation detail, not user-facing).
Verified with Playwright against mocked NOAA + Open-Meteo responses forcing
a live weather-overlap condition: the high-tide alert, chart legend, finder
hint, Weather overlap outlook card, and finder table's overlap-window row +
pressure-only badge all render the new wording exactly as written; the new
NOAA/NWS/NHC links resolve to correct per-station URLs (verified station
9439040 → forecast.weather.gov/MapClick.php?lat=46.21&lon=-123.77, etc.).
`npm run check` unaffected (it only audits events.json links, not tide
pages). Did NOT touch the "not for safety decisions" disclaimer banner or
the Terms' core liability/no-warranty language, which the review said were
already strong — only the feature framing and the missing official-source
links changed.
Files: seo/tools/build-tides.mjs, assets/js/tides.js, terms.html
(+ regenerated /tides/, all 101 station pages, 20 state pages).

## 2026-07-14 — Removed tides from nav/homepage, purpose button, and the entire custom-countdown feature

- **Tides**: removed the "Tide Charts & Times" link from the nav dropdown
  (`build-inline.mjs` MENU) and the tide card + featured-beach preview from
  the homepage (`build-home.mjs`: `BEACHES`, `tideCurveSvg()`, `TIDE_MINI`/
  `FEATURED_BEACH`/`AC_TIDES`/`AC_TIDE_FEAT` globals, the `.tc-tide` HTML
  block). `/tides/` itself and all station/state pages are untouched and
  still fully live — only the nav entry point and homepage card are gone.
  `functions/api/geo.js` is now unused (was only called by the removed
  featured-beach card); left in place, documented as dead in CLAUDE.md.
- **Tides purpose button**: removed the "Why are you checking tides?" panel
  from `/tides/` — `PURPOSE_HTML` in `build-tides.mjs`, and the
  `purposeGet/Store/Tally/Add/Remove` + panel-wiring code in
  `assets/js/tides.js`. `functions/api/purpose.js` still exists (GET-only,
  shows historical totals) but nothing writes new entries anymore.
- **Custom countdowns — full removal.** The user decided the risk of letting
  anyone generate a shareable countdown with arbitrary name/message text
  (impersonation, unauthorized use of someone's name/event, disparagement)
  wasn't worth it, even with moderation safeguards in place. Removed the
  entire feature and every reference to it:
  - Deleted: `build-create-pages.mjs`, `seo/templates/create.html` (+ now-
    empty `seo/templates/`), `quick-create-widget.mjs`, `create.js`,
    `quick-create.js`, `presets.js`, `sounds.js`, `share.js`, `countdown.js`,
    `create-occasions.json`, `create/`, `create-custom-countdown/`,
    `functions/api/share-email.js`, `functions/api/created.js`,
    `functions/api/blocked.js`, `admin/blocked/`.
  - `c.html` and `widget/index.html` rewritten as static noindex "no longer
    available" notice pages (both linking to `/countdown/`) — `/widget/`
    only ever produced `/c?...&embed=1` iframes, so it's non-functional
    either way once `/c` stops rendering dynamically.
  - `functions/_middleware.js` rewritten from 138 → ~50 lines: dropped the
    OG-tag `HTMLRewriter` rewriting and the `isDisplayPath()` dynamic-render
    fallback for singular `/<type>-countdown/<slug>[/<year>]` URLs (that
    fallback *was* the custom-countdown-serving mechanism for pretty URLs;
    curated pages use a different plural `/<category>-countdowns/<slug>/`
    scheme with real static files, so they never hit it). Also dropped
    `logBlock`/`maybeEmailBlock` (misuse-notification emails) per explicit
    instruction — kept a minimal block-and-redirect check on `/api/og`
    since it's still a live endpoint rendering visitor-supplied text.
  - `build-inline.mjs`: removed the create/quick-create bundles, nav menu
    item, `occasions`/`AC_UPCOMING`/`AC_OCCASIONS` machinery, PAGEVIEW_IDS
    entry; simplified `cssVariant`/`bundle` helpers accordingly.
  - Removed "Make your own countdown" CTAs and "How to embed" sections from
    build-home.mjs, build-events.mjs, build-countries.mjs, build-420.mjs,
    build-popular.mjs, build-calendar.mjs, build-category-pages.mjs,
    build-trending.mjs, build-microevents.mjs, build-sitemap.mjs; removed
    now-orphaned `.embed-how`/`.embed-code`/`.embed-how-actions` and the
    entire `html.embed` iframe-mode block from style.css, plus a stray
    orphaned `.tc-tides-sub` rule found during verification.
  - Updated `admin/stats/index.html`, `wrong-date/index.html`,
    `suggest-event/index.html`, `404.html`, `not-found.html`,
    `how-it-works/index.html` (rewrote the "Countdown" explainer tab to
    describe browsing/opening/sharing a curated page instead of the old
    5-step builder flow), 10 graduation-event blurbs in `events.json` that
    told readers to "make your own countdown."
  - Rewrote `privacy.html` (no accounts/no user-generated countdowns, no
    countdown-creation data collection) and `terms.html` (service
    description, acceptable use, content responsibility, DMCA notices) to
    stop describing removed functionality — kept edits minimal/factual,
    did not implement the broader Terms rewrite (operator identity,
    governing law) a separate pasted review had suggested.
  - `_redirects`: old `/create/` → `/create-custom-countdown/` redirect
    now points at `/countdown/` instead (target no longer exists); added a
    same-target redirect for `/create-custom-countdown/*`.
  - CLAUDE.md updated: build-pipeline table renumbered (step removed),
    data-files table entry removed, source-file list and Cloudflare
    Functions section rewritten to match current architecture.
  Verified with Playwright + curl against `wrangler pages dev`: nav has no

## 2026-07-14 (cont'd) — Reliability-claim & tide-liability fixes, round 2 review

Implemented the "critical: next deployment" tier of a second pasted review
(items 1-10 of that list); deferred the "high impact: next 2-4 weeks" tier
(world-clock SSR, sunrise/tide methodology pages, nav overhaul, new trust
pages, dedicated city pages) as a separate, much larger engagement to be
scoped with the user first.

- **Alarm** (`build-alarm.mjs`): dropped the absolute "screen stays on
  through the night" / "the alarm still rings on time" claims from the
  bedside-clock paragraph and FAQ answer. New wording says the page
  *requests* the screen stay awake on supported devices and that browser/
  device settings can still dim the display, suspend the page, mute audio,
  or block the alarm — matches what Terms §3 already said.
- **Timer** (`build-timers.mjs`, 4 call sites incl. FAQ + FAQPage JSON-LD):
  "sounds an alarm when it reaches zero, even if you switch tabs" →
  "continues in most modern browsers... attempts to sound an alarm,
  provided the page remains active and your browser permits audio."
- **Stopwatch** (`stopwatch/index.html`, hand-authored): "a precise,
  browser-based stopwatch" → "displays elapsed time to the hundredth of a
  second," plus a new line noting displayed precision doesn't make it
  suitable for official sports timing, scientific, or medical use.
- **Tides** (`build-tides.mjs`, `tides.js`, `style.css`): confirmed items
  4 (title) and 5 (surge wording) were already fixed in the prior tide
  round this session (title already reads "Weather Overlap" not "Storm
  Alerts"; surge language already "pressure-only adjustment," never
  "minimum"). New this round:
  - King-tide FAQ answer and the king-tide card reworded from "most likely
    to cause minor coastal flooding" to "periods with some of the highest
    predicted astronomical tides of the year... can increase
    coastal-flooding potential when combined with weather" (matches the
    review's suggested framing).
  - Rule of Twelfths section: added "a rough teaching approximation... not
    for calculating safe access or return times" directly into the passage,
    and replaced the specific "be headed back before the middle hours of
    the flood" return-time advice with the review's suggested generic
    caution (conservative margin, official local guidance).
  - Added a visible data-attribution line under the chart distinguishing
    NOAA CO-OPS (times/heights) from Open-Meteo (forecast) from
    alarm-clock.org (weather-overlap analysis).
  - Added explicit, non-drag chart navigation: « Week / ‹ Day / Today /
    Day › / Week » buttons (`.td-datenav`, wired to the same
    `setSel`/`commitSel` functions the existing drag/zoom controls already
    use) plus a "Prefer a plain list?" link to the Finder's table+CSV view
    — the chart is no longer the only way to move the date window.
  - Confirmed the tide "purpose" survey item is moot — that feature was
    already fully removed earlier today.
- **Terms** (`terms.html`, 3 spots — §1, §3, §9): softened "nothing is
  stored on our servers" / "never on our servers" / "we never see or store
  them" to "we do not maintain a permanent server-side database of..." with
  a note that standard security/request/analytics/abuse-prevention logs
  may still temporarily process information per the Privacy Policy —
  avoids an absolute claim contradicted by normal Cloudflare access logs.
- **Verified, no code change needed** (review items 8 & 17, shared alarm
  links): confirmed via code read that `/alarm-clock/?a=...` share links
  (1) never appear in `sitemap.xml` (build-sitemap only lists the bare
  `/alarm-clock/` and alarm-time pages), (2) self-canonicalize to
  `/alarm-clock/` via the page's `<link rel="canonical">`, preventing
  separate indexing, (3) use a static (non-dynamic) `og:image`/description
  so labels never leak into social previews, (4) never put the alarm label
  into `document.title` (stays a generic "⏰ Alarm!" while ringing), and
  (5) the pageview beacon posts only a fixed page id + anonymous visitor
  id — never the URL, query string, or label.
- Verified with Playwright + curl against `wrangler pages dev`: all new
  copy renders on `/alarm-clock/`, `/timer/`, `/stopwatch/`; the 5 tide
  date-nav buttons render and click without JS errors (NOAA/Open-Meteo
  fetches themselves are blocked by this sandbox's network policy, an
  environment limitation unrelated to the change — the nav buttons call
  the same `setSel`/`commitSel` functions the pre-existing, working
  drag/zoom controls already use). `npm run build` and `npm run check`
  both clean (same pre-existing SpaceX-date finding and external-link
  403 noise as before, nothing new).

## 2026-07-14 (cont'd) — Tide download disclaimer; alarm sharing/labels removed

Three follow-up requests after the round-2 review write-up:

- **Tide CSV/print disclaimer**: both export paths off the tide chart now
  carry the same safety line as the on-page banner ("General information
  only — not for any decision affecting safety, health, property, or
  money... Not a flood, storm-surge, navigation, or public-safety
  forecast..."). The CSV (`tides.js` csvBtn handler) gets it as a leading
  quoted row before the header row. The Print button (`window.print()`)
  turned out to hit an aggressive print stylesheet that hid *everything*
  except the bare table — including the on-screen disclaimer — so nothing
  safety-related was reaching the printed page at all; added a new
  `.td-print-note` div (screen: `display:none`; print: `display:block`)
  inside the finder card, placed so it isn't swept up by the print CSS's
  existing `.td-finder-card>p` / `.hint` hide rules (used a `<div>`, not a
  `<p>`, specifically to dodge those).
- **Alarm sharing removed entirely**, per explicit instruction ("Don't
  allow sharing of custom alarms"): deleted the share icon from the main
  control bar, the "Share" button from the "Your alarms" dialog, the whole
  `#ac-share-dlg` popup, and every JS function behind them (`shareURL`,
  `share`, `shareTargets`, `copyLink`, `shareRow`, `openShare`,
  `alarmToParam`). Also removed the *inbound* side — `parseURL()`/
  `specToAlarm()`, which read a `?a=time|label|repeat` query param into a
  new alarm on load — so a hand-crafted link can't inject an alarm either;
  `load()` now just reads localStorage. (Left the unrelated `?set=1`/
  `AC_SETTIME`/`popout=1`/`bedside=1` query params alone — those are the
  site's own preset-time landing-page mechanism, not user-generated
  content.) Removed the orphaned `.ac-share-*` CSS and the About page's
  "You can share a link that pre-loads a set of alarms" sentence.
- **Alarm labels no longer freely editable**, per explicit instruction
  ("only editable by the current function that inserts the date and
  time"): the label `<input>` is now `readonly` and always mirrors
  `autoLabel()` — the existing function that already generated a label
  like "7:00 AM · daily" from the time/repeat fields and let the visitor
  type over it. Removed the type-over path (`labelAuto` flag, the
  input-listener that tracked it) so there's no way to get arbitrary text
  into an alarm label anymore; `saveNew()` now stores `autoLabel()`
  directly rather than reading the (no-longer-editable) field. `fire()`'s
  ring/notification text simplified to match (no more "label — time"
  concatenation, which would have doubled up now that the label always
  includes the time). Together with the sharing removal, this closes the
  privacy vector the round-2 review flagged (labels like "Take insulin" or
  "Court hearing" leaving the device) at the source, rather than only
  gating it at the sharing step.
- Verified with Playwright: opening "Add" auto-fills the label (e.g. "Jul
  15 · 6:11 AM"), the field has the `readonly` attribute and rejects typed
  input, changing the time live-updates the label, and the saved alarm
  list shows no page errors. Confirmed via curl that `#ac-share`,
  `#ac-edit-share`, and `#ac-share-dlg` no longer render, and that the
  CSV/print disclaimer text is present in source. `npm run build` and
  `npm run check` clean (same pre-existing SpaceX-date finding only).
Files: seo/tools/alarm-widget.mjs, build-alarm.mjs, build-tides.mjs,
assets/js/tides.js, assets/css/style.css.

## 2026-07-14 (cont'd) — Technical audit: security headers, sitemap lastmod, canonical

User pasted a strategic review (items 23-30: reposition as a "time
information platform," internal linking, FAQ expansion, trust pages,
linkable content ideas) and said the next phase would be a deep technical
audit (structured data, canonical, robots, sitemap, security headers, CWV,
indexation). Asked the user how to sequence it; they deferred the choice.
Started with the technical audit since it's self-contained/verifiable and
doesn't need the user's voice (unlike the About/Data Sources/Editorial
Standards pages, which do). Findings + fixes:

- **Security headers were essentially absent.** `_headers` only set
  Cache-Control + one X-Robots-Tag for /admin — no CSP, no
  Strict-Transport-Security, no X-Content-Type-Options, no X-Frame-Options,
  no Referrer-Policy, no Permissions-Policy. Added all of them, scoped to
  what the site actually does (checked every external fetch/embed first):
  `connect-src` allows only NOAA CO-OPS + Open-Meteo (the only cross-origin
  fetches, both in tides.js/build-sun.mjs); `frame-src` allows only
  youtube-nocookie.com (the SpaceX-launch click-to-play embed);
  `frame-ancestors 'none'` + `X-Frame-Options: DENY` since the countdown
  widget/embed feature is gone and nothing should ever be framed.
  `script-src`/`style-src` need `'unsafe-inline'` because the site inlines
  every page's CSS/JS by design (build-inline.mjs) plus a couple of inline
  onclick= handlers — a nonce/hash-based CSP would need per-page hashes
  computed at build time, flagged as a larger follow-up, not attempted here
  given the blast radius of getting it wrong sitewide. `Permissions-Policy`
  blocks camera/microphone/payment/usb/etc. and FLoC (`interest-cohort=()`)
  while explicitly keeping geolocation/fullscreen/accelerometer/gyroscope
  allowed for self (sun/tides location search, alarm/timer full-screen, the
  alarm's OLED anti-burn-in devicemotion listener). Caught two real
  regressions via Playwright before shipping: `fullscreen=(self)` blocked
  the YouTube embed's own fullscreen button (fixed by allowlisting
  youtube-nocookie.com specifically), and `gyroscope=()` blocked the
  alarm's devicemotion-based burn-in shift (fixed by allowing it for self).
  Verified zero CSP/Permissions-Policy console violations across 18 page
  types after the fix, and confirmed Lighthouse best-practices/SEO/perf
  scores are unaffected (0.96-1.0, the one non-1.0 is a sandbox artifact —
  blocked NOAA/Open-Meteo calls in this environment, not a real issue).
- **Sitemap lastmod was a real anti-pattern.** Every single one of ~1,570
  URLs carried the same `<lastmod>` — the build's run date, stamped
  unconditionally on every page every time. Google's own guidance says a
  blanket "always today" lastmod is an unreliable signal it may discount
  sitemap-wide. Rewrote `build-sitemap.mjs` to compute each URL group's
  lastmod from the real git history of the generator + data file(s) that
  produce it (e.g. all `/tides/*` pages use the newer of
  `build-tides.mjs`/`tides.js`/`tide-stations.mjs`'s last commit date;
  birthday pages use `people.json`'s vs. other curated events using
  `events.json`'s). Falls back to today if git is unavailable or a path
  has no history (e.g. a shallow CI clone), so it degrades to the old
  behavior rather than breaking. Confirmed real differentiation:
  1,127 URLs now show yesterday's date, 441 show today's (accurately,
  since this session touched a lot today) — not 1,568 identical stamps.
- **Canonical tags**: found and fixed one page missing it entirely
  (`/widget/`, the discontinued-notice page — every other page, including
  its sibling `/c.html`, already had one).
- **Verified, no changes needed**: robots.txt (correct Allow/Disallow,
  `/api/og` carved out of the `/api` disallow); noindex coverage (only the
  9 pages that should be — error pages, admin, discontinued notices,
  report/wrong-date forms, /sun/anywhere/ — everything else defaults to
  indexable, no accidental noindex on curated content); JSON-LD across 11
  sampled page types (WebSite/WebApplication/FAQPage/BreadcrumbList/Event/
  Place all parse and carry required fields).
- **Flagged for the user's decision, not changed unilaterally**: birthday-
  countdown pages use `Event` schema, which Google's structured-data
  guidelines discourage for non-attendable content like a birthday date —
  stripping it from ~180 curated pages would lose any current Event
  rich-result eligibility, a real tradeoff not mine to make solo. Also
  noted (not fixed, low priority): a handful of titles/meta descriptions
  run a few characters past the soft 60/160 guideline in a random 40-page
  sample, and no duplicate titles found in that sample.
Files: _headers, seo/tools/build-sitemap.mjs, widget/index.html.

## 2026-07-14 (cont'd) — Bake real values into World Clock + 420 at build time

User asked whether pages could be rendered "server-side" on Cloudflare so
crawlers see real content (tides, the 420 clock, world clock) instead of
blank placeholders that only fill in via client JS — this is exactly the
gap round-2 review item #4 flagged for World Clock. Cloudflare Pages serves
static files (no per-request render), so true SSR isn't the mechanism;
explained the two real options — build-time baking (the same pattern
`update-spacex.mjs` already uses for SpaceX data) vs. a Cloudflare Function
rendering live data into the response before it's cached — and that tides
is the harder case (external NOAA/Open-Meteo APIs) needing its own
decision. User chose to scope this pass to World Clock + 420 only.

- **`build-world-clock.mjs`**: ported the client's exact tick() math
  (offMin/sunTimes/hm — same SunCalc-style formula) to Node, computed once
  per city at build time, and baked real values into every card's initial
  HTML: live time (wrapped in `<time datetime="2026-07-14T05:30:00+01:00">`
  per the review's suggested markup), ☀️/🌙 day-night icon, date + UTC
  offset, and sunrise/sunset — replacing the "--:--"/"—" placeholders that
  used to sit there until JS ran. Card order was already west→east in the
  source array, which doubles as a sensible static default (the client
  still re-sorts relative to the visitor's own zone on load, unchanged).
  The "vs your time" diff tag is left for the client alone to fill in,
  since there's no visitor to be relative to at build time.
- **`build-420.mjs`**: added `snapshot420()`, computing the real days
  remaining to the next 4:20 PM (daily page) / next April 20 (annual page)
  in UTC at build time, baked into the d/h/m/s digits instead of "--".
  Chose UTC as the build-time reference since there's no visitor to be
  timezone-correct for yet — the client's `target()` immediately
  recomputes the true, visitor-timezone-correct value within the same
  tick the page loads, exactly as before; this only changes what's in the
  page before JS runs.
- **`build-home.mjs`**: the homepage has its own smaller mini-widgets for
  both (6-city `#wc-mini` list, `#home-420` big digits) that had the same
  gap — same treatment, using the same UTC-at-build-time reference for the
  420 mini and per-timezone `Intl.DateTimeFormat` for the 6 world-clock
  mini cities.
- Verified with Playwright + curl: raw HTML (no JS) now shows real times/
  sun data/countdown digits on all three surfaces; after JS runs the values
  tick forward correctly with zero console errors; world-clock's
  visitor-relative reordering still works correctly on top of the
  pre-baked cards. Manually cross-checked the 420 daily/annual day counts
  against wall-clock math (280 days to next April 20 from July 14 — exact
  match). `npm run build`/`npm run check` clean, no new findings.
Files: seo/tools/build-world-clock.mjs, build-420.mjs, build-home.mjs.

## 2026-07-14 — Remove storm flags, weather overlap & barometric pressure from /tides/
- User asked to strip all storm/weather/barometric-pressure content from the
  tide tool. Removed: the "Weather overlap outlook" card (forecast-vs-tide
  overlap + criteria hint), the chart's storm-window shading + 🌀 labels +
  barometric-pressure line, the summary page's weather-overlap alert banner,
  the table's "Weather" column and surge-adjustment cell, the CSV export's
  weather column/reasons, and the FAQ question "Does weather change the
  tide?". Station pages' "Official sources" card trimmed to just NOAA's own
  prediction page (dropped the National Weather Service forecast + National
  Hurricane Center links). Updated terms.html §5 to drop the weather/marine
  forecast disclaimer language, and `_headers` CSP `connect-src` to drop
  `api.open-meteo.com` (the weather host — kept `geocoding-api.open-meteo.com`,
  a different endpoint used only by /sun/'s location search).
- Removing the weather fetch also removed its free side-effect: the station's
  UTC offset (used for sunrise/sunset + night-shading), which the old code
  derived from the Open-Meteo response. Replaced with the same technique
  `/world-clock/` already uses: each curated station's IANA time zone is now
  resolved once at build time via the vendored `tz-lookup` (no network call)
  and baked into `window.TIDE_STATIONS`/`TIDE_CFG`; the client computes an
  exact, DST-aware offset from it via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` — zero API calls,
  more accurate than the old weather-derived value. Non-curated stations
  (found via the hub's full NOAA-directory search, no baked zone) fall back to
  a longitude + US-DST-window approximation.
- While baking zones, systematically verified all 101 curated stations'
  tz-lookup results against their expected UTC offset and found two genuine
  misresolutions on political (not geographic) time-zone boundaries:
  `eastport-me` (was resolving to Atlantic Canada's `America/Moncton` instead
  of `America/New_York`) and `pago-pago-as` (was resolving to Samoa's
  `Pacific/Apia`, 24h off, instead of `Pacific/Pago_Pago` — American Samoa
  didn't switch date-line sides with Samoa in 2011). Added a `TZ_OVERRIDE`
  map in build-tides.mjs for these two.
- Verified: full `npm run build` + `npm run check` clean (the 715 "blocked"
  link findings are pre-existing 403s from this sandbox's outbound proxy on
  external sites, unrelated to this change). Grep swept all generated
  `/tides/` output + tides.js/style.css for storm/weather/pressure remnants —
  none remain except one internal, non-user-visible JS source comment
  documenting the API's scope. Confirmed via `wrangler pages dev` + Playwright
  that no weather UI renders and there's no console error from the change
  itself (NOAA fetches fail in this sandbox due to outbound proxy
  restrictions, not the code). Confirmed `window.TIDE_CFG.station.tz` bakes
  correctly for both override stations, and replicated the client's
  `resolveOffSec()` math in Node against real `Intl` data: eastport-me → -4h
  (EDT, correct for July), pago-pago-as → -11h (correct, no DST), a control
  station (san-francisco-ca) → -7h (PDT, correct) — the fix works end to end.
Files: seo/tools/build-tides.mjs, assets/js/tides.js, assets/css/style.css,
terms.html, _headers, CLAUDE.md, all generated tides/**/index.html.

## 2026-07-14 — V3 roadmap Phase 1 (product standards, browser limits, About)
- User supplied a "V3 Engineering & Quality Implementation Plan" — a large,
  14-section standards document (product standards checklist, browser
  limitations, About page, homepage focus, tide scope, world clock behavior,
  error reporting, editorial/style standards, QA checklist, long-term
  vision). Given CLAUDE.md's "one feature per session" rule and that several
  sections implied a full-site audit, asked the user up front how to phase
  it; they chose: do a well-scoped Phase 1 now (below), treat full-site
  audits (every generated page against the Product Standards checklist,
  sitewide button/style-guide pass) as separate future sessions since those
  are genuinely large, independent efforts.
- **Homepage**: title/H1 → "Online Alarm Clock, Timer, Stopwatch and
  Countdowns" (was "...& more") per the roadmap's exact wording. Removed the
  World Clock mini-card (roadmap explicitly lists it under "do not
  reintroduce") — World Clock stays in nav and as its own page, just not a
  homepage card. Merged the now-orphaned Sporting Event Countdowns card into
  the Birthday/Political row so no tool-grid is left with one lonely card.
  Cleaned up the now-dead `wcMiniTime`/`wcTick`/`#wc-mini` build-time-snapshot
  and client-tick code this card was the only consumer of, plus the
  now-unused `.wc-mini` CSS (kept `.cd-mini`, which is shared).
- **World Clock** (`/world-clock/`): was ticking every second with a flat
  `setInterval`; now ticks once a minute, aligned to the actual minute
  boundary (not 60s-from-page-load, which would drift the visible update
  away from the real minute tick), and pauses entirely via the Page
  Visibility API while the tab is hidden — catching up immediately on
  return so the display isn't stale for up to a minute after switching back.
  Dropped the seconds field from both the live display and the build-time
  SSR snapshot (a per-second display doesn't make sense once updates are
  once-a-minute). Verified with Playwright's `page.clock` (deterministic
  virtual time): confirmed no update while hidden across 5 virtual minutes,
  immediate catch-up on resume, and a normal tick when virtual time crosses
  a minute boundary while visible.
- **Tide pages**: per the roadmap's "no safety recommendations" line (in
  tension with its own Legal QA section wanting disclaimers *kept* — asked
  the user, they confirmed: keep disclaimers, cut advice), removed the one
  affirmative safety-advice sentence from the tide-speed educational card
  ("check official local guidance, observe posted warnings, and allow a
  conservative margin…"), keeping the surrounding tide-physics content
  (bores, flat-beach danger, Gulf tides) purely educational. Left every
  legal disclaimer as-is (the `notice-warn` "use at your own risk" banner,
  the print-note, the "not a flood/navigation/safety forecast" hints) —
  those satisfy the Legal QA section, not the "safety recommendations" ban.
- **Error reporting, sitewide**: the existing "Report abuse" footer link
  (auto-injected on every page by `build-inline.mjs`) didn't carry the
  page's own URL, so a report from e.g. a sunrise or tide page landed with
  no indication of which page it was about — `/report/` already had
  `?url=` prefill logic built in (used by the countdown-specific
  `/wrong-date/` flow) but nothing populated it for the generic report
  link. Fixed once, centrally: `injectReportLink()` now reads the page's
  own canonical URL (already set by `setHtmlOrigin()` earlier in the same
  pipeline pass) and appends it as `?url=`. This reaches every page in one
  change — including Sunrise, Tide and World Clock pages, which the roadmap
  named explicitly and which had no page-specific report path before.
  Chose this over adding the date-specific `/wrong-date/` form to those
  page types (its copy and required fields — "the correct date," a
  verification-source link — don't fit a sunrise/tide/timezone accuracy
  report) or building a third form (unnecessary; the existing `/report/` +
  `/api/report` pipeline just needed the URL wired through).
- **New pages**: `/browser-limitations/` (throttling, background tabs, Wake
  Lock, mobile OS behavior, battery optimization, why browser timing can't
  be guaranteed, when to use a dedicated device) and `/about/` (why the site
  exists, independent operation, commitments, how corrections/error
  reporting work) — both hand-authored root pages following the same
  pattern as `how-it-works/`, `report/`, `terms.html` (brand/nav markup
  that `build-inline.mjs`'s `injectNav()` replaces idempotently, a
  `<link rel="stylesheet">` placeholder `build-inline.mjs` inlines into a
  real `<style data-ac="css">` block). Registered both in `build-inline.mjs`'s
  `HTML` list and `build-sitemap.mjs`.
- **Linked Browser Limitations** from the pages the roadmap named: Alarm
  Clock (`/alarm-clock/` main page + the existing `/alarm-clock/about/`
  disclaimer page), Timer (the shared default "how it works" paragraph used
  by the hub and any use-case page that doesn't override it, the hub's own
  FAQ answer, and the per-duration-preset "how it works" paragraph — so all
  ~62 preset pages plus the hub carry the link with two shared-template
  edits, not 62 individual ones), Stopwatch (`stopwatch/index.html`), and
  Countdown pages (the one shared footer template in `build-events.mjs`
  used by every birthday/holiday/sports/event page).
- Verified: full `npm run build` (1572 files touched, zero warnings) and
  `npm run check` clean (same pre-existing 715 sandbox-network-blocked 403
  external-link findings as before, unrelated). Confirmed via `grep` that
  the report link on `/sun/new-york/`, `/tides/miami-fl/` and `/world-clock/`

## 2026-07-14 — V3 roadmap Phase 2 (exhaustive Product Standards + style audit)
- Continuation of the V3 plan: the user chose "exhaustive page-by-page audit"
  for the Product Standards checklist when asked earlier, so this pass
  actually checked every one of the site's 1,579 real pages rather than a
  per-template sample — done by writing a one-off Node audit script
  (`scratchpad/audit.mjs`, not committed) that scans every generated
  `index.html` + root `*.html` and checks: unique `<title>`, unique meta
  description, single `<h1>`, canonical present and correct for its own
  path, breadcrumb (HTML or JSON-LD) present, a report-error link present,
  and structured data present. Manual reading of 1,500+ pages wasn't
  practical; scripted structural checks against the *actual build output*
  gave real exhaustive coverage without the cost.
- First pass found 30 pages with no structured data, 5 with no
  canonical/breadcrumb, plus a couple of script false-positives (fixed the
  script, not the site): terms.html/privacy.html's extension-less canonical
  (`/terms` not `/terms.html`) is intentional — Cloudflare Pages' clean-URL
  handling serves it there — and `admin/*` (the owner-only, noindex
  dashboard) isn't public content the checklist is meant to cover, so it's
  excluded from the scan.
- Fixed the 30 missing-structured-data pages by adding `breadcrumbLD()`
  (already every other page's pattern) at each page's actual generator:
  `/popular/` + every `/<category>/popular/` page (shared `pageHtml()` in
  build-popular.mjs), `/trending/`, `/calendar/`, `/countdown/`,
  `/alarm-clock/about/`, the 7 timer use-case pages (egg/tea/study/
  meditation/plank/brush-teeth/workout — extended the existing `t.isDuration`
  breadcrumb+FAQ `ld` logic in build-timers.mjs so all timer pages, not just
  the 62 duration presets, carry a breadcrumb), and the hand-authored
  `how-it-works/`, `about/`, `browser-limitations/`, `affiliate-disclosure/`,
  `suggest-event/`, `promote-event/`, `terms.html`, `privacy.html` (inserted
  directly since these are static files, not generator output).
- The remaining 5 findings (missing canonical on `404.html`/`c.html`/
  `not-found.html`, missing breadcrumb on `widget/index.html`/`c.html`) are
  the discontinued-feature notice pages and error pages — all four are
  `<meta name="robots" content="noindex...">` by design, with an
  intentionally bare `.brand` (no logo/nav) to discourage engagement with a
  dead page. Canonical/breadcrumb don't apply to noindexed dead ends, so
  left as-is rather than forcing SEO furniture onto pages Google is told
  not to index.
- Also fixed two `/wrong-date/` links that weren't carrying `?url=` like
  the rest of the site (build-category-pages.mjs's per-category hub,
  build-countries.mjs's shared shell) — same auto-URL-prefill pattern used
  everywhere else, just missed in these two spots.
- **Style guide audit**: buttons — the homepage's Timer/Stopwatch mini-cards
  both said bare "Open" side by side (ambiguous when read out of context,
  e.g. by a screen reader tabbing through); changed to "Open Timer" /
  "Open Stopwatch" per the roadmap's canonical button set. Same fix on
  `how-it-works/`'s per-tab CTAs ("Open the timer" → "Open Timer", "Open the
  stopwatch" → "Open Stopwatch"). Dates — checked the site's actual date
  formatting: prominent single-date displays (an event page's main "when"
  statement, weekday name, "fact-checked" date) already use `month: "long"`
  (matches the roadmap's "July 14, 2026" example exactly); compact hub-list
  date badges intentionally use `month: "short"` for density across long
  lists — a deliberate, sensible two-tier pattern, not an inconsistency, so
  left alone rather than forcing "July" into every list row site-wide (would
  visibly break list-row width on mobile for no real benefit). Times — the
  World Clock already defaults to 12-hour with a "24-hour" toggle exactly
  per spec; internal `hourCycle:"h23"` usage elsewhere is programmatic epoch
  math, never rendered to a user; the curated-link time fallback already
  defaults to `"08:00"`.
- Verified: full `npm run build` (1562 files touched, zero warnings),
  `npm run check` clean (same pre-existing 715 sandbox-blocked findings),
  and a second audit-script pass confirmed the structural-data/canonical/
  breadcrumb counts dropped to exactly the 5 expected, deliberate
  exemptions. Confirmed the new JSON-LD parses (spot-checked
  `timer/egg-timer/`) and the button-label changes render correctly via
  Playwright against `wrangler pages dev`, with zero console errors.

## 2026-07-14 — Full-screen alarm: Browser Limitations + Warnings links, new screen-warnings page
- User asked for a "Browser Limitations" link and a "Warnings" link in small
  text at the bottom of the full-screen bedside alarm clock — specifically
  visible in the minimal state once the on-screen controls fade away, not
  just a fleeting link that fades with them. Screen burn-in/warnings content
  didn't exist anywhere on the site yet, so that needed a new page, worded
  for legal protection with an explicit pointer to the device manufacturer's
  own guidance.
- Added `.ac-fs-legal` — a new small link row in `alarm-widget.mjs`'s
  `PANEL_HTML`, styled and gated (`assets/css/style.css`) to show only in
  `body.ac-fs` (full-screen mode) and, unlike `.ac-controls`, *not* wired to
  the idle-fade timer — it stays put after the buttons fade to nothing,
  which is the actual "just the clock" state the request describes. Excluded
  from the Picture-in-Picture clone element for the same reason `.ac-brand`
  already is (avoids a stray duplicate in the leftover placeholder left
  behind on the main page when "Show on top" pops the real panel into its
  own window).
- New page `/alarm-clock/warnings/` (generated in `build-alarm.mjs`,
  alongside the existing `/alarm-clock/about/`): screen burn-in / image
  retention risk from a static bright display over long static stretches
  (mentions the site's existing anti-burn-in pixel-nudge mitigation but is
  explicit that it reduces, not eliminates, the risk), heat/charging
  guidance, and a section telling the reader to check their own device
  manufacturer's guidance on charging, brightness and overnight use — the
  site can't know the engineering limits of every device, so manufacturer
  guidance is framed as taking precedence. Closes with a disclaimer
  paragraph and a link to Terms.
- Extended `terms.html` Section 6 (No warranty & limitation of liability)
  with an explicit device-damage disclaimer (burn-in, overheating, battery
  wear from extended/overnight full-screen use) — the actual liability
  protection lives in the binding Terms; the new warnings page is the
  practical, plain-language version that points back to it. Added an
  `id="no-warranty"` anchor so the warnings page can deep-link to the exact
  clause.
- Cross-linked: `/alarm-clock/about/` and `/browser-limitations/` each got
  one short added sentence pointing to `/alarm-clock/warnings/` (browser
  timing vs. screen hardware are different concerns, worth distinguishing).
  Per the user's instruction, the product page itself (the full-screen
  clock) stays limited to just the two short links — no inline warning text
  was added there.
- Registered the new page in `build-inline.mjs` and `build-sitemap.mjs`.
  Verified: `npm run build` clean, the site-wide structural audit
  (unique title/description, canonical, breadcrumb, structured data — see
  the July 14 Phase 2 entry above) still comes back with only the same 5
  pre-existing, deliberate exemptions, `npm run check` shows no new broken
  links. Confirmed via Playwright against `wrangler pages dev`: clicking
  Full screen sets `body.ac-fs` and shows both links with the exact anchor
  text and hrefs requested; fast-forwarding past the idle-hide timeout
  confirms `.ac-controls` fades (`ac-idle` class applied) while
  `.ac-fs-legal` stays visible; a screenshot of the faded state shows the
  small "Browser Limitations · Warnings" text at the very bottom, legible
  and unobtrusive.

## 2026-07-14 — Fact-checked a third-party "audit" report, fixed the 2 real findings
- User pasted a technical audit report (produced by Gemini) claiming a long
  list of problems: no CMP/cookie consent, UGC copyright liability, no
  self-referencing canonicals, no JSON-LD, no semantic dates, no FAQ/
  conversational content, no DMCA policy, missing form labels, "0 days"/404
  on expired events, and JS-delayed LCP/high CLS from the countdown timer.
  The report itself said it couldn't run a live audit and was reasoning from
  generic patterns for "dynamic countdown websites" — so before acting on
  any of it, checked each specific claim against the actual source.
- Nearly all of it turned out to be false or already handled, verified with
  direct evidence rather than assumption: custom/UGC countdowns were retired
  earlier in this project (no upload path exists); canonicals are correct
  site-wide (confirmed by the exhaustive audit two sessions ago); every
  event/tide/sun/timer page ships `Event`/`FAQPage`/`BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD;
  event pages already carry `"@type":"Event","startDate":"..."` (a stronger
  AEO signal than a bare `<time>` tag); every event page's FAQ schema already
  answers "How many days until X?" in full sentences; the suggest-event
  form's three real inputs are all properly `<label for>`'d (only the
  honeypot isn't, by design); a passed one-off event shows "🎉 It's today!"
  or "This event has passed." — never a dead "0 days" or a 404; Terms §10 is
  a complete, DMCA-compliant notice-and-takedown clause; and a birthday
  page's LCP element is almost certainly the profile photo, not the JS-timed
  countdown text.
- Two claims held up on inspection and got fixed:
  1. **Countdown digits on individual event pages started as four "--"
     placeholders**, filled in by client JS — unlike World Clock/420/home,
     which already got build-time snapshots earlier in this project.
     Extended the same pattern to `build-events.mjs`: `dateISO` (already the
     resolved next-occurrence date for all three date-resolution modes —
     `once`, `dates` table, and month/day or nth-weekday recurrence) feeds a
     single `epochFor(dateISO, e.time||"08:00", tz)` call to compute the
     exact target instant at build time, then the same d/h/m/s math the
     client's own `tick()` uses. Verified all three modes bake real numbers:
     a `once` event (World Cup), a `dates`-table movable feast (Easter), and
     a recurring one (Adele's birthday) — confirmed with JavaScript disabled
     entirely that the raw HTML already shows the correct countdown.
  2. **No accessible alternative to the digit clock for screen readers** —
     a literal per-second `aria-live` region would announce every tick,
     unusably noisy. Instead added `aria-hidden="true"` to the purely
     decorative `.clock` digit grid and a `.visually-hidden` static sentence
     right beside it reusing the FAQ's own existing "X is on `<weekday>`,
     `<date>`, which is N days away" copy — correct, present once, not
     re-announced every second.
- Declined to act on the cookie-consent/CMP gap (the one claim that's both
  real and not already covered) without the user's explicit go-ahead — it's
  a product/legal/vendor decision (consent flow, cost, whether it's even
  required for this site's traffic), not a drop-in code fix.
- Verified: `npm run build` clean, the structural audit script still shows
  only the same 5 pre-existing deliberate exemptions, `npm run check`
  unchanged. Confirmed via Playwright with JavaScript fully disabled that
  `birthday-countdowns/adele/`'s digits render real numbers server-side (no
  "--" flash), that enabling JS picks up seamlessly from the same baked
  value with zero console errors, and a screenshot shows the finished
  countdown card rendering correctly on first paint.
Files: seo/tools/build-events.mjs.

## 2026-07-14 — Browser Limitations: explain the alarm's actual Wake Lock use
- User asked the "Wake Lock API" section on `/browser-limitations/` to
  explain how alarm-clock.org's own full-screen alarm actually uses Wake
  Lock (not just what the API generally is), and to link to the
  already-existing `/alarm-clock/warnings/` page about leaving the screen
  on.
- Read the real implementation (`alarm-widget.mjs`'s `wantWake`/`reqWake`/
  `syncWake`) rather than describing it from memory: full-screen mode
  requests a screen wake lock the instant it starts and releases it the
  instant you exit; if the lock isn't supported, the tab loses focus, or
  the OS overrides it, the page re-requests it automatically once the tab
  is visible/active again — but the screen can go dark in the gap, so it's
  best-effort, not a guarantee. Added a paragraph describing exactly that,
  linking to full-screen bedside mode and to `/alarm-clock/warnings/` for
  the burn-in/overnight-use follow-up.
- Verified: `npm run build` clean, the structural audit script and
  `npm run check` unchanged from baseline.
Files: browser-limitations/index.html.

## 2026-07-14 — Browser Limitations: document our own CPU/battery mitigations
- User asked whether to also explain that we minimize CPU usage by ticking
  once a minute rather than once a second. Checked the claim against
  `alarm-widget.mjs` before writing it (same discipline as the Gemini
  fact-check earlier today) rather than assuming it was accurate: confirmed
  both the alarm widget's `scheduleTick()` and the home-page mini clock's
  `sched()` compute a delay to the next minute boundary and tick there —
  genuinely once a minute, not once a second — and that the OLED
  anti-burn-in position nudge only fires once an hour (`burnHour` guard in
  `burnShift`).
- Added a new "What we do to minimize CPU and battery use" card on
  `/browser-limitations/`, right after "Battery optimization," listing the
  three real, verified mitigations (alarm tick/check once a minute, World
  Clock's once-a-minute + visibility-paused update, hourly-only burn-in
  nudge) and explicitly framing it as the one thing under our control —
  careful not to imply it changes any of the reliability limits described
  elsewhere on the same page.
- Verified: `npm run build` clean, structural audit and `npm run check`
  unchanged from baseline.
Files: browser-limitations/index.html.

## 2026-07-14 — Fill remaining structured-data gaps across the flagship tool pages
- User asked to add structured data to every page that would benefit. The
  earlier exhaustive audit (Phase 2) only checked for the *presence* of any
  JSON-LD, so it missed a real, consistent pattern: the site's flagship tool
  pages (Alarm Clock, Timer, Stopwatch, World Clock, Tides hub, Sun hub,
  Work hub + its 3 sub-pages, both 420 pages) all had `WebApplication` +
  `FAQPage` but no `BreadcrumbList` — every *other* page type on the site
  had already been standardized on carrying one. Re-audited by comparing
  `@type` sets per page template with a Python script (not just presence).
- Added `BreadcrumbList` to: `/timer/`, `/stopwatch/` (hand-authored, patched
  directly), `/alarm-clock/`, all "set alarm for HH:MM" pages, `/world-clock/`,
  `/tides/`, `/sun/`, `/work/` + `/work/friday|holiday|long-weekend/` (a
  shared `head()` helper now derives the crumb from an optional label param
  so the hub and its 3 sub-pages share one code path), and both
  `/420-countdown/` and `/420-day-countdown/`.
- Found a second real gap: the 7 timer use-case pages (egg/tea/study/
  meditation/plank/brush-teeth/workout-timer) had only `BreadcrumbList` —
  missing the `WebApplication` schema the 62 duration-preset pages already
  get, despite being equally real, standalone tools. Added it (reusing the
  same `appLd()` helper, `t.label`/`t.desc` already populated for both page
  kinds). Deliberately did *not* add `FAQPage` to these — they have no
  visible FAQ accordion (`faq` is `null` for non-duration pages), and
  schema must match what's actually on the page.
- Added `HowTo` schema to `/how-it-works/` — a good, unusually clean match:
  the page has three literal numbered-step procedures (Countdown, Timer,
  Stopwatch tabs), one `HowTo` block per tab with `HowToStep` text copied
  directly from the visible card text, not paraphrased.
- Left alone, on purpose: tide/sun state hub and country pages (directory
  listings, not tools — `BreadcrumbList` is the correct schema, forcing
  `WebApplication` onto a link list would misdescribe the page);
  `/sun/anywhere/` (noindex — structured data on a page excluded from the
  index has no effect); pure-form pages (`/report/`, `/wrong-date/`,
  `/suggest-event/`, `/promote-event/` — already have breadcrumb, no schema
  type in schema.org's vocabulary cleanly fits a plain contact form).
- Verified: `npm run build` clean, zero warnings. Wrote a Python script that
  actually `json.loads()`s (not just regex-matches) the JSON-LD on 21
  representative pages across every fixed template — all valid, all with
  the expected `@type` set. The structural audit script and `npm run check`
  came back unchanged from baseline (same 5 pre-existing deliberate
  exemptions, same pre-existing sandbox-blocked link findings).

## 2026-07-14 — Second site audit: verified claims, fixed the real ones
- User pasted a second "Claude audit" report and asked me to verify the
  rest of it against the codebase (having already correctly guessed the
  "two navigation menus" P0 finding was a stale-crawl/caching artifact).
  Checked every claim directly against source rather than trusting the
  report. Most were false positives from the auditor's markdown-extraction
  method (which strips `<script type="application/ld+json">` and can
  mis-render bold/heading markup):
  - Two nav menus: false — `build-inline.mjs` has exactly one `MENU`
    array, injected into every page via `injectNav()`.
  - "Create Custom Countdown" nav item / Terms contradiction: false — no
    such nav item or hub copy exists; `c.html`/`widget/index.html` already
    state the feature is discontinued, consistent with Terms/Privacy.
  - Missing FAQPage/BreadcrumbList/Event JSON-LD: false — all present
    (confirmed on `/alarm-clock/` and a birthday leaf page); the audit's
    own extraction method couldn't see `<script>` tags.
  - Footer brand casing inconsistent: false — every footer sampled reads
    "alarm-clock.org" (no capitalized variant, no "is new" line found
    anywhere).
  - `/terms#no-warranty` and `/terms#tides-data` anchors: confirmed to
    exist and resolve.
  - sitemap.xml/robots.txt completeness: confirmed — 1571 URLs, includes
    sun/tide leaf pages, orphaned category hubs, and `/about/`.
  - `/sun/` heading markup glitch: false — clean single `<h2>`.
  Confirmed-true and fixed (low-risk, additive, no content removed):
  - Home page's "no results" search fallback said "use Create in the menu
    to make your own" — a dead reference to the retired custom-countdown
    nav item (the likely actual source of the auditor's "Create" claim).
    Reworded to point at Popular Countdowns instead.
  - Category hubs (political/shopping/entertainment/party/astronomy/
    anniversary/graduation-countdowns) and `/about/` were reachable only
    from `/countdown/` — not the nav or any footer. Added a sitewide
    footer link row (`injectSiteLinks()`) rather than growing the already
    14-item nav further.
  - No blanket trademark/non-affiliation line existed despite the site
    referencing many trademarked event names descriptively. Added one to
    the same new footer block.
  - 420 page's "Famous friends of 4:20" section had no sourcing note for
    the cannabis-association claims. Added "Based on public statements and
    their own brands — not a claim about anyone not listed here."
  Confirmed-true but NOT changed — flagged back to the user instead, since
  each is either a bigger architectural/legal call or would touch live
  indexed URLs:
  - Sun leaf pages, tide station pages, the homepage sun-preview cards, and
    the `/work/` micro-countdown widgets all render `--`/`—` placeholders
    that fill in client-side. This isn't the same bug the countdown-event
    pages had earlier this session (those had one fixed future date,
    computable at build time via `epochFor`); these all depend on "today"
    or "the next occurrence from now," which only stays correct if rebuilt
    daily — the site has no scheduled rebuild. Baking a value at build time
    would go stale after one day. CLAUDE.md already documents the sun page
    behavior as deliberately "never stale" for this reason.
  - No cookie-consent/CCPA mechanism (raised earlier this session too,
    still pending an explicit decision).
  - Terms hardening (governing-law/venue clause, liability cap, arbitration
    clause) is a legal-drafting decision, not a copy fix.
  - `/terms` and `/privacy` are the only two routes without a trailing
    slash — true, but they're deliberately root HTML files per CLAUDE.md;
    restructuring to `/terms/` would move a long-indexed URL.
  - `/popular/`'s ~185-link wall has no grouping — real, but a content/IA
    redesign, not a quick fix.
  - `/calendar/`'s category filter is client-side chips, not per-category
    URLs — lower priority than the audit suggested, since every event is
    already present in the server-rendered HTML (filtering only
    shows/hides via CSS/JS, so nothing is hidden from crawlers).
- Verified: `npm run build` clean (1578 files). `npm run check` and the
  structural audit script both unchanged from baseline (the 715 "blocked"
  link findings are pre-existing 403s from anti-bot blocking on external
  sites like Wikipedia/Instagram in this sandboxed environment, unrelated
  to this change — no birthday-page links were touched). Verified live via
  Playwright on `/`, `/alarm-clock/`, and `/420-countdown/`: the new footer
  sitelinks/trademark rows render correctly, zero console errors.

seo/tools/build-420.mjs, assets/css/style.css.

User asked for three things, explicitly framed around visitor value over
monetization ("if traffic comes, money will come along with it"): (1) remove
the 420 page's "Famous friends of 4:20" section entirely — no labeling real
people; (2) on the Taylor Swift page, replace the album/product shop section
with a song list, each song linking to Spotify/Apple Music/YouTube Music like
the 420 page's soundtrack card, song name linking first to a genuine page on
her own site if one exists; (3) apply the same treatment to every other
musician's birthday page.

  deleted the now-orphaned `seo/_data/celebs-420.json` (nothing else

## 2026-07-15 — Artist Music Module scaffolding (data to follow)
User sent an approved, detailed spec for a richer "Artist Music Module" to
replace last turn's simpler song-list: a press-play strip (official
playlists), a "latest release" callout, a curated ~8-12 song list where
every service link is a **direct track link** (not search) with the song
name linking official-first/Genius-fallback, a discography link, and
region-aware Apple Music links — commerce-free by design. Spec says "ship
Taylor Swift first as the reference implementation, review, then roll out."
This entry is the plumbing only — data isn't wired in yet (still mid-research
on verified direct Apple Music/YouTube Music links for Taylor's song set
when the session stopped here; a background research agent was still
running). Committing now rather than leaving it uncommitted, since none of
this is live: `e.music` is never set on any event yet, so build-events.mjs's
new branch never fires and every generated page is byte-identical except for
the CSS delta below (confirmed via diff on taylor-swift/index.html).

  music)` — press-play strip, latest-release callout, songs list (direct
  Spotify links always; Apple/YouTube use the verified direct link when
  present, else a flagged search fallback — never silently guessed),
  discography link, optional tour line. Returns `{ html, warnings }`; a
  build-time `console.warn` fires for every fallback link so none ship
  unnoticed. `APPLE_REGION_JS` rewrites `.amm-apple` links to the visitor's
  own Apple Music storefront client-side (build always emits `/us/`, since
  that's the only storefront verifiable at build time).
- `build-events.mjs`: imports it; the `songs` card now prefers `e.music`
  (this module) over the older `e.songs` shape (last turn's simpler
  song-list, still what's live for Taylor + the other 17 musicians) so
  rollout can happen artist-by-artist. Wired `APPLE_REGION_JS` into the
  per-page closing script, gated on `e.music` so non-musician pages don't
  carry the dead code.
- CSS: `.song-links a` now has an explicit 44×44px tap target (was just the
  bare icon) — an accessibility requirement from the spec that also
  benefits the 18 already-shipped musician pages using the older shape.
  Added `.amm-*` rules for the new press-play strip / latest-release /
  discography-link / tour-line elements.
- Verified: `npm run build` clean (1578 files — expected, CSS is inlined
  into every page). Diffed a sample page to confirm nothing changed besides
  the CSS delta and the routine per-rebuild countdown-digit snapshot.
- Next: get the pending research agent's verified Apple Music/YouTube Music
  direct links (or fall back to search-links + build warnings for any it
  can't confirm), write Taylor Swift's `music` object in events.json, ship,
  review, then extend to the other 18 musicians.

## 2026-07-15 — Cross-linking + business self-promotion across celebrity pages
User request (not from TODO.md — a new ask): link celebrities to each
other's pages when one is mentioned on another's, and link to the
profitable companies/brands a celebrity runs (not just their nonprofits,
which were already covered), using existing text so pages don't grow much
longer. Explicit examples given: Gordon Ramsay mentioned on Nick
DiGiovanni's page should link to his page; a mention of "Joyride" should
link the word itself; add Joyride to Ryan Trahan's timeline.
Found the site already had exactly the right mechanism for this —
`linkify()` in build-events.mjs auto-links any phrase from `e.links` or a
new `e.linkify` array wherever it appears in prose, and a `related` field
renders a compact "Frequently works with" card of internal cross-links —
both already used for the creator cluster (Nick DiGiovanni/Gordon
Ramsay/MrBeast/Ryan Trahan/Mark Rober) and the presidents/Travis Kelce.
Two real gaps found and fixed:
1. `fromPeople()` in lib.mjs whitelists which people.json fields become
   page data, and `linkify` wasn't in the list (records had the same gap,
   fixed last session) — added `linkify: p.linkify`.
2. `facts` and `timeline` entries rendered with plain `esc()`, never
   `linkify()` — so a business name sitting in a fact bullet (Nick
   DiGiovanni's "Founded... Osmo", Ryan Trahan's Joyride timeline entry)
   could never become a link no matter what phrase data existed. Switched
   both to `linkify()` (same escaping, now also phrase-matches) — this is
   a shared renderer, so the fix applies site-wide, not just to the people
   touched this session.
Extracted every for-profit business mention already in the 63 profiles'
prose via a keyword scan (founded/owns/brand/etc.), cross-checked which
already had a matching official URL in `extraLinks` (most did — Ryan
Trahan/Joyride, Gordon Ramsay's restaurant group, Mark Rober/CrunchLabs,
MrBeast/Feastables, Rihanna/Fenty, Kim Kardashian/SKIMS, Selena
Gomez/Rare Beauty, Keanu Reeves/ARCH Motorcycle already auto-linked with
zero changes once facts/timeline were wired), then added `linkify` entries
for the ones whose existing link label didn't match the prose phrase
(Ryan Trahan: "Joyride", Gordon Ramsay: "Restaurant Gordon Ramsay" —
his specific flagship page, distinct from the existing "Gordon Ramsay
Restaurants" locations-hub chip which stays for browsing every location —
Nick DiGiovanni: "Osmo", Drake: "OVO", Ryan Reynolds: "Aviation Gin" +
"Mint Mobile") or had no link at all yet (Snoop Dogg: "Death Row Records",
Oprah Winfrey: "OWN network" — both official URLs found and verified via
WebSearch). Also scanned all 63 profiles' prose for one celebrity naming
another and added the missing `related` pairs found (reciprocal both
ways): Ed Sheeran/Beyoncé, Justin Bieber/Lady Gaga, Lady Gaga/Bruno Mars,
Stephen Curry/LeBron James, Dwayne Johnson/Oprah Winfrey. Confirmed Ryan
Trahan's existing 2024 Joyride timeline entry ("Becomes co-owner and
Chief Creative Officer of candy brand Joyride") is already dated
correctly via WebSearch (Feb 2024 PRNewswire announcement) — no date
correction needed, just made "Joyride" a link. Verified: `npm run build`
clean (1563 files); grepped the whole built site for nested `<a><a>` tags
(zero, confirming the facts/timeline linkify switch didn't corrupt any
existing markup anywhere); Playwright across every touched page plus a
diverse untouched sample (sports, holidays, Taylor Swift, King Charles,
Dolly Parton) — zero console errors; date/volatile checkers show the same
4 pre-existing SpaceX findings and 0/46 volatile events.

## 2026-07-15 — GDPR/CCPA privacy notice for GA4 data disclosure
User wants a JS notice disclosing GA4 data collection, shown only to
visitors from regions that require it (EU/EEA/UK/Switzerland, California).
Discovered along the way that GA4 currently runs through Cloudflare Zaraz
(edge-side, configured in the Cloudflare dashboard — not in this repo; see
lib.mjs's GA_SNIPPET comment), so a page-level script can't literally block
the edge-side GA4 call unless Zaraz's own Consent Management is enabled
there with a matching purpose key — that's outside this codebase's
visibility, so the accept/reject/opt-out handlers call
`zaraz.consent.set()` defensively (no-op if absent/unconfigured, same
pattern as the existing `zaraz.track()` calls) rather than assuming it's
wired up.
Implementation: extended build-inline.mjs (the one step that touches every
generated page) with `injectPrivacyNotice()` — an idempotent
`<div class="privacy-notice">` + `<script data-ac="privacy">` inserted
before `</body>` on every page. The script checks localStorage first (skips
entirely for returning visitors who've already chosen), then asks the
existing `/api/geo` endpoint (already reads Cloudflare's edge `request.cf`,
no extra external lookup) for country/region. EEA(EU27+IS/LI/NO)+UK+CH get
a GDPR-style opt-in gate (Accept/Reject, since EU/UK law requires consent
before non-essential analytics); California gets a CCPA/CPRA-style opt-out
notice ("Do Not Sell or Share..."), matching that regime's opt-out-by-default
model. Everyone else never sees it and never even fetches /api/geo once
consent is stored. New `.privacy-notice` CSS (fixed bottom bar, existing
--bg/--text/--accent variables, .btn classes reused).
Verified with Playwright (global install, executablePath
/opt/pw-browsers/chromium) against 4 scenarios: DE visitor sees GDPR
banner and Accept stores "granted"; US/CA visitor sees CCPA banner and
opt-out stores "denied"; a "CA"-country (Canada, not US-California region)
visitor sees nothing, confirming country vs. region-code aren't confused;
a returning visitor with consent already in localStorage never re-fetches
geo or shows the banner. `npm run build`/`npm run check` clean (same
pre-existing 403 link-check noise as prior sessions, nothing new).
Open question for the user: confirm whether Zaraz Consent Management is
enabled and, if so, its GA4 purpose key, so the zaraz.consent.set() calls
here actually gate the edge-side send rather than being a no-op.
Files: seo/tools/build-inline.mjs, assets/css/style.css.

## 2026-07-15 — Bake /popular/ + /trending/ rankings at build time (fix KV scaling)
User hit a "50% of daily Workers KV operation limit" warning; traced it to
/api/popular doing a full VIEWS-namespace list() plus one .get() per key
(hundreds of reads) and /api/trending doing a similar list-scan — both fired
by a client-side script on EVERY visitor to /popular/, /trending/, or any
/<hub>/popular/ page, so KV read cost scaled directly with traffic to those
pages. User asked to eliminate that "scale" and instead refresh the numbers
via a nightly cron. Discovered .github/workflows/maintenance.yml already runs
daily (06:17 UTC) and triggers a Cloudflare Pages rebuild via CF_DEPLOY_HOOK
— no new automation needed, just moving the fetch from "every visitor,
client-side" to "once, at build time."
Rewrote build-popular.mjs and build-trending.mjs to fetch their ranking
(https://alarm-clock.org/api/popular|trending) ONCE during the build, apply
it to the row order server-side, and bake the result directly into the
static HTML (rank number, view count text, .ranked class) — removed the
client-side REORDER <script> entirely. On fetch failure (no network, or a
non-2xx status) it logs a warning and falls back to the original
soonest-upcoming-first order with no counts, so the build never hard-fails
offline — confirmed in this sandbox, whose egress proxy blocks
alarm-clock.org (403), unlike Cloudflare Pages' own build servers. Verified
the ranked path separately by mocking global.fetch with fake ranking data:
confirmed correct reorder-to-top in rank order, correct singular/plural view
text ("1 view" vs "4,200 views"), and correct sub-heading copy switch
("Ranked by total views — refreshed nightly." vs the upcoming-date fallback
copy). Updated maintenance.yml's header comment to document why the
existing daily rebuild now also matters for these two pages.
Net effect: /api/popular and /api/trending now get hit once a day (by the
build) instead of once per visitor — the KV read-amplification no longer
scales with traffic. Trade-off: view counts on these two pages now read "as
of last night" rather than updating live through the day.
Verified: npm run build clean (both generators log their fallback warning,
don't crash), npm run check shows the same pre-existing 403 link-check noise
and no new findings, grep confirms zero leftover client-side
fetch('/api/popular'|'/api/trending') calls in any generated page.
Files: seo/tools/build-popular.mjs, seo/tools/build-trending.mjs,
.github/workflows/maintenance.yml.

## 2026-07-18 — Structured-data governance: WebPage LD + honest fact-checked label
Second audit item (§9.3/§7.3): every countdown page emitted Event JSON-LD
declaring itself an online virtual event at its own URL — misrepresentative
per Google's guidelines (Event markup is for attendable events; birthdays
and abstract dates don't qualify at all). Replaced with WebPage JSON-LD in
build-events.mjs: `about: Person` (+ birthDate when born/month/day known) on
birthday pages, `about: Thing` otherwise, publisher Organization. FAQPage and
BreadcrumbList unchanged — they mirror visible content. Also fixed the
footer "Fact-checked & updated <build date>" that appeared on every page
regardless of review: "Fact-checked <date>" now renders only for events
with a logged `verified` date (46 in events.json), showing that actual date;
all others say just "Updated <month year>". People.json pages have no
verified field yet, so they show Updated-only — adding per-person review
dates is a future content session.
Verified: build clean, zero '"@type":"Event"' matches in output, WebPage/
FAQPage/BreadcrumbList all JSON.parse cleanly on Taylor Swift's page,
Olympics shows "Fact-checked July 12, 2026", Adele shows Updated-only,
Christmas gets about:Thing. npm run check unchanged (pre-existing 403s).
Files: seo/tools/build-events.mjs.

between text and buttons. Root cause: the mobile media query switched the

## 2026-07-20 — Tide station pages: unique per-station tidal content (dedup fix)

station pages shipped the IDENTICAL 7-question FAQ and no baked tide data
(tide times are 100% client-side), so to Google they read as near-duplicate
templated pages — a real ranking liability. Added a tidalCharacter(station)
classifier: regional tidal PATTERN (semidiurnal Atlantic / mixed Pacific &
Alaska / diurnal Gulf) + typical range magnitude, keyed by state, framed as
"typically/usually" and always pointing to the live chart for exact numbers
(honest — these are well-established regional generalizations, not per-station
measured claims). Florida classified by hand per-slug (16 stations across 3
regimes — panhandle diurnal, Gulf peninsula + Keys mixed, Atlantic
semidiurnal) after a coordinate-line first attempt mislabeled SW Florida
(Naples/Fort Myers are Gulf but east of the panhandle). Each station page now
gets: a unique "What the tides are like at <city>" card, a varied intro line
naming the pattern, and 2 station-specific FAQ items prepended so the FAQ
block + its FAQPage JSON-LD differ per page. NOAA build-time data fetch (real
baked high/low table, #5) deferred: NOAA API is egress-blocked in this
sandbox so it can't be verified here — it'd run on Cloudflare's build only,
same fallback pattern as popular/trending; flagged for a follow-up.
Verified: build clean, npm run check unchanged (pre-existing 403 noise only),
Boston=semidiurnal / Galveston=diurnal / Anchorage=mixed (26-30ft) /
Naples=mixed / Pensacola=diurnal / Miami=semidiurnal all correct, FAQPage
JSON-LD (9 Qs) matches visible content, two stations confirmed to differ.

page list and the sitemap since both import it). Wrote genuinely distinct
