## 2026-07-20 — Feature 3 of 3: baked "today's tides" table (NOAA at build time)
The biggest single tides win, and the last of the three. Station pages' tide
times were 100% client-side, so the HTML a crawler/AI engine indexes for
"<city> tide today" had no actual times. Added a build-time NOAA fetch
(build-tides.mjs): for each of the 101 stations, fetch its high/low
predictions for ITS OWN local today (Intl in the station's IANA tz +
NOAA time_zone=lst_ldt, which returns station-local wall time), 8-way
concurrency, per-station try/catch. Bakes a "<city> tide times for <weekday,
date>" table (High/Low · time · height ft above MLLW) right after the live
tool, so the actual answer is in the static HTML. Daily maintenance rebuild
refreshes it; tide predictions are fixed astronomy so the baked table is
accurate all day. Same egress reality as OG/popular/trending: NOAA is blocked
in this sandbox, so all fetches fail here and the table is simply omitted
(0/101, live chart still works, build clean) — it populates on Cloudflare's
real build (verified the baking path by mocking global.fetch: 101/101 baked,
"Boston tide times for Monday, July 20 / High tide 4:18 AM 9.7 ft" formats
correctly). Confirmed graceful path leaves no empty table/heading; the 5
"tide-today" hits on a page are the inlined CSS only.
Files: seo/tools/build-tides.mjs, assets/css/style.css.

All three requested features shipped (illustrations, OG cards, baked tide

## 2026-07-21 — Nightly rebuild timing fix (tide "today" for western US)
Decision from the "tide edge-injection" question: SKIP per-request edge
injection (recreates the KV/subrequest traffic-scaling we just escaped; tide
predictions are fixed astronomy so the baked table is accurate all day and
human visitors already get live client-side data). Instead make the existing
nightly rebuild solid. The daily maintenance.yml already triggers a Cloudflare
Pages rebuild (which re-runs npm run build with real internet, refreshing the
baked today's-tides tables + popular/trending). Its one real flaw: it ran at
06:17 UTC, before "today" had started in the western US, so every Pacific/
Alaska/Hawaii station baked YESTERDAY's tide table and stayed a day behind for
crawlers. Moved the cron to 11:20 UTC — past midnight in Hawaii (UTC-10) so
every mainland+AK+HI station bakes the correct local date; no downside for the
date/link audit. Dashboard dependencies to confirm (can't verify from repo):
the Pages project's build command must be `npm run build`, and the
CF_DEPLOY_HOOK repo secret must be set, or the rebuild step is skipped. File:
.github/workflows/maintenance.yml.

## 2026-07-21 — KV write reduction (view counter) + paid-plan guidance
Question: how to get more KV scaling. Answer given: KV limits are governed by
the account's Workers plan; upgrading to Workers Paid ($5/mo) raises writes
1k/day→1M/mo and reads 100k/day→10M/mo (link + steps given to user). Code side:
removed the redundant per-IP stats-dedupe key ("sip:") from functions/api/
views.js. It gated the stats/trending buckets to one visit per IP per page per
day — but that condition is exactly "ipc === 0" (the per-IP daily tally's
pre-increment value), so the stats path now gates on `if (ipc > 0) return`
instead, eliminating one KV read AND one KV write on every new counted visit
(~1 of ~6 writes on the hot path) with identical behaviour. Verified: no other
function reads the sip: prefix (write-only dedupe), node --check passes, stats
buckets (vh/vd) + public counter (v:) + per-visitor/-IP dedupe all intact.
Durable fix is the plan upgrade; the real scale end-state (migrate view/pageview
counting to Cloudflare Analytics Engine, which isn't bound by KV write limits)
is teed up as a post-upgrade task to do once the paid plan is active so it can
be verified on real infra. File: functions/api/views.js.

## 2026-07-21 — Decision: defer Analytics Engine (KV scaling resolved by paid plan)
User upgraded to Workers Paid, which raised KV limits ~33× writes / ~100× reads
— the scaling problem that started this thread is solved. Weighed the full
Analytics Engine migration (unlimited telemetry writes, SQL analytics) and,
given current traffic has a long runway on paid KV (especially after the sip:
write reduction), user chose to DEFER AE entirely rather than add a second data
store + API token + dual read-paths now. Signal to revisit: KV writes
approaching ~500k–700k/day (Workers&Pages → VIEWS KV → Metrics), ~150k+ new
visits/day. No code change this step. If revisited: counter KV logic stays;
only vh:/vd: stats buckets move to AE with a KV fallback. No files changed.

labels, and yellow-high / grey-low dot markers with bold-white "ft" readouts —
colours copied from tides.js drawChart. Plots a realistic mixed-semidiurnal
curve (uneven twin highs) from a fixed harmonic sum; deterministic, no network,
always renders. (2) Split the single "Tides for nearest beach" button into two
side-by-side buttons: Nearest (primary → /tides/near-me/) and Search (secondary
→ /tides/). CSS: .tc-foot-2 flex row, snapshot svg gets border-radius. The old
tideCurve() schematic stays in use on station pages (labeled as a pattern
schematic) — unchanged. Verified via screenshot: card renders as a real-looking
tide chart with both buttons. Files: seo/tools/tide-curve.mjs,
seo/tools/build-home.mjs, assets/css/style.css.

## 2026-07-21 — Rework /tides/near-me/ into a nearest-list router + add 14 beaches
Per user, the near-me page was messy (two boxes + an inline chart that made
opening the real pages pointless). Rebuilt it as a clean ROUTER: on load it
auto-geolocates and lists the tide stations AND beaches closest to you, sorted
by distance, each linking to its own page — no chart. If location is denied it
shows a "Use my location" retry + the popular list + state directory. Key fix:
the nearest list now reads a COMBINED index (window.TIDE_NEAR = stations +
destinations), so beach towns people actually search for surface — e.g. from
Lincoln City, OR the list leads with Lincoln City (0 mi), Depoe Bay, Pacific
City, Newport… (Lincoln City already had a page; it just wasn't discoverable
because near-me only used stations.) Removed the chart wiring: chartCardHtml use
on near-me, tides.js load there, chartOnly/tdLoadStation in tides.js, the
build-inline bundle registration, and the dead .nm-lead CSS. Added 14 popular
beach destinations (cocoa-beach/daytona-beach FL, gulf-shores AL, tybee-island
GA, hilton-head SC, nags-head NC, ocean-city/wildwood NJ, rehoboth-beach DE,
laguna-beach/oceanside/carmel/pismo-beach/morro-bay CA), each sourced to its
computed nearest NOAA station with honest distance disclosure. Made the
destination generator robust to single-station states (DE has no hub): breadcrumb
/state link/"all tides" chip fall back to /tides/ instead of 404. Added Atlantic
FL_TIDE classifications for the two new FL beaches. Verified headless: auto-list
near Lincoln City + Cocoa Beach, denied-location fallback, station/hub/dest boot
clean. Files: seo/tools/build-tides.mjs, assets/js/tides.js, seo/tools/
build-inline.mjs, assets/css/style.css, seo/_data/tide-destinations.json.

## 2026-07-26 — Golden hour, blue hour & moon phase on sun pages
Added a "Golden hour & blue hour" card to every sun page (city, /sun/my-location,
/sun/anywhere). Shows morning + evening golden-hour (sun −4°→+6°) and blue-hour
(−6°→−4°) ranges, computed via nSunCalc at angles 6/−4/−6; golden ranges use the
sun-main gold highlight. Subtle side-note below: tonight's moon — phase-accurate
SVG glyph (dark disc + lit semicircle + terminator ellipse; correct at every
phase incl. new/full/both crescents) + phase name + % illuminated. Baked at build
for crawlers, recomputed live on load (bands follow the date picker; moon tracks
real "today"). Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs (moonData/moonName/moonSvg/sunBands
+ goldMoonCard, SSR fields, PAGE_BODY_JS client twins), assets/css/style.css
(.sun-gb*, .sun-moon*). Verified headless on /sun/seattle/. Note on the "prebuild
hourly" ask: pages already recompute with new Date() on load, so times are
accurate to the moment regardless of build cadence — daily rebuild + client
recompute already achieves it; left the cron daily (hourly would 24× cost for no
accuracy gain).

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — Timer hand-off, more sun cities
Two follow-ups after the golden-hour session:
- Timer unification: preset pages (/timer/N-minutes/) now have an "Add timer"
  button. It hands the current timer off to the multi-timer board on /timer/ —
  writing a seed to localStorage (ac_timer_handoff) with the absolute end-time —
  then navigates. The board reads the seed once on load, recreates the timer
  (resuming a RUNNING one to its exact end-instant, so no hiccup/reset thanks to
  wall-clock timing) and opens the Add dialog for the next timer. Egg variant
  excluded. Files: seo/tools/build-timers.mjs. Verified over HTTP with Playwright.
- Sun cities: added 18 iconic sunrise/sunset destinations to the shared
  cities.mjs (Bali, Bar Harbor, Cabo San Lucas, Cancún, Cannon Beach, Dubrovnik,
  Ibiza, Key West, Malé, Malibu, Marrakech, Nice, Papeete, Phuket, Queenstown,
  Santorini, Sedona, Zanzibar) → /sun/ pages (1075→1093) + World Clock datalist.
- Homepage: short educational note on why day length varies with latitude/season.
  Stopwatch: options moved behind a settings gear; full-screen control parity.

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — Sunrise card note trimmed
- Shortened the Sunrise & Sunset note on the homepage. It was three clauses long
  and explained axial tilt; now one sentence that just tells the reader what the

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — State tide hubs: honest, location-led meta
- Problem: /tides/oregon/ was titled "Tide Times for 3 Coastal Stations" and its
  description ended "…king-tide dates for 3 coastal locations." The page actually
  lists every Oregon location in NOAA's tide-prediction directory (filed into the
  county sections client-side) plus 10 curated beach towns — so the station count
  undersold it badly and told a searcher nothing about finding their own beach.
- Fix (build-tides.mjs, state-hub block): title is now "<State> Tide Charts —
  High & Low Tide Times by Location"; the description is generated by stateDesc()
  and names real places spread along the state's shoreline, e.g. "Oregon tide
  times by location: today's high and low tides for Astoria, Depoe Bay, Bandon
  and every other NOAA tide location on the coast, county by county."
- anchorPlaces() picks those names: stations + curated destinations, sorted on
  whichever axis the coast actually runs (lat vs cos-scaled lng — so Gulf/panhandle
  states read west→east, e.g. FL = Pensacola, Key West, Palm Beach), taking both
  ends plus the middle. Parentheticals stripped ("Kauai (Nawiliwili)" → "Kauai").
  stateDesc() tries phrasing tiers (today's / no today's / drop "county by county"
  / two names / generic) and keeps the first ≤160 chars — all 20 state pages now
  land 145–159 chars with no truncation and no invented counts.
- On-page intro rewritten to lead with "Find the high and low tides for your spot
  on the <State> coast" and to say the list covers beaches, bays, harbors, inlets
  and river mouths, "not just the N main tracking stations" — the count now frames
  the extra coverage instead of capping it.
- No numbers were invented: NOAA's directory is fetched client-side and the API is
  unreachable from the build sandbox, so per-state location counts stay unstated.
- Files: seo/tools/build-tides.mjs; full `npm run build` (check-pages ✓ 1677).

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — Location tide pages: title + snippet rewrite
- Titles for every station and beach/town page now follow one pattern via
  tideTitle(): "<City>, <ST> Tide Chart — Today's High, Low & King Tides"
  (was "… — Tide Times & King Tides" for stations and "… — Tide Times & Low Tide
  Today" for destinations). The tail names the three things the page answers;
  "Today's" is dropped when the place name would push the title past ~62 chars
  (Wrightsville Beach, South Padre Island, Kauai…), so nothing truncates.
- tideMeta() rewritten. It used to give high-tide times only, then a generic
  "See today's low tides…" tail. It now leads with the full answer — today's
  highs AND lows plus the day's peak height — and follows with what the visitor
  gets for clicking: "Miami tides today: high 7:14 AM & 7:59 PM (1.9 ft), low
  1:08 AM & 1:33 PM. Free NOAA chart with heights, sunrise & sunset, and king
  tides up to a year ahead." Someone searching "low tide <place>" now sees the
  answer in the SERP and still has a reason to open the page.
- Candidates are tried longest-first (drop the height, then the extras, then the
  low times); the first that fits ~160 chars wins. Destination pages get their
  own tails ("Free tide chart from the nearest NOAA station…") so the snippet
  never implies a beach town has its own station.
- plainPlace() hoisted to module scope and applied to snippet copy, so the meta
  reads "Kauai tides today", not "Kauai (Nawiliwili) tides today" (titles/H1
  keep the harbour name). Verified across all 143 place pages: max decoded title
  62 chars, max description 160, none over.
- Files: seo/tools/build-tides.mjs; full `npm run build` (check-pages ✓ 1677).

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — "Daylight" arc card on sun pages + new city meta
- New card above the sundial on every /sun/ city page, /sun/anywhere/ and
  /sun/my-location/: "Daylight in <City>", a stat line (day length in accent
  gold + sunrise + sunset) and a two-row chart.
  Row 1 = the sun's real ALTITUDE curve across the local day, drawn on a true
  0–90° scale, so a June arc in Miami (84°) towers over Chicago in December
  (25°) instead of both filling the panel — shape carries information. It passes
  exactly through −0.833° at the same sunrise/sunset the page prints (same solar
  geometry), with the peak labelled "Solar noon 12:58 PM · sun 68° high".
  Row 2 = the twilight rail the ask called for: night → first light → daylight →
  last light → night, gradient-blended, with ticks and times.
- One implementation, two uses: ARC_JS is a pure string-in/string-out function;
  the build evals it (new Function) to server-render the SVG into the HTML, and
  the same source ships to the browser to redraw on any picked date and to ride
  a live sun marker along the curve (today only, repainted each minute). No Node
  twin to drift, unlike the dial.
- Handles the awkward cases: midnight sun / polar night (curve stays wholly above
  or below, with a note), a sunset after midnight (rail wraps — Anchorage shows
  last light 12:13 AM at the left edge), and colliding dawn/dusk labels (stacked
  on two lanes). Labels sit on the night side of each dot so they never cross the
  curve, flipping only at the chart edge.
- Phones: the chart scales to fit rather than scrolling; a media query swaps each
  label to a short wording (tspan pairs) and scales the SVG text back up, since a
  stylesheet rule outranks the baked font-size attribute.
- City meta description rewritten per the ask: "Today's Chicago sunrise is 5:39 AM
  and sunset is 8:16 PM — 14 hr 37 min of daylight. See first light, last light,
  solar noon, and any date you pick." Longest-first tiers keep every one of the
  1,093 pages ≤160 chars (max observed 160).
- Cost: +4.8 KB gzipped per city page. Halved the curve's share by letting one
  open path both stroke and fill (implicit close runs along the horizon).
- Verified headless (Chromium) at 900px and 390px, with and without JS, on
  Chicago/Miami/Anchorage + a Dec-21 date pick: no page errors, no layout
  overflow, server-rendered and client-drawn charts agree.
- Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs, assets/css/style.css.

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — Nome fixes: chart wrap, labels, intro, golden-hour sizes
Four fixes on the /sun/ city pages, all surfaced by Nome (clock sits far from
the sun there: sunset 12:31 AM, solar noon 3:09 PM).
- Charts no longer wrap. Both the annual chart and the new arc card assumed a
  12 AM–12 AM axis, so Nome's sunset fell off one end and reappeared at the
  other, splitting the daylight in two. The annual chart now plots each day's
  times relative to ITS OWN solar noon (unwrap ±24 h) with axis labels wrapped
  back into clock time; the arc card shifts its 24-hour window to centre on
  solar noon (rounded to the hour) whenever the day's events stop falling in
  order — Nome reads 3 AM → 3 AM with one continuous daylight band. Normal
  cities are untouched: they keep the familiar midnight-to-midnight axis.
- Arc labels get a dark outline (paint-order stroke) and the live sun is drawn
  before them, so a label landing on the marker or the gold fill stays readable.
- US city labels now carry the state: cityLabel() gained CURATED_ST, the ~29
  hand-curated US cities in cities.mjs that had no `st` (the census list already
  had one). H1s read "Sunrise & Sunset in Nome, AK", per the ask; titles and
  descriptions follow. Titles were ALREADY truncating for long census names, so
  the tail now shortens in tiers — max title across 1,093 pages is 62 chars,
  max description 160, none over. Slugs unchanged, so no URLs moved.
- Intro cut from five clauses to one: "18 hours 45 minutes of daylight today —
  7 minutes less than yesterday." It was repeating the card right below it. The
  full date moved to the timezone hint line (keeps #sun-today for the JS).
- Golden hour / blue hour values dropped 16px → 13.5px (they're ranges, not
  single times, so they dwarfed their labels) and stack under their label below
  560px, which stops the wrapping on phones.
- Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs, assets/css/style.css. Verified headless on
  Nome + Chicago at 900/390px: no page errors, no overflow, date picker still
  drives the arc.

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — One tide chart renderer for all three places
The tide chart existed twice: an SVG (tide-curve.mjs) for the homepage card and
the baked page chart, and a canvas (tides.js) for the live one. That's why a
tide page visibly swapped one chart for a different-looking one on load.
- NEW assets/js/tide-chart.js — THE renderer: tideChartSvg() (pure string in,
  string out; no DOM, no canvas, no Intl — the caller passes formatted labels)
  plus tideSeries(), which draws the curve between NOAA's extremes with a
  half-cosine. seo/tools/tide-chart.mjs evals that very file so the build and
  the browser run one implementation; build-inline ships it in the tides bundle.
- The chart element is now an <svg id="td-chart"> on a fixed 700x300 viewBox.
  build-tides bakes the innards for the same 3-day window the page opens on
  (station-local wall clock as UTC ms — one frame for tide times, day ticks and
  night bands), and the browser replaces the innards with the same drawing.
  Crawlers and no-JS visitors now get the real chart, not an empty box.
- Homepage card uses the renderer too (one day, hour ticks, bigger labels, wider
  grid step), so a preview looks like the chart it opens. Per the owner: only the
  station page gets the Days / Zoom controls; previews are just the picture.
- Each chart load is now ONE NOAA request, not two: interval=hilo only. The
  extremes are what the page reports; the curve between them is tideSeries. This
  is what makes baked and live pixel-identical — the old interval=h series drew a
  visibly different (coarser, hourly-linear) line.
- Pages ship window.TIDE_BAKED (~16 days of hi/lo from the committed weekly
  file), so the first paint needs no network at all; the request that follows
  only refreshes/extends it. Answers "is the reload needed?": for the default
  view, no — it now draws instantly from baked data.
- Also: the tooltip reads the drawn curve (chartPts) instead of a separate hourly
  array; night bands got contrast on the new panel colour; "-0.0ft" labels read
  "0.0ft".
- Verified headless with a mocked NOAA (the sandbox has no NOAA access): baked
  paint before any request, identical redraw after, tooltip, Days=5, Zoom out,
  hub with ?station=, a destination page, and no-JS. No page errors anywhere.
- Files: assets/js/tide-chart.js (new), assets/js/tides.js, seo/tools/
  tide-chart.mjs (new), build-tides.mjs, build-home.mjs, build-inline.mjs,
  assets/css/style.css. tide-curve.mjs keeps tideCurve() for the schematic
  "typical pattern" figure; tideDayChart/tideChartSnapshot are now unused.

## 2026-07-26 (cont.) — Tide chart: adaptive height grid, twice as tall
- Height grid lines were a fixed 1 ft (or 2 ft over an 8 ft range), which put
  SIXTEEN mashed-together labels on Anchorage's 32 ft swing. The step now comes
  from the range via niceStep(): the smallest value on a 0.5/1/2/5/10/20/50
  ladder that keeps the count at ~6. Anchorage reads 0/10/20/30 ft, San Diego
  0/2/4/6, Naples 0/1/2/3. Grid lines also start on a multiple of the step, and
  labels handle half-foot steps.
- The station chart is twice as tall: viewBox 700x600 (TIDE_H_PAGE) instead of
  700x300, in the baked SVG, the element and the client's VBH. On a 390px phone
  that's 271px tall instead of ~135. Homepage previews stay short (TIDE_H=300,
  lines:4) — they're previews, and they still carry no Days/Zoom controls.
- The default window is now 3 days on every device. It was 2 on phones, which
  after the baking work meant a phone loaded the baked 3-day chart and then
  redrew it as 2 — reintroducing the very swap this work removed. The taller
  chart carries 3 days fine on a phone; anyone preferring 2 picks it once and
  td_days remembers.
- Marker readouts are clamped inside the panel, so a high or low sitting at the
  window edge no longer hangs its label over the ft axis.
- Verified headless (NOAA blocked, so baked-only paint) on Anchorage, San Diego
  and Naples at 390/900px, plus the live path with mocked NOAA: tooltip, Days=5,
  Zoom out, no-JS. No page errors.

## 2026-07-29 — /sun/my-location/ couldn't detect a location: two real bugs
- BUG 1, the reported one. getCurrentPosition ran with {timeout:9000}. That
  clock starts when the call is MADE and keeps running while the permission
  prompt is on screen — so a visitor who took more than 9s to read the prompt
  and tap Allow got "We couldn't get your location" immediately after granting
  it, with no retry. Reproduced headlessly (12s grant -> failure message, tool
  never shown) before changing anything.
  Fix: size the window to what we're waiting on. navigator.permissions.query
  tells us the state — 'granted' gets 12s (no human in the loop), 'prompt' gets
  45s (a human is), 'denied' fires no request at all and says how to unblock.
  A timeout on the short window escalates to the long one once rather than
  giving up. The watchdog is now timeout+3s, so it can't undercut the request
  it's supposed to backstop (it was 11s against a 9s timeout).
  Error messages now branch on PositionError.code, so a blocked permission
  reads as blocked instead of as a generic failure.
- BUG 2, found while checking. reverseName() fetches api.bigdatacloud.net to
  turn the coordinates into a town name, but that host was never in the CSP's
  connect-src — so in production every request was blocked and the page said
  "your location" forever. The .catch() swallowed it silently. Added the host
  to _headers with a note on the comment block above it.
- Verified all six paths headless: slow grant, instant grant, granted-but-slow
  (escalates 12000 -> 45000), previously blocked (no request made), denied
  prompt, and no-Permissions-API. No page errors in any.
- Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs, _headers.

    Verified no cross-contamination (timer page has no tideChartSvg; alarm page
    has no acSegDisplay). Payloads: timer 23kb, alarm 31kb, sun 39kb, tides 81kb.
  - CSS is the monolith. style.css is 132kb; every tool page inlines 91kb of it.
    A timer page carries the alarm clock's 20kb, tides' 11kb and the home
    dashboard's 7kb for nothing — ~40kb dead per page across 69 timer pages.
    Block sizes: alarm 20, tides 11, stopwatch+timer 10, home 7, worldclock 2,
    egg 2, themes 22 (themes already stripped for everyone).
  - Complication found: style.css is INTERLEAVED, not sectioned. Around L155-200
    timer/alarm/tides/generic-card rules alternate rule by rule, so physically
    splitting into files reorders the cascade and a rule that wins today could
    start losing. Two options put to the user: (A) real per-section files with a
    computed-style diff harness as the gate, or (B) rename the existing
    TOOLCSS-START/END markers to named sections (CSS:alarm, CSS:timer, …) and
    have build-inline delete unneeded ranges — same ~40kb saving, zero cascade
    risk because it only removes, never reorders. Recommended B now, A as its
    own session. Awaiting the choice.
- Files: assets/css/style.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — Preset timer pages: control row sized to the read-out
- The four controls now span exactly the LED display's width. New .t-controls
  class (added by build-timers only on the non-egg path, so the egg layout and
  the stopwatch are untouched); buttons flex:1 1 0, sound button fixed.
- The width is calc(4.63*clamp(44px,15vw,108px)) — the SAME clamp .seg-screen
  uses for its font-size, so the two track by construction. The display is a
  fixed 8 glyphs plus em padding, making its width a constant 4.63x that font
  size. First attempt used .tool-time's clamp (17vw) and matched below 635px but
  left the row 56px short once the 108px cap kicked in; measuring the ratio at 8
  viewports found the constant and fixed it. Now within 0-1px from 360 to 1200.
- Button font/padding scale with clamp(), so desktop is proportionally larger:
  buttons go ~68px wide at 390 to ~121px at 900.
- Verified idle AND running (Edit hides, Pause/Reset appear): one row, no
  overflow, at 360/390/900. No page errors.
- Tooltips: sound "Change alarm sound", Edit "Edit Timer Duration", Add "Add
  another timer" — title + matching aria-label on each. The /timer/ hub's own
  multi-timer sound button was left alone (different control set, not asked for).

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — Timer spacing, and a performance audit of every page group
- .tool-warn bottom margin 22px -> 62px (the requested extra 40px).
- Display -> button row tightened from 44px to 19px (~1/3). Most of that 44px was
  the empty .tool-msg slot, which reserves a line so the "time's up" message
  doesn't shove the buttons down when it appears; it still reserves one, just 9px
  instead of 20px, plus .t-controls margin-top 16 -> 6. Scoped with
  .tool-msg:has(+ .t-controls) so the stopwatch and egg layouts, which share
  .tool-msg, are untouched.
- PERF AUDIT (8 page groups, static scan). Already clean everywhere: 0
  render-blocking CSS, 0 render-blocking JS, 0 third-party requests in the HTML,
  every <img> has width+height and loading=lazy, no web fonts (system stack).
  Four real opportunities left, reported to the user, none done yet:
  1. The 1200x1200 illustrations render at 160px (240 on the stopwatch) — a
     ~40kb download for a 160px slot on 69 timer pages. srcset with a 320px
     sibling would save ~35kb/page. Lazy + below the fold, so no LCP impact,
     pure bytes. The 1200px file must stay (it is the social card).
  2. No preconnect for the runtime third parties the page will certainly call:
     NOAA on tide pages, Open-Meteo on sun search, BigDataCloud on my-location.
     Saves a DNS+TLS handshake before first data render.
  3. Inlining costs repeat navigations: /timer/5-minutes/ -> /timer/10-minutes/
     re-downloads ~58kb of identical CSS. Brotli makes it ~10kb and the split
     just cut it a third; structural cost of the inline strategy.
  4. Cache-Control max-age=0 must-revalidate means a revalidation round-trip per
     navigation. Deliberate (inlined HTML must never be stale) — a 60-300s
     max-age would make quick re-visits instant. Policy call, left alone.
  Also: 11-hiw.css matches NO built page — likely dead, delete in a condense round.
- Files: assets/css/parts/02-base.css, assets/css/parts/05-tools-b.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — New /moon/ section: phase today, countdowns, calendars
Third tool section in the sun/tides family. Hub + full-moon calendar + 5 year
pages; per-city /moon/<city>/ deliberately deferred to its own session.

WHY THE MATH IS ITS OWN MODULE (seo/tools/moon.mjs). There were already TWO
moon implementations and they disagreed: build-sun.mjs had correct SunCalc
illumination geometry inline, while assets/js/tides.js had a linear
"days-since-a-reference-new-moon / 29.530588853" model — which drifts up to
±14 HOURS from the true phase, and drew the phase with moon EMOJI (against the
no-emoji-icons rule). ±14 h is harmless for printing "waxing gibbous" and fatal
for "next full moon in 8 h 24 m", which is the whole point of this section. So:
- moon.mjs holds the math ONCE, as MOON_CORE (an ES5 source string). The browser
  inlines it verbatim; Node instantiates the SAME string via `new Function`.
  There is deliberately no hand-maintained "Node twin" — that pattern is exactly
  how the two implementations above drifted apart in the first place.
- Phase instants: Meeus ch. 49, full periodic + 14 planetary corrections, TT->UTC
  via the Espenak-Meeus ΔT polynomial. Under a minute, vs ±14 h.
- Position/distance: Meeus ch. 47 truncated. The single-term distance
  (385001-20905cos M') is ±2,000 km — more than the supermoon threshold's own
  margin — so the leading ~15 periodic terms are carried.
- Moonrise/moonset: hourly altitude sampling + quadratic interpolation (SunCalc
  getMoonTimes method). Caller passes the day-start instant, so the time zone
  belongs to the caller (a city page can ask in the city's own zone).
- Both old copies now call this module. tides.js gained the shared SVG glyph and
  lost its emoji; build-sun.mjs lost ~35 lines of duplicated Node + client math.

VALIDATION (two independent algorithms cross-checking each other): illumination
computed from ch. 47 at the ch. 49 phase instants, over 20 years of phases —
full moons max 0.19% short of fully lit, new moons max 0.19% lit, quarters max
0.85% off 50%. Synodic spacing varies 29.28-29.74 d (correct; a mean model would
be flat). The 2026-07-29 full moon computes to 14:35 UTC against a 14:36 UTC
reference. Harvest Moon resolves in every year 2024-2040.

TWO NAMING BUGS THE TESTS CAUGHT, both real:
- Harvest Moon is the full moon nearest the SEPTEMBER EQUINOX, not September's
  (it lands in October ~1 year in 3 — 2025, 2028, 2036, 2039 — and then Hunter's
  Moon shifts to the next one). Computed from the mean equinox (Meeus ch. 27).
- Blue moon started as a rename, which silently DELETED the Harvest Moon from
  2031 (where it is also the second full moon of September). Now the flag and
  the name are decided separately: the month name belongs to the month's first
  full moon, the second takes "Blue Moon" — unless it already holds a special
  name, which wins and just gets the badge.

PAGES. /moon/ (phase, illumination, age, distance, 4 live countdowns, moonrise/
moonset, 30-day strip), /moon/full-moon-calendar/ (2 years, names, supermoon +
blue badges, distance), /moon/<year>/ for THIS_YEAR-1..+3. Every instant is
baked in UTC — honest for crawlers, correct anywhere — then rewritten into the
visitor's zone client-side, so a phase can never be served stale. The glyph
mirrors below the equator (lat<0); the hub picks a city by IANA zone, preferring
the city the zone is NAMED after (America/Los_Angeles gave "Las Vegas" first).

GOTCHA WORTH REMEMBERING: build-inline.mjs's HTML list is EXPLICIT, not a tree
walk. The first build "succeeded" — check-pages passed, since it walks the tree —
while the moon pages silently got no CSS, no nav, no footer. Any new page family
needs an entry there as well as in build-sitemap.

Files: seo/tools/moon.mjs (new), build-moon.mjs (new), assets/css/parts/22-moon.css
(new), assets/js/moon.js (GENERATED), build-inline.mjs (probe + HTML list + tide
bundle + nav), css-parts.mjs (parts written after the split carry a null source
range), build-sitemap.mjs, build-sun.mjs, build-tides.mjs, icons.mjs, tides.js,
21-tides.css, package.json.
OPEN: /moon/<city>/ mirroring /sun/<city>/ 1:1 (reuses the city DB, state hubs,
search index and coastal-links.json unchanged) — the agreed next session.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — The real lunar near side behind every phase glyph
The phase glyph was a flat disc + terminator ellipse; now it shows the actual
moon — maria, craters, ray systems — with the shadow sweeping across a fixed
face, which is what makes a crescent read as THE MOON.

seo/tools/moon-face.mjs (new): the near side GENERATED from real selenographic
coordinates, not hand-drawn. Each feature is placed by its true lon/lat and
sized from its true diameter, projected orthographically (x=cos(lat)sin(lon),
y=sin(lat)) and foreshortened by √(1−ρ²) along the radial direction — so
Crisium is a narrow oval hard against the eastern limb and Frigoris a thin
northern ribbon, as in a photograph. ~20 maria as multi-lobe clusters (blurred
pass to fuse + crisp pass on top, or they read as bubbles), ~40 named craters
in three kinds (dark-floored Plato/Grimaldi, bright young Tycho/Aristarchus/
Proclus, plain rims), the 5 great ray systems (rays UNDER the maria — they are
splashed highland dust, genuinely overprinted by the basalt), seeded-PRNG
highland speckle weighted to the southern highlands (byte-stable builds), and
limb darkening as a radial gradient (a stroked ring read as a border).
Mare/highland contrast follows real albedo (~0.12 vs ~0.06 ⇒ seas at half the
highland brightness); a lighter first pass looked like grey clouds.

DELIVERY: ONE hidden <defs> sprite per page (27KB), injected by build-inline;
every glyph is a <use href="#ac-moon-face"> (~365 bytes), so the /moon/ hub
draws 35 moons for one sprite. Injection runs AFTER the JS bundle is inlined —
tide pages draw their moon at RUNTIME from bundled moon.js, so the need for
the sprite is only visible once the bundle is in the document (the first
attempt checked before inlining and left tide pages referencing a sprite that
was never injected). Idempotent; pages with no moon get nothing.

mnGlyph rewritten: shadow is ONE path (limb arc + terminator arc, sweep flags
by phase/hemisphere) — deliberately NO mask/clipPath, those need document-
unique ids and pages regenerate glyphs in the browser next to 30+ baked ones.
Fallback disc underneath if the sprite is somehow absent. Earthshine: shadow
opacity runs .91 (crescent) → .985 (near-full), physically motivated. `south`
now rotates face+phase together 180° for southern-hemisphere visitors.

Also: homepage moon card (phase + next-full-moon days, baked; the 3×/day
rebuild cron keeps it fresh) beside the work card, /moon/ pageview beacon
(pgmoon) which was missed at launch, moon icon in icons.mjs. CSS verified:
fullCss() still === style.css, moon section still reaches only moon pages.

VISUAL VERIFICATION (playwright, deviceScaleFactor 2): full 11-phase cycle at
r=68/17/14 N + S hemisphere, zoomed terminator at r=150, hub desktop+mobile,
calendar, homepage card, tide-page runtime row (NOAA stubbed). One bug found
this way: an over-eager string replace produced href="ac-moon-face" (no #) —
every glyph silently fell back to the flat disc. The check that caught it
asserts on the RENDERED <use> box, not just the markup.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — /moon/<city>/: moonrise & moonset by location (1,093 pages)
The agreed follow-up: city pages mirroring /sun/<city>/ 1:1, with the
moonrise-on-sun-pages work folded in since they share the plumbing.

WHAT EACH PAGE IS. The phase is worldwide; what earns a city page its URL is
what isn't: moonrise/moonset with COMPASS DIRECTION ("rises at 9:27 PM in the
ESE"), whether the moon is up right now and how high, a 7-day rise/set table,
and the next four phases in the CITY's own zone. All baked city-local for
crawlers, recomputed live (still city-local — that is the page's promise, so a
NY visitor reading the Portland page sees Portland times).

MATH ADDED to moon.mjs MOON_CORE: mnPos (altitude + compass bearing via the
standard hour-angle formula; az from south +180 -> bearing) and mnCompass
(16-point names). Validated physically: a waning gibbous from 45°N rises ESE
and sets WSW, alt≈0 at the rise/set instants the solver returns.

REUSE, NOT NEW PLUMBING: the city list IS build-sun's SUN_ALL (curated world +
census US top-1000, aliases already resolved) and SUN_STATES, so every /sun/
city has a /moon/ twin and the state hubs pair 1:1. Coastal pairing comes from
the same coastal.mjs map — a moon city page links its tide station exactly
where the sun page does (nearest-city-per-station rule, so e.g. san-francisco
links, malibu doesn't — behavior matches /sun/).

SUN-PAGE FOLD-IN: the golden-hour card's "Tonight's moon" line now carries
"rises 8:58 PM, sets 6:37 AM · moonrise details ->" linking the /moon/ twin —
baked in build-sun's ssr() via moonTimes + a tz-day-start helper, refreshed in
updateMoon() client-side (mnTimes was already inlined there via MOON_CORE).

HUB: "Moonrise & moonset by city" card (top-24 census cities by population +
all 51 state hubs behind a <details>), and the tz-matched moonrise card now
links "full moonrise page for {city}".

VERIFIED (playwright): Portland viewed from NY tz stays Portland-local with
directions; Sydney flips face+phase; Nome renders; boise-id (suffixed slug) ok;
state hub chips; sun/chicago fold-in live + linked; hub chips/states/card-link;
390px no overflow. check-pages green on all 2,828 pages (was 1,684).

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — location search on the moon pages + /moon/anywhere/
Search box on /moon/, every /moon/<city>/ and the new /moon/anywhere/, matching
the sun/tides search: type a city, a state name or code ("oregon", "tx"), or
"city, ST"; results land on the real static page.

REUSES /sun/cities.json rather than emitting a moon index. A sun slug IS a moon
slug (both families generate from SUN_ALL), so one file serves both and there is
nothing to keep in sync. That invariant is now ASSERTED in build-moon — if the
two lists ever diverge the build fails loudly instead of shipping dropdown rows
that 404. Verified: 1,087 index entries + 267 seed entries, 0 without a page.

/moon/anywhere/ (noindex,follow — a tool, not a landing page) is what makes
"any US location" true rather than "any of the top 1,000": towns the index
doesn't carry resolve through Open-Meteo geocoding and land here with
lat/lon/tz, which computes the same moonrise/moonset/7-day table for arbitrary
coordinates. Also takes GPS (BigDataCloud reverse-geocode for the name) and
remembers the last spot. It reuses the city page's painter verbatim — CITY_JS
is now definitions only, with the bootstrap split into CITY_BOOT — so the two
page types cannot drift.

CSS: the moon pages now pull the shared `search` section (probe already matches
td-qrow). One new rule, .card:has(#mn-results:not([hidden])){position:relative;
z-index:40} — without it later cards paint over the dropdown, since every .card
has backdrop-filter and is its own stacking context. Confirmed minimal: removing
it reproduces the overlap, and nothing further is required.

TESTING NOTE worth keeping: file:// gives false failures here — a directory URL
doesn't resolve to index.html and absolute hrefs resolve to the filesystem root.
The suite runs against a local http server instead. A first overlap check also
mis-flagged rows clipped by the list's own overflow:auto as "covered"; the check
now only considers rows inside the list's visible box.

Files: build-moon.mjs (searchJs/searchCard/SEARCH_SEED/buildAnywhere/guard),
build-inline.mjs (page list), assets/css/parts/22-moon.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — moon face: ray systems and bright ejecta
The impact splash was missing. Three things added, all on the bright end:

1. RAYS NOW SIT ON TOP OF THE MARIA, not under them. This was the real bug: the
   first version reasoned that basalt overprints highland dust, so it painted
   rays first. Backwards — every ray crater is far YOUNGER than the sea it lies
   on, which is exactly why Tycho's rays visibly run across Mare Nubium and
   Serenitatis in any photograph. Painting them under buried the single most
   recognisable feature on the disc.
2. Ejecta APRONS — a diffuse bright splash each ray crater sits in, so the rays
   read as debris thrown out of a hole rather than lines drawn on the surface.
3. SPARKS — small fresh high-albedo craters (Messier's twin "comet tail",
   Censorinus, Dionysius, Menelaus, Manilius) that catch the eye on a full moon.

Ray systems went 5 -> 14 (added Byrgius A, Anaxagoras, Stevinus A, Furnerius A,
Thales, Glushko, Autolycus, Zucchius, Madler).

TAPER is what makes them look real. A uniform-width, uniform-opacity stroke per
ray read as a laser beam — the first attempt was badly overdone. Each ray is now
a chain of 3 segments thinning and fading outward (fade = 1 - 0.58t), with a
slight wobble so they are not perfectly radial. Two overshoots were needed to
find the level: too bright (starburst), then too faint (quadratic falloff +
heavy blur erased them). Settled between.

SIZE: the sprite went 27kb -> 57kb, and it is inlined into 1,100+ pages, so that
is ~33MB of tree. Trimmed back from a 65kb peak by dropping the 4th ray segment
(a near-invisible tail costing ~8kb) and omitting opacity="1". 7.8kb gzipped,
which is what actually crosses the wire. Worth watching if more is added here.

Files: seo/tools/moon-face.mjs only. check-pages green on 2,829; search suite
still passes; sprite still injected exactly once per page that draws a moon.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — date selector drives every moon card
A <input type="date"> beside the moon in the hero, on /moon/, all 1,093 city
pages and /moon/anywhere/, defaulting to today. Everything on the page is now
computed from ONE instant — mnRefMs() — so picking a date moves the phase,
illumination, moon age, 30-day strip, next-phase countdowns, moonrise/moonset
and the 7-day table together, instead of each card carrying its own "now".

TODAY STAYS LIVE: mnRefMs() returns Date.now() while the selected date is today
(the default), so the page ticks exactly as before; only an explicitly chosen
date freezes it to noon. The 60s interval is likewise gated on mnIsToday(), so
a chosen date cannot creep. A "Today" chip appears once you leave today.

REFERENCE ZONE: MN_TZ (null = visitor's, else the city's). The hub reasons in
the visitor's zone; a city page reasons in the CITY's — verified by opening
Sacramento from a Tokyo browser and getting Sacramento's date in the picker.
Offsets use the standard format-then-diff trick applied twice so DST settles.

The 7-day header carries a SECOND picker. Both are the same control: every
[data-mn-date] is wired to one handler that writes all of them and repaints
once, so they cannot disagree — driving either one moves the whole page.

Also: the 30-day strip card is now on city pages and /moon/anywhere/ too (it was
hub-only), directly below the hero, and the city "next phases" tiles gained
data-mn-kind + a countdown span so they re-solve and re-sort on a date change.

TESTING NOTE: a "phase doesn't follow the date" failure was my assertion, not
the code — Dec 25 2026 is ALSO a waning gibbous, so comparing phase names alone
proved nothing. Compare name+percentage. (Second time this session a weak
assertion cried wolf; the first was rows clipped by overflow:auto.)

Files: build-moon.mjs (DATE_JS, dateBox, hub/city/anywhere markup + painters),
assets/css/parts/22-moon.css (.mn-hero-top/.mn-datebox/.mn-cardhead).

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — homepage moon strip, nearby picker, counties, card order
Four changes plus a copy edit.

1. HOMEPAGE MOON CARD: the bottom link is replaced by a scrolling 30-night
   strip; each night links /moon/?date=YYYY-MM-DD. DATE_JS now honours ?date=,
   so the moon page opens already showing that night — and /moon/ resolves the
   visitor's own location from their zone, so it is that night where they are.
2. NEARBY PICKER under the date box in the hero, on /moon/, every city page and
   /moon/anywhere/. City pages bake their 12 nearest (great-circle); the hub
   fills it once the location resolves. Jumping carries the selected date, so
   you can compare one night across locations.
3. STATE PAGES BY COUNTY. Needed data we did not have: us-cities.json has no
   county and guessing would have put wrong facts on 51 public pages. Added
   make-city-counties.mjs — joins kelvins/US-Cities-Database (CC0) by city+state
   and VERIFIES each hit by distance, so a name match >30mi is rejected (that
   caught La Quinta CA matching a namesake 141mi away). 1005/1005 resolved:
   915 exact, 14 normalised (St./Saint, "Lexington-Fayette"), 76 nearest-town-
   in-state within 25mi. Four accuracy bugs were caught by spot-checking and
   fixed, not shipped: Philadelphia landed in Delaware County (the dataset has
   two rows; a city sharing its name with a county now wins), "Miami-dade"
   casing, "Baltimore City County"/"Orleans County"/"Anchorage County" from a
   blanket " County" suffix (LA=Parish, AK=left bare, independent cities keep
   "X City"). Counties A-Z, cities A-Z within.
4. Homepage order is now tides -> sun -> moon.
Copy: the /moon/ hint is now "Moon rise & set depend on location, phase do not."

BUG WORTH REMEMBERING: /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/ written inside a JS TEMPLATE
LITERAL emits /^d{4}-d{2}-d{2}$/ — the backtick string eats the escape before
the browser sees it. Needs \\d in the .mjs source. Added a scan over every *_JS
template for single-backslash regex escapes; it reports clean now. Also: the
hub painted before mnInitDate read the URL, so a ?date= arrived too late —
it repaints if the URL selected a non-today date.

Files: build-moon.mjs, build-home.mjs, make-city-counties.mjs (new),
seo/_data/city-counties.json (new, 32K), 11-home.css, 22-moon.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — /sun/ hub: simpler controls under the search box
Removed the "My Cities ▾" and "Edit" buttons and the saved panel they opened;
"My location" is now "Use my location"; added "Nearby cities" beside it, which
drops the 8 closest cities into the search dropdown with distances.

Removing the markup was safe with no JS deleted: every My Cities handler was
already null-guarded (renderMy() returns early without #sun-mylist, the button
listeners are behind `if(myBtn)`), so the block no-ops. The sun_favs STORE stays
— the ＋ Save buttons still feed the "near you and beyond" mini dials and the
tides page reads the same key — so saving still does something visible.

Nearby ranks by real great-circle miles, not the squared-degree sort used for
tie-breaking elsewhere (degrees of longitude shrink toward the poles, so that
ordering is visibly wrong at high latitude), measures from the same curNearest
the rest of the page uses, and lazy-loads /sun/cities.json so the list is real
towns nearby rather than only big world cities.

TWO BUGS FOUND BY TESTING, both pre-existing in spirit:
1. The open dropdown COVERED the button that opens it, so the second click could
   not close it. .hs-results is absolutely positioned from wherever it sits in
   flow, so the <ul> now comes AFTER the button row.
2. curNearest took the FIRST city in the (alphabetical) LIST matching the
   visitor's zone: New York -> "Atlanta", London -> "Edinburgh". Harmless while
   it only ranked mini dials, but the new list says "Closest to X" out loud, so
   it now prefers the city the zone is NAMED after — the same rule /moon/ has
   used since it was written. This improves the mini dials too.

Files: seo/tools/build-sun.mjs only.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — moon: real location instead of a time-zone guess
mnGuessCity picks the city a zone is NAMED after, so every visitor in
America/Los_Angeles was told "Los Angeles" — including one in Portland. A zone
that spans the whole US west coast simply cannot answer "where am I".

Fixed by refining with /api/geo, the existing Cloudflare Function that echoes
the coarse lat/lon already attached to the request by the edge: no permission
prompt, no external call, no cookie. It runs AFTER the first paint and only
swaps in a closer city, so the page never blocks on it and still works when the
endpoint is missing (local preview, Functions disabled) — verified by stubbing
404, null coords, and three real cities.

That exposed a second bug: the hub's Nearby list only had LIST (curated world
cities), so Portland offered Cannon Beach 67mi and Seattle 145mi while
Beaverton sits 7mi away. It now pulls /sun/cities.json (previously loaded only
when someone typed in the search box) and refills once it lands. City pages were
never affected — they bake their neighbours from SUN_ALL at build.

Also: the hero's date + nearby controls are right-justified at every width
(.mn-hero-top spans the card, margin-left:auto on .mn-heroctl), moon left,
controls hard right, facts beneath.

NOTE: the same tz-guess wart is still in build-sun.mjs's curNearest. It now
prefers the zone-named city (fixed earlier today), so it says "Los Angeles" to a
Portland visitor too. /api/geo would fix it the same way — not done here, since
the ask was the moon page.

Files: build-moon.mjs, assets/css/parts/22-moon.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — /sun/ hub location, and the moon pickers lose their labels
Three asks, one session.

1. /sun/ "Nearby cities" answered a Portland visitor with "Closest to Los
Angeles" and eight southern-California suburbs. Same root cause the moon page
had: curNearest starts as a TIME ZONE match, and America/Los_Angeles is not a
location. Fixed the same way — /api/geo (Cloudflare's edge IP geo: no prompt,
no external call, no cookie) refines it after first paint. New setFix(lat,lon,
precise) is the single entry point for a position: it re-runs the match once
/sun/cities.json lands (matching LIST alone answers Portland with Seattle at
145 mi while Beaverton sits at 6), drops a stale re-match via fixSeq, and never
lets a coarse fix overwrite a granted GPS one. "Use my precise location" now
goes through it too, so GPS also gets the full 1,087-city index instead of the
curated world list, and the open dropdown refreshes in place.

2. The moon page's own edge refine had the same LIST-only wart: it read FULL
before anything had loaded it. It now asks ensureFull() and re-matches from
mnEdgeRefill when the index arrives, with mnPrecise guarding the GPS case.

3. Removed the DATE / NEARBY / FROM captions above the moon pickers (aria-label
carries the accessible name; the select's first option already says what it
does) and gave the whole family one right-justification rule instead of three
half-conflicting ones — .mn-datebox/.mn-nearbox now share margin-left:auto +
align-items:flex-end, so the narrow "Today" button lines up under the field, and
.mn-cardhead dropped justify-content:space-between so the 7-day picker stays
right when the row wraps. Sun pages already had no captions and were already
right-justified (verified: 19px to the card edge, same as the moon's now).

Verified in headless Chromium against a stubbed /api/geo: Portland → Beaverton
6mi/Lake Oswego 7mi on both pages; London → London; endpoint 404 → clean
fallback to the old tz guess, no page errors; GPS at Denver survives a
late-arriving edge fix and index on both pages.

Open: `npm run check` still reports the 4 passed one-off dates (World Cup final,
3 SpaceX launches) — pre-existing, needs a /maintain pass with network.

Files: build-sun.mjs, build-moon.mjs, assets/css/parts/22-moon.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — sun & moon batch 1: live sun position, full twilight, select fix
Worked from a third-party SEO review of /sun/ and /moon/. First finding: most of
its 15 "priorities" already ship — golden hour and blue hour, solar noon, daily
change, nearby cities as real links (not just a dropdown), seasonal copy, the
per-city daylight swing, the yearly chart, cross-links to moon/tides, FAQs. Only
four items were genuinely missing, and those are this batch.

1. THE DROPDOWN BUG, which the review ranked #1 and I had misdiagnosed as purely
a location problem. A <select> only fires "change" when the value CHANGES, and
both bfcache and form-state restoration bring the chosen option back when you
press Back — so re-picking the city you had just viewed fired nothing and the
control looked dead. mnInitNear now resets selectedIndex to 0 before navigating
and again on pageshow. Reproduced and fixed under Playwright: pick → Back →
pick the same option → navigates.

2. Live sun position (new card on every /sun/ city page, /anywhere/ and
/my-location/): altitude, azimuth, compass direction and which band is running
— full daylight through the three twilights to night — with a small horizon
diagram. New SUNPOS_JS + nSunPos in lib.mjs, deliberately the same Meeus series
sunCalc uses, so feeding it a sunCalc sunrise returns −0.833° (verified at four
latitudes incl. southern hemisphere, where noon azimuth correctly flips to
~360°). Baked for the build minute so crawlers see real numbers; repainted on
load, every 30s, and on visibilitychange. It ignores the date picker on purpose
— "right now" is always now.

3. Nautical and astronomical twilight. Only civil ("first light/last light")
existed. The golden/blue-hour card is now "Twilight, golden hour & blue hour"
with all three bands morning and evening. At Reykjavik and Nome in July the
deep bands correctly print "—" (the sun never reaches −12°/−18°) and the note
explains the dash rather than leaving it bare.

4. Moon "up right now" gained the compass bearing — "Yes — 12° up, look SE".
mnPos already returned azimuth; the page was throwing it away and recomputing
altitude by hand.

Not done, for later batches: fast autocomplete, interactive year-chart hover,
daylight rank ("212th longest day"), monthly moon calendar, richer FAQs.

Files: build-sun.mjs, build-moon.mjs, lib.mjs, assets/css/parts/20-content.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — batch 2: real autocomplete, shared by /sun/ and /moon/
The review's "search works but doesn't feel fast" was three separate things.

1. THE INDEX ARRIVED TOO LATE. /sun/cities.json was fetched by the FIRST
KEYSTROKE, so the opening characters were matched against the small baked seed
and the list visibly rewrote itself a beat later — typing "port" showed almost
nothing until the real index landed. Both pages now warm it on
requestIdleCallback (600ms setTimeout fallback) after first paint, so the first
key already has all ~1,090 cities.

2. RANKING. The old matcher was two buckets, prefix then substring, in index
order. Now three — whole-label prefix, word-start ("louis" in "St. Louis"),
then buried substring — so "salem" gives Salem, Salem, Winston-Salem, and only
then Jerusalem. Inside a bucket the index's own population order stands, with
cities within 100 mi of the visitor pulled forward by a FACTOR (i*0.1) rather
than to the front. That distinction is the whole design: it makes "salem" give
Salem OR in Portland and Salem MA in Boston (verified both), while "new" still
gives New York and not Newberg, OR 20 miles away.

3. IT DIDN'T BEHAVE LIKE AN AUTOCOMPLETE. Added Down/Up through the
suggestions with a highlight and aria-activedescendant, Escape to close, Enter
on the highlighted row (falling through to the old "best match" when nothing is
highlighted — the pages keep owning Enter, so there was no need for two
listeners on one input fighting over registration order). The typed run is now
bolded inside each suggestion, built with createTextNode + <b>, never
innerHTML: city labels are data.

Also: the matcher called toLowerCase() on all ~1,090 labels on EVERY keystroke.
Lowercased keys are computed once per index now.

All of it is one shared AC_JS in lib.mjs — /sun/ and /moon/ search the same
index and had grown two near-identical copies of the matcher, which is exactly
how they would have drifted. Smoke-tested 11 page types for JS errors, clean.

Files: lib.mjs (new AC_JS), build-sun.mjs, build-moon.mjs, 20-content.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — batch 3: /moon/calendar/, a real month grid
The review asked for a moon calendar. The site already had TWO calendars —
/moon/<year>/ and /moon/full-moon-calendar/ — but both are LISTS of the four
primary phases. Neither answers "what will the moon look like on the 14th",
which is the only question a calendar exists to answer. So: a day-by-day grid.

/moon/calendar/ (hub, this month + every month linked) plus 60 month pages at
/moon/calendar/<month>-<year>/ across MOON_YEARS, in the sitemap at 0.55.
Seven equal columns; out-of-month days drawn but dimmed so the shape of the
month reads; today outlined; primary phases called out in accent. Real
moonGlyph SVGs, not emoji — the project rule, and they mirror correctly and
scale, which emoji do not.

THE DATE PROBLEM, which is the whole reason this needed care: a phase is one
instant worldwide, so the calendar DAY it lands on depends on where you are.
August 2026's full moon is the 28th at 04:18 UTC — which is the evening of the
27th in California. The grid is baked in UTC (crawlable, and the convention the
year tables already use) and re-bucketed client-side into the visitor's own
dates. Verified in three zones:

  UTC          full moon 2026-08-28, new moon 08-12
  Los Angeles  full moon 2026-08-27, last quarter 08-05, first quarter 08-19
  Tokyo        full moon 2026-08-28, new moon 08-13

Each cell links to /moon/?date=YYYY-MM-DD — a URL the hub already understood
(mnInitDate parses it), so the "clickable" ask needed no new plumbing.

TWO FAILURES WORTH RECORDING, both caught by the gate rather than by me:
1. check-pages refused the build — the new pages still linked
   /assets/css/style.css because build-inline discovers pages from an explicit
   list, not the filesystem, and nothing had told it about moon/calendar/.
   This is exactly the fail-safe doing its job: an un-inlined page would have
   shipped a render-blocking request.
2. My first fix put `await import()` inside a non-async .map() callback. Now
   build-moon exports CAL_SLUGS, a flat list both build-inline and
   build-sitemap consume instead of re-deriving paths.

Files: build-moon.mjs, build-inline.mjs, build-sitemap.mjs, 22-moon.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — batch 4: today's daylight rank
The last "annual statistics" item from the review. Sun city pages already named
the longest and shortest day; they could not say where TODAY sits between them.
Two new lines in the "Daylight & seasons" card:

  Of the 365 days this year, today is the 78th longest here.
  The shortest day of the year is 144 days away, on December 21 — 8 h 42 m.

The annual scan used to step every OTHER day — fine for finding extremes, no
use for a rank — so it now walks all 365/366. Ranked on exact milliseconds but
tied within a minute: either side of a solstice, neighbouring days differ by
seconds, and "the 3rd longest day" is false precision when it is one second off
the 4th. Above 4 ties the sentence changes shape instead of picking arbitrarily.

POLAR DAYS ARE NOT GAPS. A day with no sunrise is either 24 hours of daylight
or none, and which one decides whether it sorts above or below every ordinary
day; the old scan just skipped them (`if (!sc.rise) continue`). Solar noon
altitude tells them apart, via nSunPos from batch 1. No city in the index sits
far enough north to hit it, so it was unit-tested directly: Longyearbyen 78°N
returns MIDNIGHT SUN in July and POLAR NIGHT in January.

Hemisphere correctness falls out for free and is a nice check: Portland is the
78th longest day on July 30, Sydney the 286th — 365-286 = 79th shortest, the
mirror image, as it must be.

ONE BUG WORTH RECORDING. The client recompute (the rank changes at midnight and
the page can be cached for hours) first enumerated the year in the VISITOR's
calendar. A reader in Tokyo was told a different fact about Portland than a
reader in Portland — the solstice line moved a day. "The 78th longest day in
Portland" is a fact about Portland, so it now reads today and the year from
C.tz, the same dkey() rule the rest of the page uses, and steps UTC noon
exactly as the build does. Verified identical from five zones.

Known nit, pre-existing, NOT introduced here: the year chart's note says the
longest day is Jun 21 while the facts card says June 20. The chart samples every
third day; the scan walks every day; the two dates are a second of daylight
apart. Worth reconciling in a later pass.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — homepage moon card: my-location + search
The moon card was the only location-aware card on the homepage with no way in:
the sun and tides cards each end in a pair of buttons (my location / search),
the moon card ended at the 30-night strip. Added the same pair, sharing the
existing .home-sun-foot / .home-tides-foot rules rather than a third copy.

"Search a Location" goes to /moon/, which already has the search box (and, since
batch 2, real autocomplete). "See My Location" needed one small thing: it points
at /moon/anywhere/?geo=1, and that page now honours ?geo=1 by calling its
existing askGeo() on arrival. Without it the button would have landed the
visitor on a page whose only content is another button to press — the sun card's
/sun/my-location/ equivalent asks immediately, so this now matches.

Verified in three clean contexts (the first run was a false alarm — /moon/
anywhere/ restores the last location from localStorage.mn_any, and reusing the
browser context made it look like the page was auto-locating without the param):

  fresh, no ?geo,  permission granted  -> waits for a tap   (unchanged)
  fresh, ?geo=1,   permission granted  -> locates, Portland, OR
  fresh, ?geo=1,   permission DENIED   -> ordinary search page, no error

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — batch 5: the year chart becomes readable
Last item from the review. The annual sunrise/sunset chart showed a shape and
nothing else — you could see days lengthening but could not ask it anything.
Now hover, drag or arrow-key any date and get sunrise, sunset, day length, the
change from the day before, and that evening's golden hour.

The curves are still drawn from every-third-day samples, but the READ-OUT
solves the exact day under the pointer, so what it reports is the real answer
for that date rather than the nearest plotted sample. Polar days say "midnight
sun" / "polar night" instead of going blank.

Keyboard: the chart is a real control now — tabbable, arrows step a day, Shift
a week, Home/End jump to the ends of the year, Escape dismisses. It announces
through a visually-hidden live region (which already existed as
.visually-hidden — I added a duplicate .sr-only and removed it again).

ALSO FIXED, the nit flagged in batch 4: the chart said the longest day was Jun
21 while the facts card said June 20. The chart took its extremes from the
every-third-day DRAWING samples, so the solstice landed on whichever of the
three days happened to be sampled. Sampling is fine for a curve and wrong for a
date; the extremes now get their own every-day scan and the two agree.

THREE MEASUREMENT TRAPS, all mine, all caught by testing rather than by reading:
1. First pointer test reported empty tooltips — the chart was below the fold,
   so mouse.move was landing outside the viewport entirely.
2. Then "77px overflow" and "guide line 730px off" — both measured while the
   tooltip was hidden, when getBoundingClientRect returns all zeros.
3. The real bug the corrected measurement then exposed: the tooltip is placed
   as a percentage OF ITS WRAPPER, and the wrapper was filling the card while
   the svg is capped at 660px, so every reading sat sideways of its line.
   Wrapper now shares the chart's max-width. Line tracks the cursor within 1px
   at 900px and 700px wide, exactly at 390px.

The floating panel cannot fit beside the line on a ~350px-wide chart at any x,
so below 560px it stops floating and becomes a strip across the top. Overflow
is now <=0 at all three widths.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — one cross-link component, and a gate that proves it
Reviewed a cross-linking strategy doc. Most of it already shipped; the parts
worth doing were the ones it didn't name — consistency and verification.

1. ONE COMPONENT (seo/tools/crosslinks.mjs). Sun, moon and tide pages all
linked to each other already, but through three separately-written blocks: a
rich card on sun pages, a sentence in a paragraph on moon pages, one <p
class="hint"> on tide pages. Same relationship, three levels of detail, three
voices — the same drift that gave /sun/ and /moon/ two different search
matchers. Now one strip of tiles: sun times, moon phase, predicted tide
station, minus whichever family the page belongs to. The doc's framing line
lives IN the component so it can't be dropped from one family by accident.
The tide tile bakes no tide times on purpose: NOAA is a runtime fetch, and
baking would put a network dependency behind ~2,200 sun and moon pages.

2. A RECIPROCITY GATE (check-crosslinks.mjs), and it earned its keep on the
first run. CLAUDE.md has described these links as reciprocal since the coastal
map landed, and coastal.mjs's own comment asserted it. It was true of the DATA
and merely hoped of the PAGES. The gate found four genuinely one-way pairs:

  /tides/dauphin-island-al/     -> /sun/mobile-al/        (no link back)
  /tides/fernandina-beach-fl/   -> /sun/jacksonville-fl/  (no link back)
  /tides/kiptopeke-va/          -> /sun/hampton-va/       (no link back)
  /tides/wrightsville-beach-nc/ -> /sun/wilmington-nc/    (no link back)

Cause: four cities are the nearest listed city for TWO stations, and
sunToStation keeps only the closest, so the second station pointed at a page
that pointed elsewhere. Fixed with sunToStations (all pairs, nearest first) —
no page loses content and every pair is two-way. 2,434 links across 2,248
pages now verified on every build.

ON THE DOC ITSELF, two recommendations I did not follow and said so: dropping
the /tides/ explanation of why tides are hazardous on flat beaches and bores
(that is physics, already hedged, and its ABSENCE is the harder position to
defend — the doc conflates warnings with recommendations, which cut opposite
ways), and removing "photographers" from the golden-hour definition (the term
is defined by reference to photography; naming who uses it advises nobody).
Its closing ask — reposition tides as predictions plus a conditions disclaimer
— was already done and already stronger than proposed.

Follow-up not taken: sun pages still inline MOON_CORE for updateMoon(), whose
target elements left with the old moon paragraph. Dead but harmless; worth
pruning for page weight.

Files: crosslinks.mjs (new), check-crosslinks.mjs (new), coastal.mjs,
build-sun.mjs, build-moon.mjs, build-tides.mjs, 20-content.css, package.json,
CLAUDE.md.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — targeting "sunrise tomorrow"
Audit first: the word "tomorrow" appeared on a sun city page exactly ONCE, in a
countdown line that only renders after sunset. A crawl at midday saw none of it.
The 7-day table did answer the question — as "Fri, Jul 31", which is the answer
to a date query, not to the words people actually type. So the pages ranked for
"<city> sunrise" and had nothing to say to "sunrise tomorrow <city>".

Added, on all 1,093 city pages:
- A "Sunrise & sunset tomorrow in <city>" card: tomorrow's rise, set, day length
  and the delta vs today, plus the weekday and date spelled out. Baked for
  crawlers, repainted client-side — the baked value comes from the BUILD clock,
  and someone reading at 11pm local wants the day after THEIR today.
- An FAQ entry, which also lands in the FAQPage JSON-LD, so the answer is
  eligible as a rich result rather than only as body text.
- Today / Tomorrow labels on the first two rows of the 7-day table, so the word
  sits next to a real time.
- Title: "<city> Sunrise & Sunset Times — Today & Tomorrow". A deliberate change
  to 1,093 titles; the head term is untouched and still leads.

ONE BUG FOUND WHILE DOING IT, and it had been silently eating content. The
description picks the first candidate under ~160 chars from a list the comment
describes as "longest-first". The list was actually SHORTEST-first, so candidate
one always won and every richer variant was dead code. Adding a tomorrow variant
to that list would have shipped nothing. Reordered richest-first; 1,093 of 1,095
descriptions now name tomorrow's sunrise, none over 160.

Verified across latitudes: Portland 5:54 AM / 8:41 PM, Nome 6:03 AM / 12:14 AM
(sets after midnight), Sydney 6:49 AM / 5:16 PM (southern winter).

Not done, offered: /sun/tomorrow/ as a hub for the generic head term.
Pre-existing, not mine: /sun/my-location/ is indexable with a 186-char
description that will truncate in results.

Files: build-sun.mjs, assets/css/parts/20-content.css.

## 2026-07-30 (cont.) — tide search: find any NOAA location, not just the curated 101
Reported: searching "Nome Alaska" on /tides/ returned nothing, while /sun/ and
/moon/ find any US town. Nome has its own NOAA station (9468756).

TWO CAUSES, and the first is the interesting one. The full ~3,300-station NOAA
directory was ALREADY wired up — but behind a "Search all 3,300+ NOAA stations"
button that only appears AFTER a search has failed. So the capability existed
and the honest visitor experience was still "nothing came up". Same class of
bug as the sun search's index arriving on the first keystroke: the data was
fine, the moment it loaded was not. It now warms on idle, unprompted; the
button stays as the retry path when the fetch fails (verified: directory
blocked -> no JS errors, button still offered).

Second: labels carry two-letter codes ("Nome, Norton Sound, AK") and the
matcher did one raw substring test, so "Nome Alaska" could never match. Now the
query expands state names to codes and every TOKEN must appear, so "Nome",
"Nome Alaska", "nome, ak" and "alaska nome" all land on the same station.
Longest state names are substituted first so "new york" is not eaten by "york".

Also: the curated tier and the directory overlap, and dedupe keyed on
slug-or-id, so Boston listed twice (curated has a slug, the directory row only
an id). Claims the id too now.

ON "not all cities are coastal": inland searches are answered honestly rather
than with a distant station. /tides/near-me/'s geocoded path now measures
against the full directory and refuses to present anything past 120 miles as a
result — it says the place is almost certainly inland and offers the station as
context. Denver returns "no station matches" on the hub.

I FIRST PATCHED THE WRONG COMPONENT. /tides/ runs assets/js/tides.js; the block
I edited first was /tides/near-me/'s separate search. Those edits are kept (the
near-me search gained the same state parsing, full-directory reach and inland
honesty) but the reported bug was never in that file.

Still true and worth fixing later: NOAA's mdapi is blocked by this
environment's network policy, so the directory could only be tested against a
stub with real station ids and coordinates.

## 2026-08-01 — positioning cleanup, /methodology/, measured timing

Three things, in order.

**Footers.** 2,782 clock/timer/astronomy pages carried seven countdown-category
links (graduations, shopping, parties). That was right when the hubs were
reachable only from /countdown/; they now have inbound links from /countdown/,
the homepage countdown block, and every page in their own category. Tool pages
get one "Browse event countdowns" link instead; the 228 countdown/event pages
are unchanged. `TOOL_PAGE` in build-inline.mjs is the classifier, and anything
it doesn't match keeps the old behaviour. Also dropped the unsupported "best
countdown, reminder, and event site online" line from the suggestion box.

**/methodology/** — hub + 5 pages (sunrise/sunset, moon phase, tide
predictions, time zones, browser timing). The design rule: the generator READS
its accuracy figures from JSON written by measurement scripts and THROWS if a
field it quotes is missing. A methodology page cannot ship an invented error
bar. Linked from the sun/moon/tide/world-clock/timer hubs, the homepage guides
line, the site search index, /browser-limitations/ and /stopwatch/.

**The measurements.** measure-timing.mjs (Chromium 141, headless): a foreground
1s tick drifted −1 ms over 60 s, max tick error 1 ms; at 20x CPU throttle max
tick error 10 ms and ~1.6 s/hour for a naive counter, while the Date.now()-
recomputed value stayed exact; nested setTimeout(0) clamps at ~4.2 ms.
measure-sun.mjs: the closed-form rise/set solver is within ~60 s of an
iterative solve typically, 115 s worst (65°, September equinox), 48 comparisons.

TWO THINGS I THREW OUT rather than publish. The page-freeze test appeared to
work — CDP accepted `Page.setWebLifecycleState: frozen` with no error — but the
page kept ticking at 999–1001 ms and still ran JS on request, which a frozen
page cannot do. It never engaged; publishing those ten ticks as "a frozen tab
keeps counting" would have been a measurement of nothing. The test is now a
control that ASSERTS the freeze fails. And the two pages originally linked to
seo/tools/measure-*.mjs on GitHub — the repo is private, so those links would
have 404'd for every visitor. Now named as paths, not linked.

Open: no Firefox/Safari numbers, and there won't be until those engines can be
installed. Hidden-tab throttling and rAF-while-hidden are unmeasurable headless
for the same reason the freeze was; all four sit in `unmeasured` with reasons.

## 2026-08-01 — alarm close-button bug, homepage intro, pop-out windows

FIXED A REAL DEAD BUTTON, and the cause was not where it looked. The
full-screen alarm's top-left X worked on /alarm-clock/, via ?bedside=1, and
while idle — I could not reproduce it. The cause was popTop(): it clones the
panel as a placeholder and did `cloneEl.removeAttribute('id')`, which strips
only the CLONE'S OWN id and leaves every descendant id in place. After using
"Show on top" the document held a second #ac-close-tl, getElementById returned
the clone's listener-less copy, and the X became a button that looked right
and did nothing. Three fixes: strip every id from the clone; delegate the
close handler so it follows whichever button was clicked (the panel MOVES
between documents, so init-time references go stale); and make closeView()
tear the view down directly when there is no real fullscreen to exit — that
last case was independently broken and is now covered by a test.

Homepage intro cut from six clauses to one, opening on "Set an alarm or start
a timer" instead of on what the site does not ask of you. Links were already
between the intro and the alarm card. Stopwatch: the Lap button now carries
the chosen noun, so picking "Interval" no longer leaves the one control you
press as the only thing still saying "Lap".

NEW: POPOUT_JS in lib.mjs — "Always visible" on the timer and stopwatch,
matching the alarm's "Show on top". Document Picture-in-Picture; the host
element is MOVED not copied, so a running clock keeps running and its buttons
keep their listeners. The helper strips every id from its placeholder clone,
which is the alarm bug above written down so it cannot recur. Injected into
<head> by build-inline, keyed off the call site. Desktop Chrome/Edge only —
the button hides itself where the API is missing, plus a <560px media query.

Verified live: stopwatch ticks 00:01.77 -> 00:02.87 inside the floating
window, Lap present, restores cleanly with the placeholder removed and no
duplicate ids; timer counts 00:04:59 -> 00:04:58 in its own window.

build-timers.mjs, stopwatch/index.html, assets/css/parts/02-base.css.

## 2026-08-01 — close-button hardening, home nav removed, pop-out rebuilt

CLOSE BUTTON, second attempt. Last session's fix (duplicate ids in popTop's
clone) was real and shipped — verified in the built HTML — but did NOT fix the
reported symptom. It still could not be reproduced headlessly: the X works on
/alarm-clock/, via ?bedside=1, while idle, and when clicking the SVG path
directly. So this pass stops chasing a cause and removes every way the control
can fail: closeView() now asks the browser to exit, then VERIFIES 350ms later
and tears the view down itself if the browser did not — the previous version
called exitFullscreen() and trusted it, which is silently a no-op if the state
was entered some other way. Plus Escape as a second exit, pointerup alongside
click (400ms dedupe) in case a touch layer eats the synthetic click, and the
hit target grown 32 -> 46px. STILL UNREPRODUCED; asked the owner for browser
and device.

Homepage: the .home-nav pill row is gone. It repeated as bordered chips the
same eight destinations the intro sentence links and the eight cards below
already are — three copies of one nav above the fold.

POP-OUT REBUILT. It was missing from /timer/ entirely (the hub renders the
multi-timer board, not the single-timer card I had edited) and buried as a
sixth button in the stopwatch's secondary row. Now its own row with a size
selector (Small/Medium/Large/Extra large, remembered) on /stopwatch/, /timer/
and every duration page. The pop-out carries the tool only — 0 headings or
instructions, verified — with a Close button bottom-right and a note reading
"Always on top".

NO always-on-top TOGGLE, deliberately. A Document Picture-in-Picture window is
floated above other windows by the OS and a page cannot turn that off, so a
switch would be a control that does nothing. The note states the behaviour
instead.

The read-out is sized in cqw against the host container, so it follows the
window: measured 300px -> 45px digits, 500px -> 75px, 760px -> 114px.

## 2026-08-01 — the top-left close button, third pass (the actual structural fix)

The owner's report that the BOTTOM-RIGHT ✕ works and the TOP-LEFT one does not
is what finally located this. Both run the same closeView() through the same
delegated handler, so the difference was never the code — it was the DOM.

#ac-close (works) sits in .ac-controls, a direct child of body.
#ac-close-tl (broken) sat inside #ac-panel > #ac-stage, and in full screen
#ac-stage is position:fixed with z-index:9998 — a STACKING CONTEXT. The X's
z-index:10001 was scoped inside it and could not rise above anything painted
over the stage, and every ancestor between it and the viewport was one more
thing able to trap its fixed positioning or intercept its hit testing.

It still would not reproduce headlessly — position, stacking and hit testing
measured correct at 900, 1000, 1366, 1440 and 1920px. So instead of guessing
at the remaining environmental difference for a fourth time, the button was
given the SAME STRUCTURAL POSITION as the one that works: moved to be a direct
child of body at startup, outside every stacking context, with z-index raised
to max. It is display:none unless body.ac-fs so the move is free, and it also
stops the button travelling into the picture-in-picture window when popTop()
moves the panel.

Verified both buttons and Escape at 1920x1080, 1366x768 and 900x700; the X
still renders at 12,12 46x46 and survives Show-on-top.

LESSON: when two controls share a handler and only one works, stop reading the
handler. The bug is in the tree, not the function.
