## 2026-08-01 — full-screen stopwatch overlap (two separate causes)

Reported: in full screen the digits sit on top of the Start/Lap buttons. There
were TWO bugs stacked, and fixing only the first left the symptom identical.

1. MINE, from the pop-out work. The .sw-pop-host wrapper I added around the
   display and the controls made them ONE flex child of .sw-fs instead of two,
   so the column's gap:22px no longer applied between them — a 22px overlap.
   Fixed with display:contents on the host, which generates no box at all, so
   both go back to being direct children of whatever lays them out. POPOUT_JS
   re-declares display:block inside the pop-out, where the host has to be a
   real box to act as a size container.

2. PRE-EXISTING, and the bigger one. .sw-fs #sw-time used transform:scale(1.5)
   above 1000px and scale(1.9) above 1500px. A transform does not affect
   layout: the display kept the box of its unscaled self and painted up to 1.9x
   bigger over the top of it, spilling across the buttons — and getting worse
   the wider the window (-29px at 1920). Replaced with font-size (min(162px,
   15vw) and min(205px,14vw), matching what the old scales rendered), so the
   box grows with the digits and the column below simply moves down.

Verified at 1920, 1500, 1400, 1100 and 900px: display fits its row at every
one, 36px to the buttons, 38px to the stats. Pop-out still ticks and restores.

Files: assets/css/parts/{02-base,05-tools-b}.css, seo/tools/lib.mjs.

## 2026-08-01 — pop-out: whole row scales, size menu inside the window

The read-out was the only thing sized in container units, so a wide pop-out
gave you huge digits next to a 14px Start button. Every part of the row now
scales together against the host container: the timer's name, the separator
and Reset link, the close cross, the read-out, and the Start button. Measured
on the multi-timer board — 260px: name 11px / digits 29px / Start 11px;
780px: name 26px / digits 74px / Start 24px, card height 74 -> 184. Stopwatch
the same (digits 39 -> 117px, buttons 11 -> 22px).

A size menu (Min / Sm / Med / Lg / Xlg) sits in the pop-out's bottom bar next
to Close, and the choice is remembered. Heights are multiplied per host —
hk 1.8 for the multi-timer board, which stacks three cards in a column, 1.15
for the stopwatch's lap table, 1 for a single timer.

RESIZE IS BELT AND BRACES, because resizeTo() on a Document Picture-in-Picture
window is not guaranteed and could NOT be tested here — headless has no real
separate window, so the PiP reports the page viewport and resizeTo is a no-op
regardless of what a real browser would do. So the handler resizes, then
MEASURES whether the window actually changed: if it did, the container units
do the rest and no multiplier is applied; if it did not, --pop-k scales the
contents so picking a size still visibly does something.

Files: seo/tools/lib.mjs, build-timers.mjs, stopwatch/index.html.

## 2026-08-01 — indexing audit: three real blocks found and removed

Owner asked whether anything stops the site being indexed. Audited meta robots,
robots.txt, _headers X-Robots-Tag, _redirects and canonicals.

THE WORST ONE, and it was not a noindex. /alarm-clock/12-pm/ — a real 127KB
generated page, "Set Alarm for 12:00 PM", listed in sitemap.xml and carrying no
noindex — was 301'd to /alarm-clock/ by two lines in _redirects. The page could
never be indexed, and the sitemap and the server were telling Google opposite
things about it. "Set alarm for 12 PM"/"for noon" is a real query and 12-pm is
in alarmTimes() like the other 47. Redirect removed. Added a check to this
audit: no sitemapped URL is 301'd (now zero).

/sun/anywhere/ and /moon/anywhere/ carried noindex,follow and were deliberately
kept out of the sitemap. Both are substantial real pages (225KB and 195KB, own
title and H1, search box + geocoder + full times for any coordinates). They
answer the "sunrise anywhere"/small-town queries the 1,074 city pages cannot.
noindex removed in build-sun.mjs and build-moon.mjs, both now in the sitemap.

DELIBERATELY LEFT noindex, because indexing them would hurt: 404.html and
not-found.html (indexed soft-404s are a quality problem), /admin/reports/ and
/admin/stats/ (owner dashboards, also robots.txt-disallowed and X-Robots-Tag
noindex,nofollow), /c.html and /widget/ (tombstones for the retired custom
countdown and embed builder — they say "no longer available", so ranking for
them wastes crawl budget and dead-ends a visitor), /report/ and /wrong-date/
(thin submission forms).

Clean: robots.txt disallows only /not-found, /admin and /api; no cross-domain
or cross-page canonicals anywhere; timers.json "noindex" list is empty.

Files: _redirects, seo/tools/build-sun.mjs, build-moon.mjs, build-sitemap.mjs.

## 2026-08-01 — entity SEO: the place stated as facts and marked up

Owner's point: the sun/moon/tide pages published a time for a place and said
almost nothing about the place. Confirmed — /sun/boston/ named "Massachusetts"
once in passing, no country, no coordinates, no time zone as a fact, and the
1,074 sun and 1,074 moon pages had NO Place or GeoCoordinates markup at all.
(Tide pages were the exception; they already had one.)

New seo/tools/place.mjs — one answer to "which place is this page about", so
the three families cannot drift, the same reason crosslinks.mjs exists. Emits
a visible "About <city>" block (city, state, country, IANA zone + readable
name, UTC offset, latitude, longitude, nearest major city) and Place JSON-LD
with GeoCoordinates, PostalAddress and containedInPlace — both from the SAME
object, so the visible facts and the markup cannot disagree.

Now on 1,104 sun pages, 1,104 moon pages, 143 tide pages. Zero pages carry two
Place entities: the tide markup was ENRICHED in place (county, state, country,
url) rather than duplicated.

NEAREST MAJOR CITY is computed, and the rule took three passes. It must be a
city the reader has heard of, so a candidate has to be big outright (200k) or
several times this city's size, within 180 miles, and the candidate list is
passed IN (always SUN_ALL) so the anchor is guaranteed to have a page. Two bugs
found by inspecting output: "pop >= selfPop * 4" reads as "pop >= 0" when the
city has no population field, so Boston anchored to Chelsea and New York to
Hoboken; and a curated world city must anchor only to another curated city, or
New York anchors to Jersey City. Now Cambridge->Boston 3mi, Abilene->Fort Worth
141mi, Amsterdam->Brussels 108mi, Boston->none, Nome->none.

TWO MISTAKES OF MINE WORTH RECORDING. build-tides already declared a local
`const placeLd`, which shadowed the imported helper and turned a function call
into a string call; I diagnosed that as a circular import and wrote that false
explanation into the source before checking. Both the shadowing and the wrong
comment are fixed; the local is now `placeJsonLd`.

ELEVATION IS NOT INCLUDED, and this is the one thing the owner asked for that
is missing. There is no elevation data in the repo — the census file is City,
State, Population, lat, lon and the curated list is city, area, tz, lat, lon —
and this environment has no outbound network to fetch any. Publishing a height
for a thousand cities without a source, inside structured markup where it is
asserted to a machine as fact, is worse than omitting it. The path is one
build-time pass over USGS EPQS or an SRTM tile set into a committed JSON, the
same shape as make-coastal-map.mjs.

Files: seo/tools/place.mjs (new), build-sun.mjs, build-moon.mjs,
build-tides.mjs, assets/css/parts/20-content.css.

## 2026-08-01 — city labels qualified; elevation pipeline ready

QUALIFIED CITY NAMES. US cities were already "Portland, OR" / "Portland, ME",
but the 98 non-US curated cities were published bare — "Amsterdam", "Tokyo",
"London" — in titles, H1s, descriptions and every chip. cityLabel now lives in
place.mjs (build-sun and build-moon each kept an identical private copy, so any
change had to be made twice) and appends a short country qualifier: the ISO
code, except where the data already carries something shorter and more
recognisable (UK beats GB, UAE beats ARE). Amsterdam, NL / Tokyo, JP /
London, UK / Dubai, UAE / Sydney, AU. Unqualified cities: 0.

ELEVATION. seo/tools/make-elevations.mjs added — USGS EPQS for US points,
Open-Elevation (SRTM) elsewhere, both free and keyless. Resumable (writes every
25 places, skips what it already has), rate-limited at 150ms with a 3-try
backoff, --dry-run/--limit/--force. Dry run enumerates 1,205 places.

place.mjs reads seo/_data/elevations.json IF IT EXISTS and renders both the
visible row and GeoCoordinates.elevation; a missing file or a missing place
omits both. Verified both directions with a temporary file: Amsterdam rendered
"-2 m (-7 ft)" and "elevation":"-2 m" in the JSON-LD while Boston, absent from
the file, showed nothing; deleting the file removed the row again. So the build
stays network-free and nothing is ever invented.

## 2026-08-02 — Rooster alarm tone rebuilt

The rooster was reported as sounding terrible, and it did: two detuned
sawtooths through a single Q 1.1 bandpass with a 33 Hz square-wave tremolo on
the gain. That is a buzzing synth. A saw pair has no noise content, one gentle
bandpass makes no vowel, and audio-rate square modulation of amplitude reads as
a failing speaker.

Rebuilt around how a crow is produced: three detuned saws through a tanh
waveshaper (harsh harmonics), pitch stepped every 12 ms with ~5% jitter (smooth
glides are the tell of a synthetic animal), three parallel bandpass formants
sweeping "uh - ah - oo", a bandpassed white-noise breath layer loudest at the
end, and four syllables at cock / a / dooo / doo timing.

The one non-obvious finding: narrow formant bands ALONE leave a near-sine at
the fundamental — measured, 96% of the energy sat in two bins — and a near-sine
is a whistle, not a bird. A high-passed slice of the raw clipped source is
mixed in alongside the formants to keep the rasp. Do not remove it.

Verified by rendering through an OfflineAudioContext in headless Chromium and
measuring: four envelope peaks at the intended times, 1.42 s audible, energy
spread 550 Hz - 3 kHz, sample peak 0.56 (in line with the other tones, clear of
clipping), and no errors from the emitted /timer/egg-timer/ page. Measured the
structure — nobody here can confirm by ear how it sounds.

Files: seo/tools/alarm-tones.mjs (+ the rebuilt pages).

## 2026-08-02 — Timer: 24-hour ceiling, and full screen on a phone

**The ceiling is 24 hours.** Not a storage limit — the point past which the tool
stops being able to do its job. It only runs while the page is open: a tab left
for a day gets discarded, the laptop sleeps, the phone locks. Past a day a
countdown is a countdown to a DATE, and /countdown/ already does that statically
with no open tab. So the refusal names the tool that does work:

  "Sorry — the timer is meant for stretches of up to 24 hours. For anything
   longer, make a countdown to a date instead."

REFUSED, not clamped. Silently turning a 100-hour request into 24 would leave
somebody watching a countdown that ends 76 hours early with no idea why. Wired
into all four ways a duration can be set — Start, Add, the Set dialog and the
?h= query override — and the hour inputs are max=24. Over the limit the dialog
STAYS OPEN with the reason under the inputs; closing it would look like the Set
had worked. Verified: 24:00:00 accepted, 24:00:01 refused.

**Full screen on the preset pages.** The card itself goes full screen, so the
display and the whole control row travel with it and every listener still
applies — no second copy of the timer to keep in sync. Two layers, because they
fail in different places: the .t-fs class gives a full-viewport view that works
everywhere, and the real Fullscreen API is attempted on top to hide browser
chrome where allowed (iPhone Safari refuses it outright, which is why the class
is not conditional on it).

Two things this got wrong first, both caught by measuring:

  - Hiding the button above 560px as the exact mirror of "Pop out" seemed neat.
    A phone turned landscape is 780px WIDE, so the one control it needed
    vanished at the moment the screen got worth filling. The two are now
    mutually exclusive on CAPABILITY (the `pip-ok` class, set only where
    Document Picture-in-Picture exists), not on width — always exactly five
    controls, and desktop Firefox/Safari get full screen instead of neither.
  - Sized on width alone, the read-out was taller than a 360px-tall landscape
    viewport, and since the layer centres its content the control row ended up
    underneath the wake-lock note. Height has to bound it (max-height:560px).

**The screen stays awake** via a new shared `WAKE_JS` / `window.acWakeLock` in
lib.mjs, replacing what would have been a fourth hand-rolled copy. The
re-request on visibilitychange is the whole reason it is shared: the spec has
the browser RELEASE the lock whenever the page stops being visible and it does
not come back on its own, so without it one glance at a notification silently
ends the lock. Verified in Chromium with a faked lock: dropped on hide,
re-acquired on show.

The bottom line of the full-screen view reads from `held()` — the ACTUAL grant —
never from the intent. Firefox has no Screen Wake Lock, iOS Safari only got it
in 16.4, and even where it exists the request can be refused for low battery.
Telling somebody their screen will stay on and then letting it lock mid-bake is
worse than saying nothing. All four states verified (granted / refused /
absent API / after exit).

NOT DONE: /stopwatch/ already requests a wake lock in its full screen, but only
once, on entry — it has the same silent-loss-on-tab-switch bug and should be
moved onto acWakeLock.

Files: seo/tools/build-timers.mjs, seo/tools/lib.mjs,
assets/css/parts/04-tool-msg.css, assets/css/parts/05-tools-b.css.

## 2026-08-02 — Full-site code review implemented (46 items)

Worked through the 2026-08-02 review end to end, in the order it suggested,
one commit per group. Everything went into the source files; `npm run build`
and `npm run check` after each.

**Wrong information that was live.** US Independence Day had read "that's
today" since July 5 (country pages rendered the static curated date, not the
resolved one). 18 country links pointed at the retired share-link product and
404'd — the dynamic fallback is gone, and check-pages now gates every internal
href against what is published. `nextOccurrence` had no `once` branch, so every
one-off event — including the Aug 12 total solar eclipse, 10 days out — was
missing from /calendar/ and the "Happening soon" rails; it also measured from
`new Date()` with a strict `>`, so a build on an event's own day baked next
year's date into the answer card, FAQ and JSON-LD. /sun/melbourne-fl/ linked to
Melbourne, Australia. Non-US labels used ISO codes that read as US states
("Toronto, CA"). Two supermoon thresholds disagreed. The tide methodology page
described an architecture that no longer existed.

**Alarm.** Firing compared minute STRINGS, so a tick delayed past the minute
skipped the alarm silently and cleanupExpired then deleted it. Now measured on
scheduled instants with catch-up, a visible missed-alarm state, and a banner
when the browser is blocking sound (which the ring shade never revealed).

**Speed.** Split the unconditional CSS core (countdown, /work/, /sun/, event,
pop-out sections), hoisted the byte-identical /sun/, /moon/ and tide
controllers into cached content-hashed files, externalized the 58.8KB moon
sprite, added long cache headers for assets, re-encoded the seven LCP heroes,
cut the homepage dials and the moon search seed. Gzipped: sun city 55.5 → 22.4
KB, moon city 51.9 → 19.0, tide station 71.7 → 31.5, home 47.6 → 35.4,
world-clock 21.8 → 16.5, timer preset 33.1 → 27.2.

**New gate.** The CSS section probes are hand-written regexes and a wrong one
ships an unstyled page that still renders. `sectionsNeeded()` derives the answer
from the CSS's own selectors and build-inline fails if a page gets less than
that. It found two live bugs on its first run: the 48 alarm-time pages had a
`.tool-uses` line with no rule, and /moon/calendar/ rendered its month grid with
no `.mn-cal` CSS at all.

**Build safety.** isMain guards on build-world-clock and build-eclipses (they
wrote pages as an import side effect, and running build-sitemap alone rewrote
them without the inline pass). DST now sampled across twelve months, because
Casablanca is +1 in both January and July and drops to UTC around Ramadan.
Dead near-me dedup, unknown tide-destination sources, Feb-29 roll, missing
curated cities in /sun/cities.json, antimeridian neighbour sort, and the /sun/
hub's "My Cities" panel (all controller, no markup).

**Edge.** The whole admin traffic dashboard, purpose tally, email log and
deployment flags answered anybody; all gated on MOD_LOG_KEY via an
`X-Admin-Key` header. `_middleware.js` ran on every request site-wide — gone,
replaced by `_routes.json` scoped to `/api/*` with the moderation check moved
into og.js, where it now also covers `label` (the field og.js actually renders,
and the one it never checked). Blocklist matching gained word boundaries for
short roots — "Grape Festival" and "Killarney" were blocked — without giving
back separator evasion.

**SEO + P3 batch.** Duplicate Anchorage pages retired with 301s, /popular/ and
/trending/ given real dates per row, /countries/ un-orphaned, titles and
separators tidied, plus the whole small-fixes list (esc() quote escaping, .ics
DTEND, event timezones, southern-hemisphere moon strip, live difference column,
stopwatch precision persistence, and the stale comments the review flagged as
inviting a bad "fix").

NOT DONE / open: Daytona 500 2027 is set to Feb 21 with `estimated: true` — the
old Feb 14 collided with Super Bowl LXI, and Feb 21 is inferred from NASCAR's
usual week-after-the-Super-Bowl slot, not from an official announcement.
`assertLossless()` in css-parts.mjs still has no caller in the build; the new
sectionsNeeded gate is the live one.

Files: most of `seo/tools/`, `assets/css/parts/*`, `assets/js/{tides,effects}.js`,
`functions/`, `_headers`, `_redirects`, `_routes.json`, `stopwatch/index.html`,
`admin/*`, `seo/_data/{events,us-cities,coastal-links}.json`, CLAUDE.md.

## 2026-08-02 — Content review implemented (Part 2, C1–C33)

Second review pass, editorial rather than structural. Same method: source
files only, build + check after each group.

**Wrong statements.** /how-it-works/ said the timer keeps running if you "lock
the screen" — the opposite of what every other page honestly warns, on the page
named "How it works", and the single sentence most likely to make somebody miss
an alarm. Super Bowl XLVIII was 43-8, not 48-3. Phelps's 23 golds are his
total, not his individual count. The alarm page's wake-lock rule left out "and
plugged in", which its own about page states correctly.

**Sports carry-forward.** CLAUDE.md's rule was followed on 3 of 13 pages
because nothing checked it. Eleven pages now carry the last edition forward in
prose, every sentence written from the page's own champions table. check-volatile
flags any page whose table ends in a year its prose never mentions, so the rule
is now self-enforcing.

**Tour de France is the one open item.** The 2026 race finished July 26; the
champions table still ends at 2025, so the page cannot answer "who won?". No
source was available in this session and inventing a winner would be exactly
the failure this site's brand is built against. The gate reports it on every
`npm run check` until a real result is added — that is the whole task: add the
2026 winner to `champions` in events.json, then a "How the 2026 Tour finished"
section.

**Dead links.** 21 `href="undefined"` links across six birthday pages (a work
with an Amazon search but no page of its own). The internal-link gate added in
Part 1 only saw absolute hrefs, so it would have missed these; it now also
catches a template that interpolated a missing value, and that was verified by
planting one.

**Cross-linking.** /calendar/ went from 2 inbound body links to 195; event
pages now link their country pages (derived from countries.json, so it cannot
drift); /moon/supermoons/ and /moon/blue-moons/ from 4-5 inbound to 1,108; sun,
moon and tide STATE hubs now cross-link with data-xlink so the reciprocity gate
sees them; alarm↔timer; popular→trending; 404 is no longer a dead end.

**Templates and tone.** flipH1 mangled memorial and anniversary H1s; "Father's
Day's story so far"; confetti and "🎂 Happy Birthday, Martin Luther King Jr.!"
on memorial pages; the same daylight-saving sentence five times on one
world-clock screen; the preset FAQ repeating the intro verbatim on ~62 pages;
one feature under three names; the long sentences on alarm/timer/tides; "145
days away" beside a clock reading 144; "How many days until The Kentucky
Derby?"; the same 13 breakfast chips on all 48 alarm-time pages; four templates
emitting an empty footer that rendered a dangling "· Suggest an event".

NOT DONE, judged not worth it: the optional FAQPage-LD de-duplication (near-zero
rich-result upside, per the review's own note) and emitting og:url site-wide.

## 2026-08-06 — /solar-system-simulator/ becomes a teaching tool

Seven asks in one session, all on the solar-system simulator: zoom into each
planet's moons; facts about the system and its bodies; a much wider layout; the
open questions science has not answered; recent findings; the asteroid belt and
comets; a playback speed control; a square frame; and launch windows with the
flight path drawn.

**No external data was available.** Every host with orbital data (JPL SSD, the
SBDB API, Wikipedia) is blocked by this environment's egress policy — 403 at the
proxy. So nothing here was fetched: every table is written into the repo and
then PROVED against something it did not supply, which is the check-planets.mjs
pattern extended to four new bodies of data. `npm run check:solar` runs it.

**New modules.** `satellites.mjs` (moon systems + each planet's GM/radius/
rotation), `smallbodies.mjs` (comets + the belt), `transfer.mjs` (launch windows
and flight paths), `check-solar-data.mjs` (the gate), `seo/_data/solar-facts.json`
(the prose that cannot be derived).

**What makes the new numbers trustworthy.** Kepler's third law ties each moon's
axis to its period, so all nine of Saturn's moons must agree on one GM and it
must be Saturn's — that catches a transposed digit in either column, which
proof-reading cannot. The Laplace resonance comes out at n₁−3n₂+2n₃ = −0.00003
°/day. Comet rows carry period and perihelion distance redundantly, so a^1.5 and
a(1−e) check them, and tp plus whole periods has to land on the apparition each
comet is known for. The belt is DERIVED from Jupiter's axis — every Kirkwood gap
is a p:q resonance — and lands within 0.25% of the published gaps. Mass and
surface gravity are computed from GM, matching the textbook values to under 1%
for all eight planets. The transfer solver reproduces the textbook Hohmann
figures and the 25.6-month Mars cadence, and its 2027-02-20 Mars closest
approach at 0.6778 AU matches the published 0.6779.

**What is deliberately NOT claimed.** Where a moon sits on its orbit is not
solved for — sizes, radii, periods and direction are real and the picture, the
read-out and the page all say the position is not. Belt asteroids are drawn as
orbit rings, never dots, because an orbit needs no epoch and a position does.
3I/ATLAS is described but not drawn (hyperbolic, not coming back). The comet page
states the size of its own error: this two-body model puts Halley's return in
June 2061 against the published 28 July 2061, and says so.

**Layout.** The frame is now square — the drawing is concentric circles, so 16:9
was radius-limited by its short side and the corners held nothing; squaring buys
~40% more radius. Pages opt into `.wrap-wide` (1500px), with prose cards capped
at 78ch so the wide shell does not produce 1500px-long lines. Two-column stage
above 1080px. The `orrery` CSS probe now matches `sol` too.

Files: `seo/tools/{satellites,smallbodies,transfer,check-solar-data,build-solar,
planets,build-inline,build-sitemap,build-home}.mjs`, `seo/_data/solar-facts.json`,
`assets/css/parts/{02-base,20d2-orrery}.css`, `package.json`, CLAUDE.md.

Open question for a later session: the recent-findings entries are dated but
nothing gates them going stale, unlike `check-volatile` for sports pages. A
`reviewed` field is in the JSON and printed on the page; a gate that warns when
it is more than a year old would be the natural follow-up.

## 2026-08-06 — Full-site review pass, and the orrery becomes a real viewer

**The review.** Worked an attached full-site review end to end. The safe half
went in as written; several items did not survive checking and are recorded here
because "the review said so" is not a reason. B1's premise was wrong (measured,
not argued). C1's numbers were stale — the bug was real but the frame had been
squared in a later commit, so the figures it quoted had already moved. The
lesson is the one this file keeps re-learning: a claim about a number is only as
current as the commit it was written against.

**Shipped from it.** `og:site_name` + an `Organization` with a real raster logo
(`make-logo-raster.mjs`, 512px and a 180px apple-touch-icon, both rendered from
the same favicon.svg so they cannot drift from the mark); AVIF + a 384w variant
for the event heroes (`make-event-images.mjs` — re-encoding smaller does not
work on dense line art, AVIF does, ~70% of the WebP bytes); `llms.txt`; an iCal
feed at `/calendar/events.ics`; one breadcrumb root; `Place` on world-clock
pages; popular and trending falling back to different lists instead of the same
one. `check-pages.mjs` gained a **double-escape gate** — `&amp;amp;` had shipped
on 1,103 simulator pages and nothing could see it, because the page is otherwise
perfectly well-formed.

**The orrery, rebuilt as a viewer.** The frame's HEIGHT is now fixed and its
WIDTH is not: `orrSvg(...,fw)` takes a width, the client measures its own box and
passes the ratio, so full screen FILLS the screen at any shape instead of
letterboxing — and the sun's distance (the most wrong figure in the picture) gets
less wrong the wider the screen. The sun beam and rays are gone, the bodies are
deliberately smaller than the frame allows (what is most wrong here is DISTANCE,
measured in Earth-diameters, so shrinking the discs while keeping the orbit wide
improves every scale figure at once), and the floor is legibility: the moon must
still show a lit half and a dark half at 390px.

**The simulator's controls.** Play / Now / Settings / Full screen in one row at
every width (below 560px the icon buttons drop their labels and keep their
aria-labels), over a full-width slider; centred normally, right-justified in full
screen. Where/when/how-long live in a `<dialog>` that applies on SAVE, so a
half-typed date never repaints. Spans are day/week/month/**year**, and a replay
setting decides whether Play loops (default) or rests on the last frame. The
date+phase read-out is drawn OVER the picture's bottom edge on a gradient scrim
— a row the picture gets back (fullscreen on an iPhone SE landscape went from
635x215 to 635x262). Full screen asks for a landscape orientation lock; where
that is refused (iOS) an inner wrapper turns a quarter turn instead, because the
UA stylesheet forces `transform:none !important` on the fullscreen element.

**Home page.** Every desktop card is now 50% or 100% wide, so rows fill. The two
simulator cards carry the simulator's own picture at its own proportions and two
links each (Educational info, Full screen). The teachers' card moved up under the
sun card and was rebuilt around one sentence and five chips. Purple is the alarm
clock's default colour. A second tagline paragraph names both simulators and the
classroom guide — nothing above the fold had mentioned them.

**Not done, needs the account owner.** `cloudflare/cache-setup.md` records the
three dashboard pieces — Smart Tiered Cache, a 1-hour Edge TTL cache rule, and a
deploy-aware purge+warm Worker — none of which can be applied from a build
environment. Part 2 is unsafe without Part 3; the file says so.

Files: `seo/tools/{orrery,build-simulator,build-home,build-solar,build-inline,
check-pages,icons,alarm-widget,lib,make-event-images,make-logo-raster}.mjs`,
`assets/css/parts/{11-home,20d2-orrery}.css`, `cloudflare/cache-setup.md`,
`llms.txt`, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `assets/img/logo-512.png`, CLAUDE.md.

## 2026-08-06 — Place cross-links audited, Play capped, the settings dialog fixed

**The audit.** Built the full place matrix — for all 1,103 places, does each of
its family pages link to each other one — rather than answering "does it link to
the simulator" from one screenshot. Result: the triangle is complete. What the
matrix flagged as gaps was mostly DESIGN, and proving that was the useful part:
the 282 tide→sun/moon/sim "gaps" are the station-chose-a-different-city mapping
(/tides/santa-monica-ca/ links to /sun/santa-monica-ca/, while Los Angeles's
registry entry points AT that station), and the 8 tide→clock ones are the
zone-representative rule in localtime.mjs (/tides/boston-ma/ links to
/world-clock/new-york/, the Eastern Time page, because that is the page that
represents the zone). Neither is a bug and neither should be "fixed".

**Two real findings.** The registry — this file's "one answer to what pages
exist for this place" — knew four families and not the fifth, even though
build-simulator makes a page for every registry city, so `sim` is the one family
that is never null. It knows five now, and the world-clock hub's chip row went
from three of the four that existed to all four. And /world-clock/los-angeles/
had no tide link at all: on a sun or moon page tideNote() covers that case from
the registry, but a clock page has no orrery and so no tideNote. It gets a tile
now, without data-xlink, because the pair map cannot express that direction and
the gate would rightly reject it.

**Play is capped at one simulated day per real second.** A fixed 25-second sweep
is right up to a month and falls apart over a year — 15 days a second spins the
Earth 15 times a second and swings the moon through half an orbit in the same
time, which is past the point an eye can follow either, and following them is
the whole purpose. A year now takes about six minutes to play through, which is
the honest cost of it being watchable; the slider is still there to jump with.

**The settings dialog.** The checkbox was rendering 346px wide — the base
`input{width:100%}` with `flex:0 0 auto`, which does not save it because an auto
basis resolves to that same 100%. It pushed its own label past the dialog edge
and gave the dialog a horizontal scrollbar. Never full-screen specific; it was
equally broken on the desktop card and had simply never been in frame. Audited
every other checkbox on the site — all already width:auto. The dialog also faced
the wrong way in full screen on a phone that refuses the orientation lock (it is
a modal in the top layer, so the rule that turns the stage never reached it),
and did not fit once turned, so full screen now pairs the date and span fields
side by side: 403px tall to 314.

Files: `seo/tools/{city-registry,crosslinks,build-world-clock,build-simulator}.mjs`,
`assets/css/parts/20d2-orrery.css`.

## 2026-08-08 — classroom, folder tabs, the lit world, projector mode, legals

- **Planets redrawn on the moon sprite's method** (`globe.mjs`): real
  planetographic coordinates, real km sizes over the real radius, radial
  foreshortening, limb darkening, real oblateness, and Saturn's actual ring
  system with the Cassini Division as a gap. Three bugs fell out: the ring
  plane was drawn horizontally instead of in the equatorial plane (and its
  near/far halves swapped), the axis indicator leaned opposite to where the
  projection puts the pole, and polar caps as blobs at lat 90 have depth
  exactly zero here so Mars had never once been drawn with a cap — caps are
  latitude bands now. Rotation phase now comes from the IAU prime-meridian
  elements; `check-planets.mjs` proves all nine against their sidereal periods.

- **Folder tabs**: curved shoulders, overlapping, one constant outline. Note for
  next time — `clip-path` is applied AFTER `filter`, so a drop-shadow outline on
  a clipped shape is removed by the clip; the working answer is an inline `<svg>`
  with `vector-effect: non-scaling-stroke`.

## 2026-08-08 — three stopwatches, each with a name and a colour

- **`/stopwatch/multiple/` capped at three** (was four). Three side by side still
  leave a read-out readable across a room; the fourth narrowed every card for
  the person who only wanted two. Copy that names the count updated everywhere
  it appears: the page itself (title, description, FAQ, prose), `/stopwatch/`,
  `/timer/` use-case pages, `/classroom/`, the home lede + classroom card.
- **Per-card name and colour**, opened by a palette button sitting to the LEFT
  of the name — one control, because "which card is lane 3?" has exactly two
  answers and they belong together. Eight presets, a native custom picker (so a
  card can match a kit or a paint tin), and No colour. New `palette` glyph in
  `icons.mjs`; panel/tint CSS at the end of `10-multitimer.css`.
- The colour is a TINT (`color-mix`, 26%) against `--bg-solid`, not `--card`:
  `--card` is translucent, and mixing into it gives a colour that shifts with
  whatever is behind the card. Tinted border applies only at `data-state="idle"`
  so a running card keeps its accent border — state outranks label.
- Names and colours are saved with the session and survive **Reset all** (which
  clears times only). A pre-existing four-watch session restores its first three
  on load; the fourth is dropped, which is the cap doing its job.
- **`/stopwatch/` gained a one-line pointer** immediately under the tool card:
  "Run multiple stopwatches →". Someone who needs a second stopwatch needs it
  while looking at the first, not four cards down the page.
- Verified in Chromium (headless, 1100px and 390px): three-card cap and button
  label, panel open/apply/close, tint applied, reload restores name + colour +
  running state, Reset all keeps both.

## 2026-08-08 (2) — one export shape, one button row, both stopwatch pages

- **CSV rewritten on both pages: one row per stopwatch, laps across.**

  old files were one row per lap (single page, with a +/- column) and one column
  PAIR per watch plus a "Final" row (multi) — neither stacked, and the multi one
  put the things being compared at right angles to the axis a spreadsheet
  compares down. The +/- column goes with the rewrite: a row of lap times
  subtracts in one formula, and two numbers per lap need a second row per watch.
  The on-screen +/- column is untouched.

  called "Stopwatch 2" contributes nothing — `stopwatch-1-stopwatch-2` is noise.
  Same base name for the share PNG.
- **Both pages export from the first tick**, not the first lap — the no-laps CSV
  shape made a plain timing a real export. "Running" counts on its own, because
  at the instant Start is pressed the elapsed time is still zero.

## 2026-08-09 — Branch restarted on the right domain; notice + PJ fix re-applied
**The domain is `timeandspace.science`, no hyphens.** The owner said so in the
opening request; this session overrode them on the strength of `site.json`,
which was itself wrong from the import and has since been corrected on main
(e1b7eca6). Everything built here before this entry targeted
`time-and-space.science`, which is not registered — including the Cloudflare
runbook, so the 301 that went live sent every alarm-clock.org visitor to a dead
domain until the owner corrected the rule. The live site was never affected.

The branch was based on the pre-fix main and had the hyphenated name baked into
4,214 files, so it was restarted from current main (`git checkout -B … origin/main`)
and force-pushed rather than rebased — none of it had been merged, and the old
tip was not worth preserving.

Re-applied on the correct base, unchanged in substance:
- **The PJ_SCRIPT duplication fix.** `injectNav()` stripped the old nav with a
  non-greedy match ending at the first `</details>`, but `MENU` appends
  `PJ_SCRIPT` after that tag, so every build orphaned one copy and appended a
  fresh one. main's pages were at **48**; they are back to 1.
- **The domain-change notice**, now reading "alarm-clock.org is now
  timeandspace.science". Self-retiring at `NOTICE_END` (2026-11-09), dismissal
  pre-paint via `html.nb-off` + `localStorage.ac_nb`, second sentence hidden
  under 560px. Strip runs unconditionally so it cannot accumulate or freeze on.
- **`cloudflare/domain-redirect.md`**, retargeted, and two corrections: it no
  longer names the Pages project (the name is an account-level label that has
  already changed once — identify the project by which one lists
  timeandspace.science under Custom domains), and it opens with the hyphen
  warning, pointing at `seo/_data/site.json` as the one place to copy the
  origin from.

Lesson worth keeping: when the owner states a fact about their own property and
a file in the repo disagrees, the file is the thing to check, not the owner.

## 2026-08-09 — fixed garbled LED digits on the home page's Timer/Stopwatch cards
- User-reported screenshot: the home page's Timer mini-card showed
  overlapping/unreadable segments instead of clean digits. Root cause:
  `.tc-big.seg-screen` sizes its font with `min(19cqw,104px)` — a CSS
  container-query unit, valid only where a browser supports
  `container-type:inline-size`. Where it doesn't, `cqw` is an invalid unit,
  the WHOLE declaration is dropped (not replaced with a sane default), and
  the cascade falls through to plain `.seg-screen`'s own
  `clamp(44px,15vw,108px)` (16-seg.css) — sized for a near-full-width tool
  page. On the home board the Timer/Stopwatch cards are half- or
  third-width (`.sp-4`/`.sp-6`), so 15vw of the full viewport badly
  overshoots the actual card and the digits overlap. Reproduced exactly
  (byte-for-byte matching look) by deleting the cqw rule in a headless
  browser at 800px width; confirmed real container widths never drop below
  325px across every breakpoint (700/900/1079/1080/1400/1800px measured).
- Same missing-fallback bug found in `.mt-disp.seg-screen` (10-multitimer.css
  — the /timer/ hub's multi-timer board and /stopwatch/multiple/, up to 3
  columns as narrow as 240px). There `overflow:hidden` stops the *overlap*
  look but still breaks — reproduced with 3 stopwatches side by side:
  severely garbled, same as the home page.
  `.mt-fs`/`.t-fs` fullscreen variants weren't touched: fullscreen
  containers are ~viewport-sized already, so the vw-based fallback isn't
  wrong there.
- Fix: added `@supports not (container-type:inline-size)` fallbacks for
  both, following the pattern `.tc-alarm` already used one rule above (11-
  home.css). `.tc-big.seg-screen` falls back to 44px (matches plain
  `.tc-big`'s own default, comfortably under the ~69px overflow threshold
  at the narrowest measured 325px card). `.mt-disp.seg-screen` falls back
  to 34px (its own clamp's designed minimum). Verified both fixes render
  clean at the narrowest breakpoints and in a 3-column stopwatch board.
- Build green (crosslinks + pages gates). No other `.seg-screen` usage is
  affected — /timer/ and /stopwatch/'s own single displays and the
  fullscreen variants are legitimately viewport-sized already.

## 2026-08-10 — wide-page layout audit: tide chart way oversized, sun's "right now" card underused
- User report: on the new 1400px-wide individual pages, a station's tide
  chart rendered taller than a laptop screen in landscape. Root cause: the
  chart SVG is drawn at a fixed, deliberately-detailed 700×600 viewBox
  (TIDE_H_PAGE, tide-chart.mjs) but its CSS was `width:100%;height:auto` —
  on the wide page that stretched an already-detailed picture to ~1,140px
  tall for no reason. Fixed by capping `.td-chartwrap`/`.td-chart-head`/
  `.td-chartset` at the chart's own native 700px (21-tides.css) — it still
  shrinks below that on a phone, it just stops GROWING past its designed
  size.
- Per the report's own suggestion ("put current info in a column to the
  right if there's room"): wrapped the chart card + safety warning +
  summary card (next high/low, today's other tides) in a new `.td-hero`
  grid (build-tides.mjs: STATION_TOOL and the hub's TOOL_CARDS both), which
  goes two-column above 900px — chart left, warning+summary right — and
  stays the original single stacked column (same DOM order) below it. Grid
  auto-placement (no explicit row on the summary card) means it works
  whether or not the warning card is present, so one rule covers both call
  sites.
- Audited the rest of the site the same way (screenshots at 1366–1920px):
  Sun, Moon, both simulators, Solar System's planet pages, World Clock,
  Alarm/Timer/Stopwatch, Classroom + all 15 lesson pages, Rocket Launches.
  Found one more real instance of the same underlying problem — Sun's
  "Where the sun is right now" card: the orrery picture (capped 440px, by
  design) sat alone in a nearly-1400px-wide row with the four stat pills
  (altitude/direction/azimuth/right now) stacked full-width BELOW it,
  each pill a bordered box with a label at the far left and a short value
  stretched to the far right — the same "big blank gap in a box" shape as
  the tide chart problem, one level down. Fixed with `.sun-nowgrid`
  (20d-sun2.css + build-sun.mjs's sunNowCard): picture and the four stats
  side by side above ~700px (same sibling shape as `.sun-dial-card`/
  `.sun-side` one card above it on the same page), wrapping to stacked
  below that with no extra breakpoint (flex-wrap). Scoped to the sun page
  only — the parallel "Where the moon is right now" card (same shared
  orrery.mjs figure) has no adjacent stat block to pair it with (those
  numbers already live in the hero card higher up), so it was left alone
  rather than manufacturing a second column with nothing to put in it.
- Everything else checked out already well-shaped for the wide page: the
  solar-system and Sun-Earth-Moon simulators, the planet detail pages
  (globe + facts side by side), the 24-hour sun dial, world-clock's facts
  table and the classroom/lesson-plan step tables all already use an
  established "label-value row gets a capped, readable width" or
  "picture-left facts-right" pattern from an earlier pass — nothing there
  needed touching.
- Build green (check-crosslinks: 6,882 reciprocal links across 3,606
  pages; check-pages: all 4,171 pages). Verified visually with Playwright
  screenshots at 500/600/900/1366/1440/1920px for both fixes, stacked and
  side-by-side. Open: didn't touch the tide finder table, king-tide grid,
  or the moon hero's top row (icon … big gap … date/location picker) —
  none of those are "broken", just plainer than they could be; worth a

## 2026-08-10 (evening) — /system/ tilt slider + ladder restoration
- Owner ask: a tilt slider on /sun-moon-earth-movement-simulator/system/.
  Built as an EARTH'S-TILT slider (0–90°, default the real 23.4°), because
  the page's central claim is "the lean is the seasons" and a claim a
  student can break teaches more than one they can only read: at 0° the
  caption says no-seasons, around 23.4° Earth, past 60° Uranus. The drawn
  axis follows live (it is now a vertical line rotated by a transform on
  #sys-axis — baked at 23.4° so no-JS/crawlers see the true axis — instead
  of trig-baked endpoints), and the fixed-in-space counter-rotator doesn't
  care what angle it holds, so the new lean rides the whole orbit exactly
  like the real one. Two caption figures are COMPUTED from the angle, not
  written per band: midnight sun reaches latitude 90−t, overhead sun
  reaches latitude t. "Real: 23.4°" chip appears when the value leaves
  23.4. Ships disabled, inline script enables (site rule). New FAQ entry
  ("What would a different tilt do?") + watch-card item 3 points at the
  slider. CSS: .sys-tiltrow/.sys-tiltnote in 20d2-orrery.css.
- REPAIR: the previous session's merge-conflict batch-resolve (`git
  checkout --theirs` over every still-unmerged path) clobbered the hand-
  resolved build-simulator.mjs, silently reverting the view-ladder edits on
  the sim hub, the 1,103 city pages and /system/ (solar kept it — its
  generator wasn't conflicted). The earlier "sys-vlad present" sanity grep
  was fooled by the INLINED CSS SELECTORS; the real check is the markup
  (`<nav class="sys-vlad"`), which is what this session greps. All four
  edits re-applied; lesson recorded here so the next merge resolves the
  hand-fixed file BEFORE batch-checking-out the generated ones.
- Build green (both gates). Playwright-verified: slider drags 0→80→reset,
  axis transform follows, band captions + computed latitudes correct,

## 2026-08-10 (late) — /system/: the tilt slider becomes a VIEW slider
- Owner clarified the earlier ask: not the Earth's axial lean — tilt the
  VIEWPOINT, so you watch the Earth's orbit from the side (Earth looping in
  front of the sun, then behind) and see the moon's tilted orbit MISS the
  sun–Earth line at most new/full moons — the why-no-monthly-eclipse
  picture. The axial-tilt slider from earlier today is replaced (its FAQ
  entry too); the fixed 23.4° axis stays drawn and is now foreshortened
  honestly by the same projection.
- Implementation: the nested CSS rotations only know the flat plane, so on
  this one page an inline script replaces them — sun at origin, Earth on an
  ecliptic circle, moon on a circle inclined to it with a fixed node
  direction, orthographic projection at the slider's angle, painter-sorted
  depth so the Earth really passes in front of/behind the sun, mild
  size-with-depth cue, screen-space sun-true shading, Earth-facing moon
  patch, and the axis as a projected fixed space vector. The baked SVG
  keeps the old flat CSS-animated scene inside #sys-scene for no-JS and
  crawlers; JS clears and re-owns that group only. prefers-reduced-motion:
  static render, slider still works.
- Honesty ledger: the moon's inclination is DRAWN at 18° (SYS_INC_DRAWN) —
  the real 5.1° is a two-pixel wobble at this orbit's 26px — stated in the
  caption, a new scale-card row (ratio computed, not typed), and the FAQ.
  The trust hint now also names what the side view does NOT say: the node
  direction is a drawing choice, so nothing implies when eclipse seasons
  fall. Watch-card items 3 (seasons: axis parallel to itself round the
  loop) and 4 (rewritten from "cannot show" to "drag the slider") updated.
- Verified with Playwright: slider 0/45/90, captions per band, side chip,
  animation advancing (transform deltas), no page errors; both build gates
  green.

## 2026-08-10 (night 2) — /orbital-velocity-simulator/ + the "why don't they fall in" explainer
- Owner ask: an orbital-velocity simulator like a screenshot they sent, but
  ALSO showing gravity's role; plus pages explaining why the sun's pull per
  kilogram is far greater at Mercury yet Mercury is not dragged in. Their
  own reasoning ("Mercury has to travel faster — correct?") is right, and
  both pages are built around confirming it precisely and correcting the
  one common misconception underneath it (speed does not RESIST gravity;
  the planet is in free fall and keeps missing).
- New generator build-orbital.mjs → two pages:
  /orbital-velocity-simulator/ (sim, gravity card, all-eight table, FAQ)
  and /orbital-velocity-simulator/why-planets-dont-fall-into-the-sun/
  (7-section explainer: pull really is stronger → it IS falling → Newton's
  cannonball → v=√(GM/r) → mass cancels → what would actually make it fall
  → why nothing slows it).
- EVERY figure derived from two inputs only: TR_GM_SUN (transfer.mjs) and
  the semi-major axes (planets.mjs). Verified against published values:
  circular speeds 47.9/29.8/13.1/5.4 km/s, pull ratios Mercury 6.67× Earth
  and 181× Jupiter, and Mercury's real perihelion/aphelion speeds come out
  58.98/38.86 — matching reference books exactly. The table carries BOTH
  circular-speed-at-a and mean-orbital-speed and explains why they differ
  for Mercury (e=0.206), so the page cannot look wrong against a search
  result quoting 47.36.
- The sim solves closed orbits analytically (Kepler, no drift) and
  integrates only the escape case (velocity Verlet, symplectic, so it
  cannot fake an escape). One ES5 source, two runtimes — Node bakes the
  opening frame so crawlers/no-JS get a real orbit.
- Three bugs caught in verification, all fixed: (1) the speed slider's 10%
  floor could not reach the fall-in threshold at Earth (needs <9.65% =
  √(2R☉/r)) while the copy said "try it" — floor is now 5%, and the sim now
  brackets the page's stated 2.87 km/s exactly (falls in at 2.7, survives
  at 3.0); (2) the AU rings used a fixed 1 AU step, so inner-planet views
  had no scale reference at all — step is now adaptive, and my first fix
  inverted the test and drew 0.05 AU rings on a 30 AU frame; (3) the escape
  track used a fixed time step, taking giant strides near the sun.
- check-pages caught the new pages missing from build-inline's page list
  (no nav, un-inlined CSS) — the fail-safe gate doing exactly its job.
  Registered in build-inline PAGES + FAVICON_RULES, the build chain,
  build-sitemap, the nav menu, and linked from the solar simulator.
- View ladder rewritten to the owner's spec: heading "Three views of Earth,
  and how it fits with the rest of the solar system"; tiles now name the
  VANTAGE POINT (from a place on Earth / Earth & the moon's orbit around
  the sun / the full solar system) rather than sloganeering. A numeral
  element carries the sequence on phones where the kicker text is hidden,
  so each tile is one line even with the longer names.

## 2026-08-10 (night 3) — /system/: the slider tilts THE ORBIT, not the camera
- Owner: "change Earth's tilt to tilt Earth's orbit… the Earth will no
  longer tilt differently unless it's relative to the orbit… as the orbit
  tilts, the relative image will get wider and shorter."
- The geometry was already right — the axis sits at the real 23.4° from the
  ORBIT'S normal and is carried by the same projection, so it has always
  tipped rigidly with the orbit and was never independently adjustable
  (the standalone axial-tilt slider was removed earlier today). What was
  wrong was the FRAMING: label "View", copy about swinging your viewpoint.
  Relabelled "Tilt Earth's orbit", read-out "flat on → edge on", and every
  caption, watch-card item, trust note and FAQ entry rewritten to the
  tipping-the-orbit frame, stating explicitly that the axis keeps its
  23.4° lean relative to the orbit and tips along with it.
- Made "wider and shorter" literally true: a new uniform scale SC =
  1 + 0.42·sin(tilt) grows the scene as it flattens, so the orbit goes from
  160×160 px (circle) to 227×0.8 (a line) — wider as it gets shorter,
  instead of shrinking into a thin band across an empty box. UNIFORM
  (positions and body radii together) on purpose: a zoom that moved the
  bodies without resizing them would silently falsify every ratio in the
  scale card below it.
- Verified with Playwright at 0/45/90°: labels, ellipse rx/ry, axis lean
  visible edge on, no page errors. Both gates green.

## 2026-08-10 (night 4) — /system/: the bodies show real PHASES, not a rotating half-disc
- Owner spotted it from a screenshot: tipped edge on, with Earth crossing the
  sun, the shading still showed a half-lit disc "as if the sun was shining
  from the side". Correct catch and a genuine bug. The lit half was drawn as
  a fixed half-disc rotated to face the sun IN SCREEN SPACE — which is only
  right when the viewer stands square to the light. It ignored the phase
  angle entirely.
- Fixed with real phase geometry, the same construction mnGlyph (moon.mjs)
  uses for the actual moon: illuminated fraction f = (1 + cos a)/2 where a is
  the angle at the body between the sun and the VIEWER — and the viewer
  direction is (0,-sv,cv), the very vector the depth sort already uses, so
  the phase can never disagree with the front/behind ordering. The terminator
  is then a semi-ellipse of semi-minor axis r|1-2f|, rotated to point at the
  sun's screen direction. Applies to Earth AND the moon.
- Verified by reading the emitted path rather than by eye (the arc's rx gives
  |1-2f| and its sweep flag gives which side of half): Earth BEHIND the sun →
  fully lit (rx=9.99, sweep 1); Earth IN FRONT of the sun → fully dark
  (rx=9.99, sweep 0); off to the side → half lit, sweeping continuously
  between. Exactly the moon's own monthly cycle, for the same reason, which
  is a teaching bonus the flat view could never show.
- The flat-on view is unchanged by construction: at zero tilt every body sits
  in the plane perpendicular to the view, so f = 0.5 everywhere and the old
  half-disc was already the right answer there. The baked no-JS scene (the
  flat CSS animation) therefore stays correct as-is.

## 2026-08-10 (night 5) — /sun/<city>/ pairs three card rows on desktop
- Owner: on /sun/seattle/ many cards can be 50/50 two-column, some 2/3+1/3.
  Applied the existing `.duo` utility (two columns ≥980px, stacked below) to
  three pairs chosen because each is two halves of ONE thought, per the rule
  written into that CSS:
    · Sunrise & sunset TOMORROW + Twilight/golden/blue hour — the other
      times for this place: next day's headline figures, today's light
      quality. Both were 1400px bands holding three stat tiles.
    · Next 7 DAYS + Sunrise & sunset through the YEAR — the same quantity at
      two time scales, which is the pairing that actually teaches: the table
      shows tomorrow moving a minute, the chart shows where a year of those
      minutes goes. The year chart self-caps at 660px, so half-width costs
      it nothing.
    · Daylight & seasons + the FAQ — the two prose cards.
  Left full width (they use it): the daylight arc, the 24-hour dial, the
  right-now orrery, place facts, the astro strip and the neighbour chips.
- The seasons+FAQ pair is CONDITIONAL: the seasons card only exists when
  there are facts to state, and a .duo holding one child would leave a
  half-width column of blank page. Factored the FAQ into `faqHtml` so the
  two emit branches cannot drift.
- Applied the week+year pair to /sun/anywhere/ and /sun/near-me/ too.
- BUG CAUGHT IN VERIFICATION: the near-me template got the duo opener but
  not its closer (its markup after the year chart differs from the other
  two, so the batch edit's closer pattern missed it) — one unclosed div on
  that page. Found by parsing the emitted HTML for div balance rather than
  by eye; all four sun page types now parse to zero unclosed and zero extra.
  Worth remembering: check-pages does NOT check div nesting, so a structural
  edit like this needs its own balance check.
- Verified at 1440px (674px columns) and 390px (354px, single column).

## 2026-08-10 (night 8) — county cards, a two-zone time calculator, two new space cards

Four owner requests in one pass.

- **`/tides/<state>/` hubs: a card per county, two abreast.** All counties shared
  one full-width card, so a long coast was pages of headings with no edge saying
  where one county ended. Each county now gets its own card in a `.td-counties`
  grid (2 cols ≥980px, the `.duo` threshold). The county `<h2>` stays the
  immediate previous sibling of its `.td-strows` — the jump-to-county control
  and the live NOAA directory fetch both find a list that way. Jump control and
  the legend/links moved to their own full-width cards either side of the grid.
- **Home: "Time Between Two Times" card**, and the calculator behind it gained a
  time zone PER SIDE. New `seo/tools/time-diff.mjs` holds the form and the maths;
  `/time-difference-calculator/` and the home card both use it, so there is one
  calculator in two places. Cross-zone is the point: departure zone + arrival
  zone gives real flight time, and a second line says what the start time reads
  on the other clock. The answer is normalised into [0,24h) by stepping the end
  time's CALENDAR day in its own zone, so a date-line pair isn't a day out.
  Verified in a browser: LAX 10:00→LHR 06:00 = 12h, Tokyo 09:00→LA 09:00 = 16h,
  same-zone 22:00→06:00 = 8h.
- **Home: `/system/` card**, between the Sun-Earth-Moon simulator and the solar
  system — the step between them, and the picture neither neighbour shows. A
  card-sized schematic (same choice `_solarThumb` documents), keeping the one
  ratio the real page keeps: the moon's duration is `SIDEREAL/365.25` of the
  Earth's, so it laps ~13x per orbit.
- **Home: six planet-with-moons cards, Space tab only** (`data-hg-not="all"`).
  Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus, Neptune, Pluto — the set with a moons rung.
  Orbit radii, periods, diameters and direction come from `satellites.mjs`;
  each moon's animation takes its real period ratio, retrograde moons run
  backwards. Copy names only DRAWN moons (Saturn's Phoebe is outside the frame,
  so the card no longer says it goes round backwards over a picture without it).

Two bugs found and fixed on the way:
- `/time-difference-calculator/` printed its breadcrumb JSON-LD as visible text —
  `breadcrumbLD()` returns JSON and this was the one generator that never wrapped
  it in a `<script>`. So the page also had no breadcrumb structured data at all.
- `/sun-moon-earth-movement-simulator/system/` had no `id="learn"`, the anchor
  both sibling simulator pages carry and the new card links to.

`23-timediff.css` → `20h-timediff.css`, moved ahead of tides/moon in the SPEC:
the home page defers those two sections to end-of-body, which is only safe while
they are last in its cascade, and the timediff card sits high on the board so
deferring it would paint unstyled and jump. Safe to move — every selector in the
file is uniquely `.tdiff-`prefixed. `sp()` gained a `not` argument for the tab
filter. Files: build-tides, build-home, build-time-difference, build-simulator,
css-parts, time-diff.mjs (new), 11-home.css, 20h-timediff.css, 21-tides.css.
Full build + both gates + `npm run check` green; no JS errors on any page.


## 2026-08-11 — the logo is now the section menu
- Owner: make the top-left logo a dropdown listing Time / Earth / Space /
  Classroom, each opening the home page on that tab.
- Built in build-inline's injectLogo, so it lands on all 4,178 pages at once
  — which is the point: the home board's four tabs were reachable ONLY by
  landing on the home page and pressing one. From any of the other ~4,000
  pages there was no route to a section at all. The logo is the control a
  reader already expects to mean "take me back", and the sections are what
  back should offer.
- NO NEW URL SCHEME. build-home's tab script already reads `?tab=<id>` on
  load and writes it back via replaceState on every tab press, so these
  links are the same addresses the tabs themselves mint; a shared link opens
  on the right tab. Verified end-to-end over a local HTTP server (file://
  cannot load the hoisted /assets/js, so the tabs never initialise there):
  no param -> all (14 blocks), time -> time (6), earth -> earth (4),
  space -> space (11), class -> class (4).
- "Home — everything" leads the menu: turning the wordmark anchor into a
  <summary> would otherwise drop the site-wide link to `/`, which is worth
  keeping for readers and crawlers. Inside <details> it stays in the DOM.
- Reuses .nav-dd wholesale — same markup, same panel, same
  close-on-outside-click script as the copy and hamburger menus — so the CSS
  is only the three things that differ: open leftward (.nav-dd .menu pins
  right:0, correct for the two menus at the far end of the bar, off-screen
  for this one), restore the logo's 45px height now that it is no longer
  inside .brand-name, and lay the rows out as icon + label.
- MARKERS, NOT A TAG MATCH (`<!--bd-->…<!--/bd-->`, same pattern as
  NOTICE_RE): the hand-maintained pages are rewritten in place, so the
  pattern must find its own previous output as well as the original anchor,
  or the menu would freeze on those pages after the first injection.
  Asserted by running build-inline twice — still exactly one brand-dd.

## 2026-08-11 (later) — the domain as a wordmark, site-wide; breadcrumb out
- Owner: set the name as the brand — TimeAndSpace.Science — with capitals at
  each word start, the concepts white and "And" grey, and gaps about a third
  of a space so three words read out of one string. Site-wide, and take the
  breadcrumb out of the bar for now (navigation to be reworked separately).
- Chose grey for the DOT as well as "And", so the rule is "grey is the join,
  white is the concept" rather than three exceptions.
- THE GAPS ARE MARGINS (0.1em; a word space here is ~0.26em, so this is the
  requested third). Not spaces, and that is the whole point: a real space
  would let the name break across two lines AND would be copied — paste
  "Time And Space . Science" into an address bar and you get a search, not
  this site. The text stays the single string "TimeAndSpace.Science", which
  is what a reader should carry away. The spans are emitted with no
  whitespace between tags for the same reason. `.wm-tld` cancels the margin
  so ".Science" stays welded as one unit.
- ON THE HOME PAGE THE WORDMARK IS THE <h1>. That page's bar heading was
  already the brand ("Time and Space"), not anything descriptive — the
  descriptive text is in <title> and the lede — so nothing readable is lost
  and the page no longer carries its own name twice. Everywhere else it is a
  link to `/`, which also gives back the every-page home link that the
  wordmark anchor provided before the logo became a menu.
- Breadcrumb (.brand-cat) stripped in build-inline rather than in lib.mjs
  brand(): the hand-maintained pages carry that markup literally and never
  pass through a generator, so only the inline pass reaches all 4,178.
  Verified zero pages still emit `<a class="brand-cat"`. The BreadcrumbList
  JSON-LD is emitted separately and is untouched, so search-result
  breadcrumbs still work.
- The crumb-eating group hangs off BOTH branches of BRAND_RE: pages built
  before this change already carry the <!--bd--> markers with the crumbs
  sitting AFTER them, so a tail attached only to the anchor branch would have
  left them behind on every page. Caught by re-running the pass and grepping.
- .brand-cat's CSS rules are deliberately left in place — the owner is
  reworking navigation shortly and will likely want them back; two dead rules
  are cheaper than removing and restoring them.
- Verified: home h1 = TimeAndSpace.Science (25px at 1440, 16px at 390), inner
  pages the same as a link, colours white/muted per span, textContent exactly
  "TimeAndSpace.Science", and build-inline re-run leaves exactly one wordmark.

## 2026-08-15 — /24-hour-clock-converter/: 12-hour ⇄ 24-hour, and 48 time pages
- New tool: `/24-hour-clock-converter/` — set either clock, read the other —
  plus one page per half hour at `/24-hour-clock-converter/<hhmm>/` (48: `1430`,
  `0730`, `0000`). The hub carries the converter, the conversion rules, a
  24-row chart whose rows are all pages, and a 6-question FAQ.
- The per-time pages exist because the query is never "convert time": it is
  "1430 military time" or "what is 18:00 in 12 hour". Each page answers in its
  first line, both directions, and carries the spoken forms ("fourteen thirty",
  "half past two in the afternoon"), a clock face, and the rule for its own
  BAND of the day — midnight, morning (unchanged), noon, afternoon (−12) are
  genuinely different arithmetic, not one sentence with different digits.
- The set is `alarmTimes()` rather than a second list, so every converter page
  has a working "set an alarm for 2:30 PM" page one link away, and the alarm
  pages link back through the one shared `convSlug()`. 48 ↔ 48, permanently.
- Two complete clocks, not one shared minute field: converting changes only the
  hour and the AM/PM label, and half of what the reader is checking is that the
  minutes did not move. No `<input type="time">` on the 24-hour side — a native
  time input renders in the BROWSER's locale and draws an AM/PM segment on a US
  device, which is the one thing this page must not do.
- Everything is baked: selects, both read-outs and the spoken line are correct
  before any script runs. `CONV_JS` is byte-identical across all 49 pages, so
  build-inline hoists it to one cached `/assets/js/clock-convert.<hash>.js`.
- Files: new `seo/tools/clock-convert.mjs` + `build-clock-convert.mjs` +
  `assets/css/parts/20i-clock-convert.css` (section `convert`, probed on `cv-`);
  wired into `package.json`, `build-inline` (page list + probe), `build-sitemap`
  (49 URLs + llms.txt), and linked from `/world-clock/`,
  `/time-difference-calculator/` and all 48 alarm-time pages. `CLAUDE.md`
  updated (pipeline row 13b + shared-module notes).
- Verified: full `npm run build` (4,231 files, check-crosslinks and check-pages
  both pass), `npm run check` clean, and `CONV_JS` driven against a stub DOM for
  both directions including 12 AM/12 PM, 00:00, 23:59 and "Use the time now".
- FOUND, NOT FIXED: `/time-difference-calculator/` sets
  `og:image=/assets/img/og-default.png`, which does not exist (only
  `og-default.svg` does, and SVG is not usable as an og:image). The new pages
  omit og:image rather than copy it.

## 2026-08-15 (2) — the orrery card gets its Play button
- Owner, from /sun/near-me/: there is no play button, and "if it is not going to
  animate, what is the purpose of the slider and the JavaScript needed to make
  this work". Correct on both counts — the card had a slider, a full drawing
  engine and no way to run it.
- It was a HALF-LANDED feature, not a design choice. `orrery.mjs` carried two
  comments about what happens "while Play is running", the SVG already tagged
  the city tick and name with `.orr-citytick`/`.orr-cityname`, and
  `20d2-orrery.css` already held `.orr-spin .orr-cityname{display:none}` — but
  nothing on a /sun/ or /moon/ page ever set that class, because the only Play
  ever built was the simulator's (`sim-play`, its own `sim-fig` container).
  `git log -S"orr-play"` on orrery.mjs: nothing, ever.
- Added Play to the card, so ~2,200 /sun/ + /moon/ place pages and both
  any-location tools now animate. It sits WITH the slider it drives, not up in
  the stamp row with Now: it does not choose an instant, it walks that one
  control through the day. Same shape as the simulator's.
- **It loops the day it was started on and never leaves it**, and that is the
  design rather than a shortcut: moving the DAY makes the host page redo
  sunrise, the dial, the twilight bands and the 7-day table, which as a
  per-frame cost is a page repaint per frame. Staying inside the day costs
  exactly what dragging the slider already costs. So the Earth turns once per
  loop and the moon creeps its real 13°/day; a month at a time stays the
  simulator's job, which has the span control for it.
- A second an hour (`ORR_DAY_MS`), repaints capped at ~20/s rather than per
  frame — this rewrites an SVG string and a caption each time, not a canvas.
  `.orr-spin` goes on while it runs, which finally makes that CSS rule true for
  these pages. The field, the slider and Now all stop it; the loop's own
  `.value` writes fire no `input` event, so it cannot pause itself. Ships
  `hidden disabled` and is revealed by the script, like Now.
- Files: `seo/tools/orrery.mjs`, `assets/css/parts/20d2-orrery.css`, `CLAUDE.md`.
- Verified in Chromium on /sun/portland/: Play→Pause with aria-pressed, ~61 min
  of sky per second, the midnight wrap (1411→99 across the loop point), city
  name gone while spinning and back when stopped, slider frozen when paused, Now
  stopping playback and returning to "Where the sun is right now", no page
  errors. Full build: check-crosslinks and check-pages both pass.
- Checked while there: the card DOES open live — the 08/14 date in the report
  is Portland's own clock, which is a day behind UTC at that hour. No bug.

## 2026-08-24 — The tropics, drawn: the sun and the Earth in one frame

- The map answered "where is it light" and drew the equator and both tropics,
  which raised a question it could not answer: what *are* those two lines.
  Added `dnSide(dec, tilt)` to `daynight.mjs` — the same instant seen from the
  side, in the plane of the orbit: sunlight arriving parallel, half the globe
  lit, and the ONE line from the centre of the sun to the centre of the Earth
  meeting the surface at the point the sun is straight overhead. That is the
  same yellow dot the map marks, from a different place to stand.
- WHY THE TROPICS ARE A LIMIT AND NOT A PLACE. The axis leans by `dec` in this
  view, which is not an approximation of the seasons — the component of the
  tilt facing the sun IS the declination. So the centre-to-centre line can
  never land more than the tilt from the equator, and the two chords drawn at
  +/- r*sin(tilt) are exactly where it lands twice a year. On a solstice the
  dot IS the chord's endpoint, which is the whole point of the picture.
- Same one-source-two-runtimes rule as the rest: `dnSide` lives in DN_CORE, so
  the baked figure and the browser's redraw come from one function. The caption
  under it goes further — `sideCapText()` is called in Node to bake the
  sentence AND stringified into PAGE_JS, so its three branches (equinox,
  solstice, in between) cannot drift apart.
- Also on /day-night-map/: the tilt card now says the thing that is easiest to
  get backwards — the AMOUNT of shadow never changes, half the Earth is lit on
  every date; what the tilt moves is where the line falls and therefore whose
  share is bigger. The four season buttons now appear three times (under the
  map, under the side view, and inline in the fact rows), because the script
  already wired every `[data-dn-jump]` on the page and a button beside the
  sentence explaining it beats one you have to scroll back for.
- Files: `seo/tools/daynight.mjs`, `build-daynight.mjs`, `build-home.mjs`
  (a "Why the tropics are there" link off the hero map),
  `assets/css/parts/20d2-orrery.css`.
- Verified in Chromium at 1280/390px: 22 jump buttons all wired, the figure and
  its caption repaint together on every one, the dot lands on the Cancer chord
  in June and the Capricorn chord in December and on the equator at an equinox,
  no page errors. Below 620px the figure scrolls inside its own box rather than
  shrinking its labels to 6px. Full build passes both gates.

## 2026-08-24 (2) — /classroom/ stops being a timer page

- FOUND A LIVE BUG FIRST, and it may be the whole product problem: `/api/report`
  used `isIdea` in three places and declared it nowhere, so every submission
  that reached the webhook branch threw a ReferenceError and answered 500. The
  sender was told it had failed; the KV backstop and the owner email never ran.
  Every teacher form on the site posts through that endpoint. Fixed, plus the
  `details` cap raised from 1,000 to 20,000 characters for the two long-form
  reasons — a pasted lesson plan was going to be silently beheaded.
- THE HUB IS THREE DOORS NOW. Astronomy and Earth science lead in a `.duo`,
  Time follows, quieter — the owner's weighting, and the right one. Everything
  about timers moved down to `/classroom/time/`, which keeps the search terms
  ("classroom timer") on the site while stopping them being the first thing a
  teacher learns about it. New title, H1, dek and LearningResource JSON-LD.
- `/classroom/astronomy/`, `/classroom/earth/`, `/classroom/time/` — no new
  simulators and no new lessons, just the existing catalog arranged so the
  first click is science. Every lesson row is DERIVED from LESSONS via
  `lessonRef()`, which throws on a missing lesson rather than emitting a dead
  row, so a retitled lesson cannot leave a stale claim on three other pages.
- TWO NEW FORMS in `classroom-forms.mjs`: send the lesson you already run, and
  send the questions your class asked. Both ask for something the teacher
  ALREADY HAS, which the existing "ask us to build a simulator" form does not —
  that one asks them to imagine a tool that does not exist, and in the time it
  has been up it has produced nothing. It is kept, at the bottom.
- The promise is deliberately conditional — "if we can use it" — because a form
  that implies automatic publication will eventually make a liar of the site.
  Same rule the sponsorship pages were rewritten under.
- Child safety is a field list, not a badge: there is no field a student's name
  belongs in, both checkboxes say so, and `SAFETY_NOTE` states what is held
  rather than claiming a compliance status.
- PUSHED BACK on the brief in three places, all noted to the owner: the section
  menu stays four sections (the ask went into the sitewide footer instead), the
  doors are not equal weight, and `/classroom/time/` keeps the timer keywords
  rather than letting them evaporate.
- Files: `functions/api/report.js`, `seo/tools/classroom-forms.mjs` (new),
  `build-classroom.mjs`, `build-inline.mjs` (page list, footer), `build-home.mjs`
  (one line routing to the shorter path), `build-sitemap.mjs`,
  `assets/css/parts/20-content.css`.
- Verified in Chromium: all four pages have H1/title/nav and no dead links, the
  driving-question box follows its radio, all six of Form A's guards fire in
  order with their own message, both forms post the right `reason` and a
  correctly labelled `details` block, no page errors. Full build + `npm run
  check` pass.

## 2026-08-24 (4) — The lesson grid gets filters

- /classroom/lessons/ now opens with a filter card — Grade, Topic, Time — over
  the topic grid. Every filterable fact rides on the markup it filters
  (data-band/data-mins on rows, data-topic on cards), so a lesson added to
  LESSONS is filterable the moment it renders; there is no parallel list.
- FILTERING SHOWS ABSENCE HONESTLY: a band filter leaves each topic's
  "coming — ask for this band" row visible and the count line says "3 lessons
  (3 bands not written yet — ask below)", because a gap a teacher can see is a
  gap a teacher can ask us to fill. A topic the reader filters OUT vanishes
  entirely — they said no, which is different from us not having it.
- Time buckets from the catalog's actual spread (20 / 35–40 / 45–50) so no
  bucket is ever empty. Re-pressing a chip clears it. ?grade=&topic=&time= are
  written with replaceState and read on load, so a filtered view is a
  shareable link. Ships disabled; without JS everything is visible anyway.
- THE FILTERS CAUGHT A LIE ON DAY ONE: the hub said "Seventeen timed plans"
  and the DOM count is eighteen. The number is now LESSON_PAGES.length,
  spelled out, never typed.
- Container rolled back mid-task again (to efa7b5aaf3); recovered with
  fetch + reset to origin/main and re-applied the uncommitted filter work.
- Files: build-classroom.mjs (topicCard data attrs, filterBar, FILTER_JS,
  derived count), assets/css/parts/20-content.css (.lg-*).
- Verified in Chromium: 6 cards / 18 lessons / 12 coming rows unfiltered; band,
  topic and time filters each correct alone and combined; re-press clears;
  deep link ?grade=grades-k-2 opens filtered with the chip pressed; no page
  errors. Full build passes both gates.

## 2026-08-28 — Day/night map slider covers one 27.3-day lunar orbit

- /day-night-map/ slider was seven days. The moon marker on that map is the
  overhead point, and that point takes one SIDEREAL month to go once around
  Earth, so the bar is now that length: 27.3 days, imported from system-orbit
  rather than typed, same number as the rest of the site.
- Play still runs at six hours a second (one Earth turn in four seconds); the
  full orbit takes about two minutes and then wraps. Copy, FAQ, meta and the
  date span all follow the constant. Home card stays one day — that card is
  "right now".
- Files: seo/tools/build-daynight.mjs, day-night-map/index.html.
- Verified in Chromium: max=39312 minutes, label "27.3 days, one orbit of the
  Moon", span Aug 28 – Sep 24, thumb at the far end lands 27.3 days later.
